The No. 1 Horror Movie of 2026 Is Officially Leaving Peacock

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Unless you have a One Wish Willow, you'll want to get your final watch in

Not many directors can say their debut film was a blockbuster hit, let alone a cultural phenomenon. But newcomer Curry Barker, who wrote and directed Obsession, the No. 1 horror movie of 2026, can say he's accomplished both. The film takes a look at the dangers of getting exactly what you wish for when Bear (Michael Johnston) makes a wish that his friend Nikki Freeman (Inde Navarrette) would love him more than anyone else in the world. Despite being made on only a $750,000 budget, the film dominated the box office when it hit theaters, earning $17.2 million in its first week and a staggering $23.9 million in its second week.

To date, Obsession remains the tenth most profitable film of 2026 at the worldwide box office according to data from Box Office Mojo, earning a gross total of $523 million during its theatrical run. It's no surprise that when Obsession moved to streaming, it continued finding success on Peacock, but going into November, the end of Obsession's victory lap is officially drawing near.

Obsession is leaving Peacock on Nov. 17, 2026, the same date that the film is expected to launch on Netflix in the United States. The film first hit Peacock on July 17, 2026, and drew in 456 million minutes streamed in just its first two days on the service, according to data from Nielsen. It went on to spend more than 150 days on JustWatch’s U.S. streaming Top 10 list, further cementing its domination in the industry.

Obsession was more than just a blockbuster and streaming hit. It became a cultural phenomenon in the months that followed its release, with fans drawn into conversations online about the film's themes surrounding bodily autonomy and consent. A Nikki Freeman actor drew thousands of visitors to California's Halloween Horror Nights this fall, including Inde Navarette herself, and Obsession reached as far as Fortnite, where a Nikki Freeman skin was released for the game in November 2026 (complete with a "No, No, No..." emote).

Even Barker, who first built a following for himself making sketches and short films on YouTube, didn't expect the success that came with Obsession. In a June 2026 interview with the Associated Press, he said, "“That’s when you start to feel this impostor syndrome of like: What? It’s not that good. All I see when I watch ‘Obsession’ is the problems.”

The film follows Bear (Michael Johnston), a lonely, awkward man with strong, unrequited feelings for his friend Nikki Freeman (Inde Navarrette). After failing to confess his feelings, Bear stumbles upon a One Wish Willow in a novelty shop. The instructions seem simple enough: make a wish, break the willow stick, and watch your deepest desires come true, but when Bear wishes for Nikki to love him more than anyone else in the entire world, his dream quickly turns into a nightmare. The film has received plenty of praise from critics and audience members, including Polygon writer Jesse Hassenger, who wrote, "Navarrette gets plenty of time to believably and charmingly establish who Nikki is before the wish takes her true self away. Once that happens, she does bravura work as this imitation version, regularly flying off the handle with panicked eagerness or cackling mania, creating an unusual combination of fear and pity — a true movie monster crossing possession with a Bride of Frankenstein figure."

It's worth noting that Obsession's move to Netflix this month comes as awards season nears, and it could very well be a strategic decision to keep the film in the spotlight. Focus Features, the production company behind Obsession, is definitely pushing for consideration in the forthcoming Academy Awards. The company's website suggests Obsession for Best Picture, Inde Navarrette for Best Actress, and Curry Barker for Best Original Screenplay, among other categories.

Obsession is about to get another boom in viewership

Beauty Marks: The Best Beauty Looks of the Week

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Welcome back to Beauty Marks: Vogue’s weekly edition of the best moments in celebrity beauty, from Vogue editors’ IG feeds, and all the glam of the fashion and pop culture landscapes. Each week, we curate the nail art to pin for your next nail appointment, new recruits to ‘Team Bob’, and major red carpet moments from the week’s most glamorous affairs. As always, it’s as much about celebrity beauty as it is about the makeup artists, hairstylists, and nail artists, as well as the creators crafting the trends you’re about to see everywhere.

This week in beauty was a study in contrasts: sculptural hair met soft-focus makeup, while barely-there lips shared the spotlight with high-impact eyes. Sienna Miller made a romantic case for the blurred lip, Emily Blunt embraced a wintry shade of blonde, and Kim Petras dialed up the drama with graphic liner and ice-blonde bangs. Elsewhere, Kate Young turned a diamond into the ultimate hair accessory, Kris Jenner revisited the razor-sharp bob, and Lily Allen served up a masterclass in hair transformations. From elaborate nail art to gravity-defying chignons, there was plenty to look twice at.

Scroll through the week’s best of celebrity beauty and pop culture’s glam below, and head to the app to vote for your favorite.

Jennifer Lopez was in full bombshell mode, with glossy, caramel-swirled waves by Chris Appleton, bronzed, softly sculpted makeup and a pillowy nude lip by Ernesto Casillas, and a gleaming posy pink and almond-shaped manicure courtesy of Tom Bachik.

Emily Blunt embraces the winter blonde: her icy, champagne-toned lengths fashioned into an immaculate sculptural updo by Renato Campora. A wash of rosy blush, softly defined eyes, and a precise red lip by Jenn Streicher complemented the cool-toned elegance, with pale glossy nails by Julie K.

Natanya went full celestial showgirl for her London performance, with individually placed silver Swarovski crystals tracing razor-sharp jet-black wings, icy metallic inner corners, and cherub-pink blush by Georgia Hope. Essa sculpted her glossy reddish brunette hair into a sweeping side part, with a delicate, forehead-framing kiss curl.

Lily Allen’s West End Girl tour has been a veritable hair marathon, with six distinctly different looks dreamed up by Chad Maxwell. Our favorite? A tousled, rock-chick half-updo with choppy bangs, teased volume, and loose, face-framing tendrils. Smoky ’60s eyes and a muted rose lip by Lilly Keys completed the look.

A diamond-studded twist on the classic chignon: At the CFDA Awards, Kate Young wore her platinum blonde hair slicked into a sculptural spiral bun by Jacob Rozenberg, with a sparkling, mesmerizing black-and-diamond Taffin jewel nestled at its center. This season, it doesn’t get much chicer than this piece of hair jewelry.

Nail artist Mei Kawajiri turned a set of extra-long, square-tipped acrylics into a miniature cabinet of curiosities. Scribbled illustrations, pastel washes, and translucent 3D charms—including a tiny beer mug, a white cross, and a miniature tart—made each nail its own surreal little artwork.

Once known for her signature pixie, birthday girl Kris Jenner has been enjoying the sleek, jet-black micro-bob for some time now; here, tucked behind the ears and flicked out at the ends by Justine Marjan. Ash K Holm went for fluttery lashes, softly bronzed cheeks, and a pink-nude lip, with the full, feathery brows taking center stage.

Sienna Miller was all soft-focus romance at the Madden premiere. Nina Park gave her a beautifully blurred rose-pink lip, flushed cheeks, and delicately smudged eyes, while Jenny Cho swept her honey-blonde waves into a loose, half-up style, with wispy tendrils just touching her clavicle.

Kim Petras served icy pop princess for a rave at False Idols, with waist-skimming platinum lengths and wispy, brow-grazing bangs by James Lear. Helen Rose sharpened her baby-blue eyes with graphic black wings, spiky lashes, frosted inner corners, and a glossy, overlined pink pout.

A lesson in hair origami: George Curran crafted Victoria Turner’s buttery blonde lengths into an intricate, double-tiered chignon, with two sculptural loops stacked at the nape and a single strand left loose around the face. Hair by way of hero products from Kérastase, with makeup by Amir.

‘This goal shows everyone Rodrigo is here’ – Muniz eager to add to Fulham tally

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Rodrigo Muniz is eager to score more goals and play more minutes for Fulham after netting against Newcastle United on Saturday.

The Cottagers held the Magpies to a 1-1 draw at Craven Cottage, with Muniz cancelling out Jacob Murphy's stunner for the visitors.

There were just 179 seconds between Murphy's opening goal for Newcastle in the 80th minute and Muniz equalising for Fulham in the 83rd minute.

Fulham have drawn a Premier League home game against Newcastle for the first time in 19 meetings, their first league draw in London since March 1984, in the second tier.

Muniz, meanwhile, scored his first league goal of the season, already matching his total from 2025-26, and the Brazilian is keen to push on.

"Last game was really difficult against Aston Villa, then this week we trained hard, we knew this would be a tough game," Muniz told Premier League Studios.

"Newcastle have some really good players so happy to score to help my team-mates.

"I train hard every week to get a chance to play and to score. I need to be ready. In the first half, I said to Kevin 'I need to be ready, I need to warm up well and when I come on I need to do everything'.

"Last season was really difficult for me. This goal was really important for me. Last season, I scored only one goal, not good. I want to play more and this goal shows everyone Rodrigo is here."

Top support at the Cottage.  https://t.co/Tec1GBVAjS

— Fulham Football Club (@FulhamFC) November 7, 2026

Newcastle have avoided defeat in three consecutive Premier League games in London (W2 D1) for the first time since a run of eight games between October 2022 and October 2023 (W4 D4).

Murphy scored his first Premier League goal in 19 games, his first ever left-footed goal in the competition.

Eight of Murphy's last nine Premier League goals for Newcastle have either put his side ahead (seven) or equalised (one), and the winger was delighted with the Magpies' overall performance.

"We're unhappy not to come away with three points. But we're delighted with the overall performance," Murphy told Premier League Studios.

"We've had a tough week and we bounced back. That was a collective performance. Everyone stood up and was counted for.

"We've always been a committed group and I think we showed that. We didn't let Fulham play the style they wanted, we made it uncomfortable for them and, on another day, we were 2-0 up in the first half.

"Disappointed we conceded after the goal, but it's a point in the right direction."

Inter test will reveal Como’s true level, says Fabregas

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Como have been the talk of European football this week after putting nine goals past Lens, but Cesc Fabregas feels Sunday's clash with Inter will say more about their level.

On Wednesday, Fabregas saw his team crush Lens 9-1 in the Champions League, tying the largest margin of victory in the competition's history (also Liverpool 8-0 Besiktas in 2007 and Real Madrid 8-0 Malmo in 2015).

Como also routed Manchester United 4-1 in their previous European outing, while their domestic form has been good, too.

With 23 points on the board after 10 games in Serie A, they know a victory over the Nerazzurri this weekend would see them leapfrog the Scudetto holders in the table.

Going into matchday 11, the Opta supercomputer assigned Como a 21% chance of a stunning title win, making them second-favourites behind Inter (44.3%).

But Fabregas insists Christian Chivu's side are still the benchmark in Serie A, even if they suffered a disappointing derby defeat to AC Milan last weekend.

"It feels like every week you ask me about a new test of our maturity," Fabregas told reporters.

"We knew this season would be special for us, there is so much motivation, but we know our path.

"This is an important match, we are coming off a good run, but it is only a test to see where we can go. Inter remain the strongest team in Italy – we will see our level later.

"I never stop learning from the best. The victory over Lens can happen once – if we're lucky – in our lives.

"Inter are the reigning champions of Italy, they won the Coppa Italia, they played in two Champions League finals. But who knows? Tomorrow, we might spring a surprise with the perfect performance."

Nine goals against RC Lens, a new Champions League away-win record and a night that will stay with Como 1907.  #Como1907 #UCL #RCLComo https://t.co/XBeWAU7iSn

Fabregas also highlighted Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta as an inspiration for what he is attempting to build at Como, who have risen rapidly since the former midfielder took the reins in Serie B in 2023.

"Arteta sets a great example for all of us, with what he is building at Arsenal," Fabregas said.

"The patience shown by the club, even when there were moments of tension and he was eighth in the table, that is what we need, too. I see him as inspiration for what we want to do."

Inter did get back to winning ways in Europe this week, beating Feyenoord 3-0, though they will again be without talismanic captain Lautaro Martinez on Sunday, with the Argentine still recovering from a hamstring injury.

After scoring in three consecutive Serie A games, Thuram has failed to find the net in his last two – he has not had a longer drought since a five-match streak in February and March.

The Inter forward has scored four goals in three league matches against Como, though, only bettering this tally against Parma (five goals in five games) and Torino (seven goals in five games).

Douvikas scored a hat-trick in Como's midweek thrashing of Lens, but Inter are – along with Cagliari and AC Milan, one of three teams he has faced three times in Serie A without either or assisting.

With a goal here, he would become the 10th player to reach double figures for both home and away goals in the last three Serie A seasons, after team-mate Nico Paz and Inter duo Thuram and Lautaro Martinez, the latter of whom is out injured.

Inter have won their last 11 Serie A matches against Como, their longest winning run against a single opponent in the competition's history.

Additionally, the Nerazzurri are the side against whom Como have suffered the most overall defeats in the top flight (21).

Same fixture, let’s rewind https://t.co/t4RIINElnl

Chivu's side will be desperate to bounce back following their derby defeat last weekend, which came off the back of a 19-game unbeaten run (W13 D6) – they have not lost back-to-back Serie A games since August and September 2025 (versus Udinese and Juventus).

Inter have scored 17 goals in their first five home Serie A games this season, winning all of those matches. In the last 60 years, they have scored more than 17 goals in their first six home league games on only two occasions, netting 18 in both 1999-00 and 2021-22.

But Como should not be expected to roll over, and with both teams committed to playing on the front foot, there should be fireworks.

Inter and Como have each recorded 81 high turnovers in Serie A this season, fewer only than Roma's 96. They also rank first and second in the division for 10+ pass sequences (Como 211, Inter 188), and build-up attacks (Como 57, Inter 50).

US sanctions hit 41 companies accused of keeping Prince Group’s scam network running

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Treasury says the network concealed ownership and sustained Prince Group operations, while US rules require covered assets to be blocked.

The US sanctioned four people and 41 entities on November 5, 2026, targeting a corporate network that Treasury says sustained Prince Group's operations after earlier sanctions. The designations subject covered assets to US blocking and reporting requirements, with restrictions also reaching companies that meet OFAC's ownership threshold.

The Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) said the network helped Prince Group, which it has designated a transnational criminal organization, manage offshore structures, conceal beneficial ownership and maintain commercial activities. It links the group's affiliates to digital asset investment scams, romance scams and other online fraud, with many operations relying on trafficked and coerced workers in Southeast Asian compounds.

The action follows earlier Prince Group sanctions on October 14, 2025 and June 23, 2026. The newly sanctioned individuals are Qiu Yong, Zhao Mengyao, He Yujing and Visal Pang, according to the OFAC designation record.

One chain runs from Qiu Yong through Sun Speed Holdings Limited, registered in the British Virgin Islands, to Hong Kong money lender Premium Financial Limited. Treasury says Qiu ultimately owns the lender and controls other BVI and Hong Kong companies on Prince Group's behalf.

Zhao serves as Qiu's personal assistant and as secretary and co-director of several Hong Kong companies he ultimately controls, Treasury said.

Treasury traces Qiu Yong's ownership through an offshore company to a Hong Kong lender. Explore how US blocking obligations apply.

Designated property in the United States or US persons' possession or control is blocked and reportable to OFAC.

The standing 50 Percent Rule covers direct or indirect ownership, individually or in aggregate, by one or more blocked persons.

The named target and the ownership path both matter when assessing sanctions exposure.

The ownership diagram is a selected path within the larger network. Transaction prohibitions retain general or specific OFAC license and exemption qualifications and their stated US jurisdictional scope.

Map Treasury's reported ownership chain with directional connectors, without imputing share percentages to that chain. A separate selectable panel explains the standing 50 Percent Rule and covered-property obligations from the same announcement.

The ownership diagram is a selected path within the larger network. Transaction prohibitions retain general or specific OFAC license and exemption qualifications and their stated US jurisdictional scope.

Treasury also targeted He Yujing, the wife of Prince Group executive Chen Bo, and Pang. Treasury says Chen transferred ownership of a Tokyo mansion to He after the October 2025 sanctions action. It says Pang controls 11 Cambodian companies, several co-directed with Chen, whose own US designation came on June 23, 2026.

The November action also names companies linked to previously sanctioned Prince Group executives Dai An and Thet Li. Treasury says Singapore-based Greenbay Multi Media is wholly owned by Thet Li.

Designated persons' property and interests in property in the United States, or in US persons' possession or control, are blocked and must be reported to OFAC.

The standing ownership rule also blocks entities owned 50% or more, directly or indirectly and individually or in the aggregate, by one or more blocked persons. That makes the ownership chain relevant alongside the names on the sanctions list.

Unless a general or specific OFAC license authorizes a transaction, or an exemption applies, transactions involving designated or otherwise blocked property are generally prohibited for US persons or within or transiting the United States. Treasury says violations can bring civil or criminal penalties, with civil penalties possible on a strict liability basis.

For counterparties assessing exposure, OFAC's guidance calls for ownership due diligence on entities involved in transactions and account relationships.

Liverpool are reportedly considering a move for Brighton goalkeeper Bart Verbruggen as they prepare for Alisson Becker’s potential departure next summer.

According to a report from Football Insider, the Reds are among the clubs closely monitoring the 24-year-old Dutch international, who has established himself as one of the Premier League’s most impressive goalkeepers.

Liverpool are keen to strengthen their goalkeeping department, and Verbruggen could be an excellent long-term addition. The Brighton star has registered 11 clean sheets in 14 appearances across all competitions this season, attracting attention from several elite European clubs.

The Reds are understood to have admired the Netherlands international for some time, and they could now explore bringing him to Anfield.

Alisson’s uncertain future has made the situation increasingly important for Liverpool. The Brazilian’s contract expires at the end of the season, and the club are reportedly preparing for the possibility of losing their experienced number one.

The 34-year-old has also endured some disappointing moments this campaign, raising further questions about his long-term future. Liverpool have therefore started assessing potential alternatives, with Verbruggen emerging as an attractive candidate.

However, Liverpool will face considerable competition for his signature. Manchester United and Chelsea have also been credited with an interest in the Brighton goalkeeper, while Bayern Munich are keeping tabs on his situation.

Real Madrid are another potential destination, and previous reports suggest Verbruggen could find it difficult to reject an opportunity to join the Spanish giants.

Nevertheless, Liverpool could attempt to position themselves favourably ahead of next summer’s transfer window.

At just 24, Verbruggen has plenty of room for improvement and already has valuable Premier League experience. His performances suggest he could become a reliable successor to Alisson.

It remains to be seen whether Liverpool will make a formal approach, but he could be an exciting addition to their squad.

West Ham Go Full Circle as Blackburn Expose Familiar Problems

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

West Ham’s 2-1 defeat against Blackburn Rovers was a particularly frustrating result because, for large periods of the game, Scott Parker’s side appeared to be the better team. We started brightly, enjoyed plenty of possession and created opportunities, yet somehow managed to leave Ewood Park with nothing.

What concerns me most isn’t necessarily the result itself, although that’s bad enough. It’s the manner in which West Ham gradually deteriorated as the afternoon progressed. Even after Joël Piroe pulled a goal back late on, I thought we looked increasingly disorganised and short of confidence.

And that’s perhaps the strangest aspect of Parker’s first two matches. We started against Cardiff looking completely devoid of confidence, rediscovered it during that extraordinary second-half comeback, began confidently against Blackburn and somehow finished looking just as nervous as we had before.

I was actually quite encouraged by the opening stages. West Ham pressed high, moved the ball around reasonably well and looked determined to take the game to Blackburn. Max Kilman was particularly proactive, stepping out of defence to offer an additional passing option, while Divine Mukasa and Arne Engels were regularly involved in midfield.

Down the left, Ezra Mayers and Edvin Austbø developed a promising partnership. Mayers was willing to get forward and Austbø looked lively, producing one lovely piece of skill to beat two Blackburn players before delivering a dangerous cross.

Blackburn 2-1 West Ham: Austbo was one of the Hammers better players

However, there was a problem developing on that side of the pitch. Blackburn repeatedly managed to isolate Mayers against two opponents, and while the youngster was doing some good things offensively, he wasn’t receiving enough protection defensively.

Joël Piroe was also struggling to make the most of promising situations. On one occasion, he found himself with only a single defender to beat but hesitated for so long that three opponents eventually surrounded him. West Ham were having plenty of the ball, but Blackburn were defending deeply and making it difficult to create clear opportunities.

The second half initially offered more encouragement. Mavropanos headed narrowly wide from an Engels corner, Piroe had an effort saved and then Austbø produced an excellent cross which Bowen headed against the post. At that stage, West Ham were piling on the pressure and Blackburn were struggling to get out of their own half.

Then came the sucker punch. In the 54th minute, Blackburn broke down their right, with Mayers unable to stop the delivery into the penalty area. Andri Guðjohnsen converted, but I thought several West Ham players could have done better.

Austbø might have provided more protection for Mayers, Mavropanos could have defended the situation better and I certainly felt Mads Hermansen should have been more commanding. Whether he caught the cross, punched it or simply got something on the ball, I thought the goalkeeper had an opportunity to intervene.

It was a horrible goal to concede, particularly given how much of the attacking West Ham had been doing. Parker responded by introducing Manor Solomon for Austbø, a substitution I found surprising. The Norwegian had been one of our more involved attacking players, while Solomon subsequently struggled to make any impression.

Mohamadou Kanté was then replaced by Taty Castellanos, but West Ham were finding it increasingly difficult to establish the same attacking rhythm. Blackburn’s second goal arrived in the 71st minute, with Afolayan producing an excellent finish.

Kilman had gone down following contact to his face, but too many West Ham players appeared more interested in appealing for a foul than dealing with the danger. Engels, in particular, could have done more to close down the shot. Whatever the circumstances, you cannot simply stop defending because you believe the referee should have awarded a free kick.

At 2-0, the game appeared to be slipping away, but West Ham eventually found a route back through something remarkably straightforward. Castellanos got down the right-hand side and delivered a dangerous ball into the penalty area. Piroe reacted quickly and fired high into the roof of the net in the 82nd minute.

It was an excellent finish from a striker who, in truth, hadn’t been having a particularly good afternoon. But sometimes football really is that simple. Get the ball wide, get down the line and deliver something dangerous into the box. Give your centre-forward an opportunity to attack the ball.

Suddenly it was Blackburn 2-1 West Ham, with enough time remaining to rescue a point. Unfortunately, the expected late onslaught never really materialised. In fact, I thought West Ham became progressively worse.

Mavropanos endured a dreadful few minutes, fortunate not to concede a penalty before slipping and gifting Blackburn a counter-attack. He then slipped again while attempting to recover, while Souček subsequently played a very poor pass into Lewis Orford inside our own penalty area, creating another potentially disastrous situation.

West Ham looked increasingly nervous, and the defensive organisation seemed to disappear. There were still opportunities at both ends in stoppage time, with Todd Cantwell shooting straight at Hermansen when Blackburn might have made it 3-1.

Souček then headed directly at the Blackburn goalkeeper following an excellent chipped delivery from Bowen. It was precisely the sort of position from which we’ve seen Souček score numerous times, but once again West Ham had failed to make the most of a promising opportunity.

I keep coming back to the confidence issue because I think it explains an awful lot about what we’ve witnessed over these two matches. Against Cardiff, West Ham were absolutely dreadful during the opening 45 minutes, before producing a transformation after half-time that was almost impossible to believe.

Suddenly the players looked confident, aggressive and capable of playing some very good football. That improvement appeared to carry into the opening stages at Ewood Park, where we pressed Blackburn, moved the ball with purpose and looked considerably more comfortable in possession.

Yet by the closing stages, the confidence had completely evaporated. It was almost as though we’d gone full circle in the space of two matches, ending up precisely where we started. That is particularly disappointing because I still believe this West Ham squad possesses considerably more ability than recent performances suggest.

There were some positives. Mukasa showed invention, Austbø looked dangerous, Kilman had a decent game and Piroe took his goal extremely well. But the defensive mistakes, poor decision-making and increasingly nervous football were all too familiar.

I don’t think Scott Parker can be held responsible for everything we’ve witnessed this week. He’s barely had any meaningful time on the training pitch, and the international break offers him an opportunity to start properly implementing his ideas.

However, the evidence of these two games suggests that restoring confidence isn’t simply a matter of delivering one rousing team talk. The psychological side of West Ham’s problems may be every bit as important as the tactical side.

The frustrating thing is that we’ve already seen how much better these players can perform when they believe in themselves. Against Blackburn, we watched that belief gradually disappear again.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

10 Most Extravagant Video Games of 2026

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Ronald is a video game journalist with more than 10 years of experience in the industry, working on several of the most important Spanish websites.

As someone who's been playing and analyzing games for practically half my life, I've been getting increasingly hard to impress within the interactive entertainment industry after having experienced all sorts of things.

However, this insensitivity to the regular attempts to offer a unique campaign isn't impenetrable, as every year there are developers who manage to break through my shell of apathy and leave a truly strong impression.

Whether they're good or bad games—since the ability to surprise isn't always linked to quality—I always enjoy a campaign that evokes new sensations, given it's become increasingly rare.

Therefore, if you're looking for the kind of adventure that generates feelings you can't even describe, I recommend you try any of these ten most extravagant video games of 2026.

Games that follow in Pokémon's footsteps have left me indifferent for a very long time, but I didn't expect that one of the titles that would hook me the most this year would be Grombo Card Shop Tycoon, the early access version of a game that emulates buying and selling booster packs from the famous franchise.

With simply phenomenal humor and magnificent creature design, brimming with pop culture references, this title strikes a tremendously satisfying balance between incremental mechanics and shop management, becoming exhilarating very quickly.

Dealing with customers looking for specific cards and others who want to bet on whether they'll sell you one for double the price or give it to you for free, deciding what to sell and at what price, decorating to your liking, filling the enormous skill tree... It's a satire that's as conscious and funny as it is addictive.

Never in a million years would I have imagined that opening dozens of booster packs from my own business to find golden feces with a face or a Charizard made of sausage would be so satisfying, but Grombo Card Shop Tycoon made it a reality.

Remember the LCD aesthetic of the Game & Watch? Me neither, until I stumbled across Future Knight by accident and fell completely in love with its take on a rather forgotten variant of retro gaming.

Based solely on its glorious and unique visual style, the title's presence on this list is practically essential, but if we add the plot, a homunculus tumor inside a robot who travels to the past to defeat the tyranny of a cultist who erased humanity's memory and set up a pyramid scheme as a cult, it becomes even more compelling.

Regardless, the gameplay is equally impressive, as you control both the android, based on a 90s soap opera protagonist, and the tiny companion, resulting in an extremely fun 2D action-platformer with challenging bosses and spectacular animations.

Given its arcade-style focus, Future Knight lasts three hours yet will win you over for life, especially since, unless you're approaching old age, I'm sure you've never played anything remotely similar.

Ever since I played Ghostrunner at launch, I've been forever linked to One More Level and everything bearing its name, though even with that affection, I couldn't have imagined what Valor Mortis would bring.

The Soulslike genre is usually quite traditional and rigidly adheres to its conventions, but the studio decided to focus more on its own expertise than on FromSoftware's legacy, resulting in the only game of its kind where movement is a delight.

William inherits Jack's motion abilities in a first-person action title that's an ARPG, an FPS, and a parkour experience simultaneously, blending all sorts of gameplay concepts and philosophies that one wouldn't think should work together, yet they do so magnificently.

Add to that magnificent worldbuilding in an apocalyptic France of Napoleon Bonaparte, some of the best level design the genre has ever seen, and particularly high production values compared to its peers, and Valor Mortis becomes a rather particular specimen.

If you want to spend the autumn in the safest yet most violent way, I invite you to play the ten best action games that you need to play in fall 2026.

Faith: The Unholy Trinity is among the best horror video games I've ever played, and Tenebris Somnia became the finest spiritual successor I could have imagined for such an outstanding production.

Instead of rotoscoping, the game uses cinematic cutscenes with live actors and real sets, complemented by spectacular makeup and editing work that blends seamlessly with the title's refined pixel art to create otherworldly, bizarre, and terrifying creatures.

From its themes and levels to its creative puzzles and fascinating story and mythology, Tenebris Somnia is one of the most distinctive references the genre has seen in the entire decade, as easy to spot among hundreds of titles as finding an anvil in a haystack.

Because of its fundamental ideas and the perfect pacing with which it moves between video game and cinematic experience, you'll never expect the kind of story that awaits you, much less the fidelity with which that story is translated into gameplay.

Inside the AAA spectrum, few developers are as functionally eccentric as Capcom, which, as Pragmata demonstrates, doesn't need to sacrifice experimentation to deliver a truly enjoyable title.

The campaign unfolds through the dichotomies of hacking and shooting, Hugh's brutishness and Diana's innocence, the slow pace and dynamic levels, and so on with every element that constitutes it, because it doesn't run away from the tension of conflicting concepts.

It's an emotional and charismatic story, set against its backdrop so that, along with the spirited gameplay, it never stops moving forward, always feeling like a fresh and captivating experience that you never want to stop playing.

Most big-budget products avoid contradictions and risks, and therein lies Pragmata's extravagance: going against the grain of its class and prioritizing its creative vision above all else.

Kyle Thompson's craftsmanship is becoming increasingly refined with each new Metroidvania he releases, but he has truly outdone himself in every conceivable way with Well Dweller.

Speaking of contrasts, his latest adventure leaves you feeling both profoundly unsettled and captivated by its environments and inhabitants, as the juxtaposition of gorgeous biomes and atmospheric music with gothic and unsettling designs is mesmerizing.

Fighting bosses whose behaviors and aesthetics seem lifted from sleep paralysis is magnificent, even when it involves giant hands chasing you through the pipes of a kingdom, a dollmaker trapping your sister's soul in a plush toy, or a guardian decapitated by the moon to possess his body.

Well Dweller is a collection of fables and legends seen through the lens of its action-adventure gameplay, which, needless to say, is extraordinary and offers some of the best structures in the entire genre in terms of combat, movement, exploration, and progression.

The strategy genre displays extraordinary depth, and offers some truly unique and underrated titles.

"Extravagant" means unusual, striking, and peculiar, so I can't think of better adjectives to describe Phantom Blade Zero, which continues Black Myth: Wukong's work in breaking down cultural barriers related to China.

The video game industry of this Asian giant had only previously showcased its talent on a large scale in the mobile ecosystem, but projects like S-Game's open our minds to the world of possibilities that comes with seeing the interactive medium through their eyes.

The result? A glorious action game that can't be reduced to being inspired by one or another benchmark of the genre, but rather driven by Chinese idiosyncrasies in terms of functionality, visual identity, narrative, mechanics, and sound, offering a unique journey both on paper and in execution.

Among everything released this year, Phantom Blade Zero is by far one of the most exciting and culturally significant products, something we can't say about half of the creations that boast its production values.

If Remedy Entertainment ever releases a video game that isn't an enlightening, mind-blowing experience, I'll be the first to speak out, but Control Resonant shows us that we'll still have to wait to see if that day ever comes.

The sheer audacity of making such drastic changes from its predecessor, especially after Alan Wake 2's financial hardships, is the kind of gallantry we must praise, support, and cherish to keep the art of video games alive, because it transcends mere commercial success.

Consequently, we got a title whose story, setting, characters, enemies, and even gameplay systems transcend the ordinary, becoming part of those special titles you can only sum up as "I can't tell you about it; you have to experience it".

Even though Control Resonant isn't among my top titles from the studio, it's still magnificent and light-years ahead of what the average developer can achieve, proving once again that Remedy simply operates in a league of its own.

If you’re looking for fast-paced titles to violently lose yourself in, here are ten action games most people haven’t played but definitely should.

The name Edmund McMillen has become synonymous with irreverence, weirdness, and iconic status, as you can see games like Mewgenics from miles away and still have every fiber of your being know who created it and what to expect.

However, even with full awareness of its creator's unsettling talent, mere mortals aren't prepared to anticipate the absurd display of depth, inventiveness, and ability to cohere oddities into one of the most addictive products in video game history.

I don't know what it says about our industry that such adjectives can be applied to a work that consists of breeding cats with autism, ADHD, cancer, bipolar disorder, and other conditions to battle in turn-based duels against "fetus-like enemies", feces, or angel pigeons, but I'm glad nonetheless.

If you have the willpower to dedicate less than hundreds of hours to Mewgenics, I applaud you, but I'm not that strong, because I'm afraid I have a weakness for roguelites that have more quirkiness than pixels.

Now, the 2026 game that most often left me speechless, simply because I couldn't believe what I was seeing, is Romeo is a Dead Man, which is completely to be expected if you know who Suda51 is.

Author-driven video games aren't as common a phenomenon as they are in film, but Goichi Suda is an exception to the rule precisely because of how he can take a story so often repeated throughout the centuries across every imaginable artistic medium and still make it original.

You think you know the Romeo and Juliet fable, though Romeo is a Dead Man thoroughly subverts it to create a title with mechanics from hack and slash, visual novel, on-rails shooter, third-person shooter, creature management, and other genres I can't even name.

An intergalactic, interdimensional, and intertemporal twisted love story that plays with the rules of the universe as much as with our expectations couldn't help but be extravagant, yet that's just how the great Suda51 works.

I'd venture to say it's far from being the best game on the list, but that hardly matters because, when it comes to being unique, everyone would have to ask for its blessing just to even enter the conversation.

From the distant ICO to the recent Desktop Explorer, these are ten adventure games you can finish in under 10 hours.

10 Sega Genesis JRPGs Most Haven’t Played, But Definitely Should

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Zackari Greif is a List Writer at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2021. A lifelong gamer and former writer for GameRant and Fix Gaming Channel, Zackari has written across news, guides, interviews, previews, reviews, features, and lists, bringing a broad background in gaming journalism to h

When you think of the JRPG subgenre, you think of PlayStation or the SNES. I can’t refute that Sony and Nintendo earned their stake in that video game niche as a big Kingdom Hearts and Final Fantasy 7 fan. But there’s one other console that had an expansive library of titles in that area.

Even though it was most known for its war against Nintendo and the start of Sonic the Hedgehog’s 35-year-long running streak, the Sega Genesis had a number of good JRPGs on it over the years. Games like Phantasy Star and Landstalker have been widely noticed, but most of them are only recognized by die-hard Sega fans.

Sega's 16-bit console era has left its mark on the video game industry in ways we still see today.

As a big Sega fan myself, (to a point,) I’m here to help navigate you through some Sega JRPGs you’ve most likely left behind or just weren’t around to experience. Don’t worry, we’ve got plenty to dig into.

Back in the day, and even now, Crusader of Centy has been seen as Sega’s The Legend of Zelda clone. In truth, it has a few elements that help separate it from Link’s adventures, but the biggest is that it was actually made as part of a big Genesis-based RPG push in Japan. We’ll be talking about more of these games as we go.

Out of all the games released through this initiative, I feel like Crusader of Centy is the most well-known. But out of the people who know about it, there’s a stark divide. Retro JRPG fans have likely played it, but how many Sega fans outside of that have actually experienced it? The only thing you might know about it is the fact that Sonic’s in it. Yeah. I said it.

I believe it’s a game really worth playing. It harkens back to an era where RPG and adventure game mechanics weren’t so strictly divided. Crusader of Centy also focuses pretty heavily on exploration and discovery, namely in the way that animal companions give you abilities you only get through mixing them up. There’s a lot here for almost everyone.

I know, I know, calling Beyond Oasis a JRPG is a little controversial. Its top-down, open combat and varying challenges make it hard to call it much of anything. That’s the trouble with these games sometimes. Genres were hardly the big defining points of a game as they are now, and a lot of games got to experiment with what they offered.

In the case of Beyond Oasis, if it has story beats like an RPG, has magic you can level up like an RPG no matter how basic, and has rewards for exploring through the map like an RPG, then it’s an RPG. Even if you disagree, I’m sure you agree that more people need to play this game. It’s one of the few Sega Genesis games that play this way, and it’s gorgeous to look at on top of it. If you haven’t played Beyond Oasis, I highly recommend it.

You’ve likely heard of the Shining Force series if you’re familiar with retro JRPGs. It’s one of the most highly regarded options for the genre that the Sega Genesis had, coming second to Phantasy Star itself. The first two games are on Nintendo Switch Online’s Classic offerings, even. But because of a number of different factors, like its name or its formula changes, Shining in the Darkness isn’t mentioned a lot because it hasn’t been played a lot.

For those not in the know, many of the Shining Force games are tactical RPGs. Shining in the Darkness is the first game in the series, and it was a dungeon crawler instead. It’s a very classic dungeon crawler, too, resembling a strange combination between Dragon Quest’s presentation and Wizardry’s systems.

It very much feels like a Shining game through and through, thankfully. It’s spectacularly paced like the later games are, which makes it a great introduction to the genre. It’s also less of a pain than some of it’s contemporaries at the time. We’ve all had that struggle where a retro game is a bit tougher than we expected, right? You won’t run into that here.

For every Fire Emblem and Final Fantasy Tactics that have managed to beat the odds and become popular and prominent tactical JRPGs, there’s about ten other games that didn’t get as big of a chance to shine. To me, Warrior of Rome is one of them. It leans a bit more close to something like Civilization but it I feel like I’m just playing a Fire Emblem competitor above anything else.

And it’s very fun! The first time I discovered this game, I kind of sat there and played it for three hours, completely transfixed. It’s a fairly rudimentary game, but ordering my soldiers and watching them all fight was still entertaining, even if the graphics weren’t the best the Genesis could do. Besides, Warrior of Rome tracked multiple battles I had across the map in real-time, and that was really impressive to see on the Genesis of all things. I feel like more tactical JRPG fans could try this game out. It’s a nice little snack while you wait for the bigger pillars in this little niche.

We’re back at another Zelda-like action RPG of the old days! Togi O: King Colossus is one of those games that just gets you right into what you want. You’re given simple exposition, you get a mission from the old man you live with, and get out there. If only the game was that easy on its players.

Togi O is a game with a lot of fighting. For some of you, that’s great. For others, it’ll be the most annoying thing you’ve ever dealt with on the Sega Genesis. Enemies are allowed to deal whatever punches they can throw at you, while nine times out of ten, you’re going to be left with a low-range weapon to use. It’s not exactly fair.

You also need to defeat all these enemies In crowded dungeon rooms to get items you need to progress. Items that are part of one big fetch quest, mind you. Argh. But even with all of this, Togi O might be the last stone left unturned for some of you die-hard fans out there. And like Crusader of Centy, it’s a good, retro relic that could use more eyes on it. Give it a try if you want a bit of a nostalgic challenge.

There are many elements and mechanics that JRPGs borrowed from other genres to create their wonderful works.

Back in 1991, Sega entered a strange, fleeting partnership with what’s one of the more obscure yet important JRPG developers out there, Nihon Falcom. This partnership would only last around four years, but it would lead to some important ports of Falcom games under and joint name “Sega Falcom.” One of these would be Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes.

Now, Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes was on a number of consoles, including the SNES, but the Genesis version in particular has impressively better visuals. Plus, if you’re here, you’ll likely prefer the Genesis sound font anyway.

Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes on Genesis does away with the big, bordered display with all of the menus around it and gives you the entire screen to work with. This allows sprites to breathe and big moments and battles to shine more because of it. Even if you’ve played Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes some other way, like maybe with its PSP portable remake, I still think this version is worth experiencing. It really shines on the Genesis. And that might be through the partnership that helped it thrive.

Now this is where things get interesting. There are a lot of great JRPGs on the Sega Genesis that everyone has likely played, but even at the bottom of the barrel, there’s some hidden gems. Braver Battle Saga: Legend of the Magic Warrior is one of those games. It’s an unlicensed Sega Genesis game that dates back as far as 1996. It’s earned notoriety for being a pretty good game despite lacking the golden Sega seal of approval on the box.

It’s a classic JRPG through and through despite that. Complete with quaint little towns, a rip-off ATB system, and battle music that will make you hate how many encounters there are in this game. Great for players who think the JRPGs of the olden days are better than what we’re getting now. Because of how great this game feels and plays, it’s been restored officially with modern releases and an English translation that you can find on Steam. If you’ve been craving a retro hidden gem to come out of nowhere like this, then this game is exactly what you’re looking for.

If you loved Final Fantasy Resonance, then it's time for more JRPG love, Jobs, and pixel art.

If you’ve been digging through Sega Genesis games, looking for all the RPGs that the system has to offer, then you might have heard of this one before. I tend to harp on this one a lot because it’s a baffling and head-spinning obscure game that I feel like those self-proclaimed experts of the genre should check out. Maten no Soumetsu is well-known for being impressive visually and incredibly punishing with its difficulty.

If you feel incredibly powerful because you beat Elden Ring, or just want to suffer with a game that’s truly unforgivingly hard, I dare you to pick up this one and report back to me. Good luck!

If you’re here, I’m assuming you really like mechas. Or anime. Or Fire Emblem. Happen to be a fan the first and third ones? Boy, do I have the game for you! Vixen 357 is a war strategy JRPG that brings mechas and the same staples of Fire Emblem to one fantastic Sega Genesis game.

As you might expect, you control units in the form of human-piloted mechs. I state this kind of obvious fact because Vixen 357 pulls no punches, and it shows you the pilots bleeding before the permadeath mechanics take them away. This, combined with some gorgeous graphics, is why Vixen 357 has been saved and has recently been ported to modern consoles through Ratalaika Games and Shinyuden. Go check it out!

Remember when I spoke about an initiative back in the day that aimed to put more JRPGs on the Sega Genesis in Japan? Some games made its way over, but not all of them. Surging Aura is one of those games. It’s circled around the import and translation scene for years as a great game that was locked behind the lack of a full translation.

I’m happy to report that in the time since I last wrote about Surging Aura, a full English translation patch has been completed! Yes, you’re reading this right. There’s a brand-new (sort of) JRPG that you’ve likely never played ripe for the taking right now! Surging Aura feels like it was meant to be Sega’s answer to Final Fantasy, but it has its own distinct personality and charm to where it feels like its own unique adventure.

We have the character and monster design of the late Mutsumi Inomata to thank for that. If you’re big on JRPGs, that name might sound familiar. She was the character designer for the Tales of series starting with Tales of Destiny. So, if you’ve been a fan of her work, this game is calling out to you specifically. It’s worth your time, I promise.

While some JRPGs disappear, others return from oblivion after years without new releases.

Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.

Indie App Spotlight: ‘CueFlip’ automatically turns the page for your sheet music

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Welcome to Indie App Spotlight. This is a weekly 9to5Mac series where we showcase the latest apps in the indie app world. If you’re a developer and would like your app featured, get in contact.

If you find yourself playing an instrument with sheet music, flipping the page often requires you to stop playing to turn the page. CueFlip solves that. You can teach it how you play a song, then it can turn the page for you whilst listening.

CueFlip doesn’t just assume how it should turn the page, you can explicitly teach it how to. That starts with either a live rehearsal, or a recording you can share with it. Then, you mark the turns.

CueFlip also has a built in dictionary, where you can see certain notes on a page so you can learn how the composer meant to play them, rather than needing to guess them. Many times, these terms will be in other languages, but CueFlip offers built in translation with up to 13 languages. CueFlip covers over 20,000 terms, and it can pull terms from scanned scores as well.

Lastly, CueFlip has a neat little feature with Face ID. If you wink with your right eye, you can manually turn a page forward, and if you wink with your left eye, it’ll go backwards. Similarly, CueFlip also works with manual Bluetooth page turners in addition to the automatic turning, in case you need it.

Here’s why 15 year old classical guitarist Yuze Pan from Beijing decided to build CueFlip:

I play in the Primal Guitar Quartet, and page turns kept breaking up our rehearsals. Paper means taking a hand off the strings, a pedal or a wink is one more thing to remember in the middle of a phrase, and timed turns can’t follow a performer who speeds up and slows down. My first attempt, in 2025, didn’t work. In May 2026 I started again, this time by reading the research on audio alignment, and brought every new build to the quartet’s daily rehearsals.

CueFlip is available for free on the App Store for iPhone and iPad users running iOS and iPadOS 16.4 and later. The free version includes PDFs with annotations and automatic page turning for one score, whereas Pro unlocks unlimited page turning, the music dictionary, wink to turn pages, and much more for $4.99/month and $29.99/year.

Incredible news, sensational English wonderkid is coming back to Liverpool after injury

There's some great news in the air. An Englishman is coming back to Liverpool.

Iraola has led them to the quarter-finals of the League Cup, he's secured three wins in four games in the Champions League and the Reds are also in contention for the top four spots in the Premier League.

At the start of the season when the club will have outlined their ambitions for the current campaign those would have been the goals they wanted Iraola to achieve.

Competing for some domestic silverware, doing well in the Champions League and securing a top four spot will count as a successful debut campaign for the Spaniard.

Liverpool enter race for breakout European wonderkid

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have enjoyed great success in signing young talents and turning them into first-team stars.

Rio Ngumoha and Trey Nyoni are two perfect examples from Andoni Iraola's current squad, but the likes of Harvey Elliott and Raheem Sterling show that the Reds have been at this game for quite some time.

There are fewer places that offer a better pathway for wonderkids than Liverpool's Kirkby academy, and the recruitment team at Anfield are now reportedly trying to entice one of the world's most exciting prospects to Merseyside.

A new report has claimed that Liverpool are lining up a move for a generational talent, but they will face tough competition.

How long should a PC processor actually last?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

If you treat it right, it should last until it's obsolete.

Building a new PC is a major investment, especially with prices of some components skyrocketing recently. Given the expense, it's natural to wonder not just if you can afford it now, but how long a PC should last if you do fork over the cash to build one. The central processing unit (CPU) is one of the biggest expenses in building a PC, but you likely won't need to buy a new one too often.

Under most conditions, you don't need to worry about your processor wearing out from regular use. Manufacturers don't list specific expected lifespans for their processors, but it's not uncommon for a CPU to remain functional for a decade or more after it's installed. Rather than your CPU failing, it's much more likely that you'll eventually want to replace it when it can't keep up with the new games or programs you use your PC for.

The most common time for a processor to fail is when it's still new. The manufacturing process can produce faulty CPUs from time to time, and when that happens, they'll likely give you trouble from the start. If you've had your CPU for a while, some of the signs that it's failing are your computer freezing for no clear reason, failing to boot or randomly restarting. All of these problems can be caused by other hardware, though, and pinning the problem to the CPU is as difficult as it is rare.

If your computer isn't performing as you'd like it to, it's worth checking if your CPU is just outdated. First, pull up your PC's Task Manager and check the Performance tab. Keep an eye on the CPU screen to see how much of the processor's power is being used. Under normal conditions, it should almost always stay below 20 percent. Some spikes are normal, but if it's constantly approaching its upper limits, you might want to consider an upgrade. Your CPU will be much more active during intensive tasks like gaming, but if it stays at 90 percent or more, especially if your GPU usage stays low at the same time, that could be cause for concern.

The biggest stressor to your PC is heat, which degrades performance of every component over time. The best thing you can do for your PC's longevity is to make sure it has adequate airflow by properly installing fans and regularly cleaning dust out of the case. Programs like HWiNFO let you monitor your system's temperature — if your CPU rises above 60 degrees Celsius under normal load or above 80 degrees during intensive tasks, it's worth looking into what improvements you can make. These numbers can vary based on the particular CPU, though, so it's best to follow manufacturer recommendations if they're available. If temperatures are high, you should also consider replacing the thermal paste on your CPU, which can last five years or more but eventually becomes less effective at regulating heat.

Overclocking your CPU — increasing its speed beyond the manufacturer's recomendations — can also reduce its lifespan, though usually it will still last until it's obsolete if you've overclocked safely. Other than time and heat, the most likely threat to your CPU is damage from a power surge or a faulty power supply. While rare, these are still more likely than a CPU simply "going bad" with age. It can be difficult to pinpoint exactly which component is failing without going to a professional technician, but with even minimal care, your CPU should keep ticking well past the point when it's time for an upgrade.

Mathys Tel, the big controversy!

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Tough sequence for Mathys Tel. Friday, the Tottenham striker could hope to appear in Zinédine Zidane’s last list for the France A team. With the injuries (Dembélé, Lepaul, Akliouche, etc.), there was a small opening, a place to take, finally seized by Pablo Pagis (Paris FC).

On Saturday, with his club Tottenham, Mathys Tel was the victim of a nasty foul by Jayden Bogle, the Leeds defender. The French striker had his left ankle crushed, and the tendon trampled, by a failed intervention by his opponent.

Orsolini praise for Bologna coach Palladino is damning for Tedesco

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Bologna won their fourth game in a row under new coach Raffaele Palladino, as Riccardo Orsolini pins the blame on sacked Domenico Tedesco. ‘We’re enjoying ourselves again.’

The Rossoblu had got off to a truly disastrous start under former Belgium international coach Tedesco, scraping one point from the opening four Serie A matches.

All three defeats were 1-0 scorelines, and he was fired on September 16 to bring in Palladino.

It has been a complete transformation, drawing the opener and then winning five of the next six games, faltering only 3-1 to Inter.

“The coach arrived in a bit of a rush, because we had started so badly and there was no time to discuss too much,” Orsolini told DAZN Italia after today’s 2-1 win away to Parma, their fourth on the bounce.

“We had to win games, he did well to get us all on the same page and bring us back together, seeing as this was a team that had done well over the years. We lost some important players over the summer, but the skeleton of that team remained.”

When Orsolini had praise for Palladino, he namechecked several previous Bologna bosses too, but notably left Tedesco out of the list.

“I’ve been here for many years and went through a lot of different situations. I enjoyed myself with Thiago Motta, with Vincenzo Italiano, and now we are enjoying ourselves again with Palladino.

“I don’t know how far this team can go, but we want to enjoy ourselves together. We rediscovered our smile.”

Bologna are climbing back up the table after four consecutive victories against Cagliari, Milan, Monza and Parma, but notably three of those were away from home.

This was Orsolini’s second goal of the season in just five appearances, as it was his comeback from almost a month out with a thigh injury.

Trump targets Slotkin in call for DOJ probe of alleged Democratic threats to Republicans

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump said Saturday that he was ordering the Justice Department to investigate Democratic leaders over what he described as threats against Republican officials, donors and supporters, without identifying the alleged communications or providing evidence of criminal conduct.

"The Democrats are going around threatening a lot of people, including the Attorney General of the United States, and others, with Impeachment and many other forms of punishment, even though they admit they have no reason to do so," Trump said in a Truth Social post.

The alleged threats, the president said, are "extortion at the highest level," he wrote. "I am ordering the Department of Justice to look into this criminal act, immediately!"

In his post, Trump claimed that Democratic Sen. Elissa Slotkin of Michigan is planning to "go after all young people that believe in Republican Values."

Trump was apparently referencing a speech Slotkin made at a University of Chicago event on Oct. 13. At the time, Slotkin said: "I want young people coming out of school to say, 'Should I go work for the Trump administration?'...I want them to be like, 'Lord, that might be buying myself a ton of problems that I don't want. And so let me avoid participating in that.' I want that deterrence message sent."

Slotkin alleges that she, her family and her staff faced physical and legal intimidation after the posting of a November 2025 video in which Slotkin and other Democratic leaders called on the U.S. military to "refuse illegal orders" following the airstrikes targeting boats in the Caribbean.

On Nov. 20, Trump called the video treason and said "an example must be set."

The FBI contacted lawmakers, including Slotkin, days later. In February, Slotkin declined to sit for an interview the Justice Department requested about the video.

"The intimidation is the point, and I'm not gonna go along with that," the senator said in February. A federal grand jury later declined to indict the video participants.

Now, Trump alleges Democrats are threatening legal intimidation against Republican leaders and their supporters.

"Republicans should get smart, fast! Threatening letters have been sent to many people, all innocent, from Democrat Political Leaders," the president wrote.

The White House and Slotkin didn't immediately respond to a request for comment.

Bill Skarsgard Says He’s “Spent” Playing Bad Guys but Would Still Play a James Bond Villain

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The 'Nosferatu' star also said he wasn't approached to play the next 007: "I wasn't even thrown into the mix."

Bill Skarsgard is feeling “spent” when it comes to playing villains.

The 36-year-old actor is known for taking on dark characters, including Pennywise in the It movies, Count Orlok in Nosferatu, Eric Draven in The Crow and Marquis Vincent Bisset de Gramont in John Wick: Chapter 4. But during a recent episode of Josh Horowitz’s Happy Sad Confused podcast, Skarsgard explained that he’s becoming more selective about taking on villain roles.

“For a villain, it would have to be something that felt original to me, that was something that I hadn’t done before,” Skarsgard said. “I feel, kind of, spent with those things. But there’s villains [that] could be really fun in different ways and if it’s the right kind.” Related Stories Movies Josh O'Connor Calls Bond Speculation "Incredibly Bizarre Pinch Me Moment" Movies Pierce Brosnan Supports Callum Turner Being Cast as James Bond: "So Strong..."

Horowitz then brought up Skarsgard’s longtime interest in playing a villain in the James Bond franchise.

“Yeah! Well, because they didn’t call me about James Bond. I don’t know what that was about,” Skarsgard quipped. “Maybe they were like, ‘We can’t do a Swede for this guy.’ I wasn’t even thrown into the mix.”

The Dead Man’s Wire actor agreed with Horowitz that he wouldn’t necessarily want to commit to leading the James Bond franchise for 10 or 15 years. Instead, he said he’d be interested in taking on a one-off villain role, citing Mads Mikkelsen and Javier Bardem as examples.

Skarsgard’s comments come as the search for the next actor to play 007 continues ahead of Denis Villeneuve’s upcoming film. The Hollywood Reporter previously reported that Callum Turner, Jack Lowden and Josh O’Connor had screen tested for the part. However, on Friday, THR reported that Robert Pattinson had also screen tested, while Nicholas Hoult’s name has emerged as another potential contender.

As for Skarsgard, he stars in Peter Berg’s war film The Mosquito Bowl, which is currently playing in theaters and set to release on Netflix Nov. 25.

Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 Goes Free for a Limited Time

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Call of Duty will always be a major force in the gaming zeitgeist; that much is certain, but the recent success of Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 was a much-needed win for the franchise. With the campaign receiving fantastic reception, the multiplayer rolling out, and the inclusion of the extraction mode DMZ, Activision is looking to keep racking up wins.

Former Skyrim lead designer Bruce Nesmith says Todd Howard knows when to make things simpler for players, and recalls a design decision that proved him wrong.

Their newest incentive was announced on the official Call of Duty Twitter: Modern Warfare 4’s DMZ is free to play for all until November 11th, giving new and returning players a taste of extraction action exclusive to the Modern Warfare reboot series. Serving as MW’s answer to Zombies, the mode has been totally revamped in preparation for new blood.

DMZ has stuck with the Modern Warfare reboot series since MW2, serving as the title’s unique mode alongside Black Ops Zombies. While Zombies leaned into the absurd paranoia and conspiracies that swirl around undercover operations, DMZ focuses on the realism and boots-on-the-ground feel that originally made Warzone work so well, playing off Modern Warfare’s thematic strengths.

MW4’s DMZ dropped only a few days ago, but has already hit the ground running, with story missions, PvE encounters, and plenty of incentives to play cool or callous when other players show. Packed with new weapons, gear, and locations, MW4’s DMZ is set on the Korean Peninsula, Hajin, where the devastation that rocked the campaign is still felt.

Stuck between squadrons of NPC soldiers, a dangerous map, and the X factor of players, the free trial will populate Hajin even more, sparking hijinks and carefully curated tactics in equal measure. Any progress made by players during their free playtime will translate to progression in the main game, giving prospective players a hit of the game’s action without asking for the full $69.99.

With the campaign receiving fantastic reception, the multiplayer rolling out, and the inclusion of the extraction mode DMZ, Activision is looking to keep racking up wins.

That price tag is honestly the biggest pain point for many players, but Modern Warfare 4’s new rewards for completing weapon progression, multiple modes with a variety of unlocks, and a phenomenal upgrade of the franchise’s gunplay make it a solid buy for anyone content with sticking to a single title for a while.

MW4’s reinventions were fully needed to pull the subseries out of the total rut that was Modern Warfare 3, and it’s incredibly exciting to see where the game will go both with its internal modes and as a guiding star for the franchise going forward. Black Ops might be up next on the rotation, but there’s no shame in taking notes from your neighbor.

Call of Duty stands alongside Grand Theft Auto as major gaming icons that have avoided aligning with any specific platform. While GTA 6 is releasing on PC at a later date, Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 dropped everywhere simultaneously, even on the Switch 2. The title’s sales give universal releases a good argument, even as console first-party titles are being tied back down.

With the gaming community finally back on its side, Modern Warfare 4 has some big opportunities to recover the franchise’s PR, which has been stumbling after questionable cosmetics and stuttering quality. Modern Warfare 4 is sticking to its aesthetic and goals well for now, but time will only tell if Warzone’s inclusion will make odd collabs start slinking in.

Infinity Ward has some time to rest on their laurels and focus on live service updates, with Season 1’s launch already bringing players back in. With a full DMZ trial, a good chunk of players are sure to stick around, even if those already in the game treat them like easy pickings for the duration.

As GTA 6's launch approaches, everyone wants part of the PR pie, including internet ISPs.

Hotel Refills Toiletries From Ketchup-Style Bottles—This Is Why You Can’t Trust What’s In The Shower

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

This upscale hotel’s housekeeping cart looks ready to dress a hamburger: red, yellow and white squeeze bottles lined up beside the guest supplies. The red one says “Body Wash.” In the shower, the bottles say Klur. Travel journalist Scott Mayerowitz photographed the setup and compared it to the condiments at McDonald’s. He’s glad the hotel is refilling its toiletries. I’m looking at the same photo and wondering how guests are supposed to know what they’re actually washing with. A post shared by Scott Mayerowitz (@globetrotscott) His follow-up photo on LinkedIn shows Klur Repose shampoo, conditioner and body wash in the shower, in the Clary Sage + Lavender scent. My guess, eyeballing the hallway and bathroom, is the Omni Boston Hotel at the Seaport. Readers familiar with the property can confirm or correct that. A September review documents Klur toiletries there, and Klur supplies Omni more broadly. The red squeeze bottle isn’t literally unlabeled. It’s self-labeled. The bottle identifies as body wash. What it doesn’t show is whose body wash. The contents may be genuine Klur, though it seems odd if it were that it would be in a ketchup squeeze bottle. Best case, this looks cheap. They went to the trouble of selecting a branded amenity for the bathroom, then made the refill process look like the back of a burger joint. I’ve Reported This At Hyatt Hotels, Too In May 2025, I wrote about Hyatt Regency San Francisco’s ketchup-style refill jug. A reader photographed an unbranded squeeze container being carried with housekeeping supplies. Guests could see the dispenser in their bathroom, but couldn’t identify the product supplying it. Then, in December, a reader found condiment containers on a housekeeping cart at Hyatt Place Phoenix Mesa. That cart also had a toilet brush beside fresh towels, which didn’t improve the presentation. At Hyatt’s Wild Palms Hotel in Sunnyvale, California, a reader photographed DRIFT bulk toiletries on housekeeping carts while the guest bathroom bottles displayed Jonathan Adler branding. The hotel advertised Jonathan Adler amenities. When One Mile at a Time followed up in January, Ben reported that the labels had been removed from the bulk jugs, and published new photographs. The hotel was still advertising Jonathan Adler. And at the Suining Marriott in China, management there actually acknowledged swapping out cheaper stuff. A guest found FORMETURE products instead of Marriott’s thisworks amenities. The general manager said the owner had changed products to save money. The hotel denied that the replacement was counterfeit, saying it was a legitimate product with quality documentation. It promised to restore the brand standard, and the guest received 8,000 points. Does This Break Any Rules? Klur’s hospitality products are is sold in bulk refill containers. I’d expect those to be used rather than squeeze bottles. Putting a different product into a Klur-labeled bottle could create a misbranding problem. Handling cosmetics under conditions that could contaminate them can violate adulteration rules. And passing off a cheaper product as the promised brand could also run afoul of Massachusetts law against deceptive business practices. Bulk Toiletries Leave More For The Guest To Worry About I’ve been criticizing this move for years. Buying larger containers can reduce packaging (but they can opt for single use biodegradable packaging too). It also lets hotels spend less on toiletries. I’ve shown a Sheraton’s bulk toiletries sitting beside disposable plastic cups, with plastic water bottles elsewhere in the room. They were surely using bulk toiletries out of an ideological commitment to end plastic use. The refill routine adds opportunities to mix up products or introduce contamination. TA 2023 study of 104 hotel soap dispensers in Germany found bacterial contamination in 70.2% of refillable pump dispensers, compared with 10.6% of nonrefillable press dispensers. Bottles that can be opened can be tampered with, and I’ve found supposedly secured dispensers left unlocked. And housekeeping has to remember to fill them. I once returned to the same room at a Marriott Courtyard a week later and found the bath gel dispenser still empty. It had been empty throughout the previous stay, too. You usually discover that problem after you’ve gotten into the shower. Hotels have also given up something that helped sell both the stay and the toiletry brand. I started using Aesop after staying at the Park Hyatt Tokyo. Taking a little bottle home extended the experience and introduced me to a product I still use. They were surely using bulk toiletries out of an ideological commitment to end plastic use. The refill routine adds opportunities to mix up products or introduce contamination. TA 2023 study of 104 hotel soap dispensers in Germany found bacterial contamination in 70.2% of refillable pump dispensers, compared with 10.6% of nonrefillable press dispensers. Bottles that can be opened can be tampered with, and I’ve found supposedly secured dispensers left unlocked. And housekeeping has to remember to fill them. I once returned to the same room at a Marriott Courtyard a week later and found the bath gel dispenser still empty. It had been empty throughout the previous stay, too. You usually discover that problem after you’ve gotten into the shower. Hotels have also given up something that helped sell both the stay and the toiletry brand. I started using Aesop after staying at the Park Hyatt Tokyo. Taking a little bottle home extended the experience and introduced me to a product I still use. I’ve been criticizing this move for years. Buying larger containers can reduce packaging (but they can opt for single use biodegradable packaging too). It also lets hotels spend less on toiletries. I’ve shown a Sheraton’s bulk toiletries sitting beside disposable plastic cups, with plastic water bottles elsewhere in the room. They were surely using bulk toiletries out of an ideological commitment to end plastic use.

The refill routine adds opportunities to mix up products or introduce contamination. TA 2023 study of 104 hotel soap dispensers in Germany found bacterial contamination in 70.2% of refillable pump dispensers, compared with 10.6% of nonrefillable press dispensers.

Bottles that can be opened can be tampered with, and I’ve found supposedly secured dispensers left unlocked. And housekeeping has to remember to fill them. I once returned to the same room at a Marriott Courtyard a week later and found the bath gel dispenser still empty. It had been empty throughout the previous stay, too. You usually discover that problem after you’ve gotten into the shower.

Hotels have also given up something that helped sell both the stay and the toiletry brand. I started using Aesop after staying at the Park Hyatt Tokyo. Taking a little bottle home extended the experience and introduced me to a product I still use.
