The 42 Best Gifts for All Types of Travelers, From Luxe Flight Essentials to Road Trip Gadgets

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Shower your favorite five-star vagabonds with these treats for their next staycation or whirlwind adventure.

By Danielle Directo-Meston, Erin Lassner November 7, 2026 12:27pm

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, The Hollywood Reporter may receive an affiliate commission.

For some travelers, there’s nothing like rolling up to the gate with a luxe piece of luggage and well-prepared flight kit, or setting out on a road trip with all of the gear needed for a smooth ride. Some of the best gifts for travelers who love a luxurious experience don’t need to cost an arm and a leg — plenty of essentials can turn any trip into a five-star one.

For instance, compact toiletry bags with the perfect number compartments can keep them organized like a jet-setting pro, while comfy airport shoes make security checks feel like a breeze. Glampers will love a chic camera for capturing their memories. And for those bitten by the travel bug (and itching for their next adventure), a subscription to coffee or snacks from around the world will transport them back to their favorite places. Related Stories Lifestyle Parke's Collab With Stoney Clover Lane Is Back by (Very) Popular Demand Lifestyle Streaming More, Spending Less: T-Mobile's 5G Home Internet Deal Starts at $30 a Month

Check out our top picks below, from softside luggage by Hollywood-loved brand Away (Meghan Markle and Jessica Alba have been spotted with its gear) to red-eye-ready essentials from Tumi (F1 superstar Lando Norris and singer Reneé Rapp have starred in its campaigns) and more. Whether you’re shopping for holiday gifts, or you’re looking for presents for the frequent flyers and road-trippers in your life, we’ve rounded some of the best travel gifts for every type of adventurer.

Got a homesick transplant or someone who just wants to rep their home state? Celebrity-loved designer Maya Brenner’s pendant necklaces are available in a range of countries (including France, England, Italy, China, Japan, Mexico, Germany and more) and all 50 U.S. states. Each charm comes on a 16-inch cable chain in sterling silver or 14-karat gold. Meghan Markle, Abigail Spencer, Rihanna and Yvonne Strahovski and are just a few stars who’ve worn her pieces.

For only $130, Quince’s roomy polycarbonate shell carry-on has 360-degree spinner wheels, a sturdy aluminum telescopic trolley handle with soft rubber for comfort, interior compression panels, TSA-approved locks and a removable laundry bag. The capacity offers enough space for six to nine outfits, and there’s a 18-by-12.5-inch interior pocket for storage. Available in a range of colorways — both core and seasonal — Quince’s signature suitcase is also offered in a smaller carry-on size and multiple checked-in varieties.

Hoka’s Clifton 11 is the latest iteration of the brand’s versatile Clifton line. These everyday sneakers are one of our favorite shoes for traveling, thanks to their heel tab that makes pulling them on a breeze after TSA checks. The sturdy silhouette also makes it easy to run through airports and cities alike.

The Empire Carryall Bag 48 is genuinely our dream weekender, especially in the newer Loved Leather finish which only gets better with age. Bonus: Its intentional lived-in look means you don’t have to be too precious with it — this stunner is suited for even the craziest of travel days.

For a long day of travel, there’s no better companion than an insanely quenching mask, and 111Skin’s Celestial Black Diamond Eye Mask is just that. Specifically created to target tired-looking eyes, the cult-favorite skin product is a Hollywood magnet, with the brand’s famous fans including Anne Hathaway, Kim Kardashian, Jennifer Anniston and Bella Hadid.

At first glance, this duo looks like an ultra chic toiletry bag set. And yes, it is. But it’s also so much more. Lined with Pristinium, OTM’s signature temperature-protective material, each case is not just created to carry your products, but built to protect them — ensuring all your formulas work just as intended.

Perfect as a stocking stuffer gift for travelers who don’t want to leave their beauty and grooming routines at home, this 11-piece travel set comes with reusable bottles and containers in stylish black and white prints. It includes three flat squeeze pouches, a mini spray bottle, two pump bottles, two mini jars with resealable lids and a storage case, plus a funnel, spatula and pipette for easily refilling. Everything is TSA approved and BPA-free, too.

For the traveler who loves to keep their baggage ultra-light, Samsonite’s Proxis global carry-on spinner weighs just 4.7 pounds. The expandable suitcase is made of the company’s durable new Roxkin outer shell that’s designed to bounce back, while the built-in USB port lets jet-setters keep their tech powered. Other features include TSA-friendly locks, rubber spinner wheels and interior organizers and zippered pockets.

If there’s one thing that many travelers learned in the past few years, it’s that lost luggage can put a tailspin in your plans. A smart tracker — like Apple’s AirTag — can help travelers keep tabs on their belongings.

Maison Wheat’s super-soft basics are our go-to uniforms for flights and long car rides, thanks to their eco-friendly blend of Tencel modal and spandex that make for easy layering on the plane. Our favorite pairing? We love the women’s Knoxville ribbed tank with an elevated silhouette and the Los Angeles joggers with a comfy, elastic-free waistband and tapered look. For jet-setting men, we recommend the Oxford quarter zip or the San Diego sweatpant. (The brand was co-founded by actor Huw Collins and his wife, Molly Shaheen.)

When they can’t decamp to far-flung destinations or don’t want to leave their favorite fragrances at home, this trio of luxe perfumes by cult-cool luxury brand Byredo will keep recipients smelling chic during their travels. (Anne Hathaway, Rosie Huntington-Whiteley and stylist Courtney Mays have said they’re fans.)

The three-piece set includes the best-selling scents Bal d’Afrique (a warm fragrance with notes of bergamot, lemon, neroli, African marigold, violet, musk, Moroccan cedarwood and more), the light and floral Blanche (notes of white rose, pink pepper, peony and sandalwood) and the woody Gypsy Water (bergamot, lemon, juniper berries, pepper, pine needles and vanilla).

Minimalist nature lovers won’t mind forgoing the fuss that comes with massive vacation homes with gift card for Postcard Cabins. Formerly Getaway, the tiny cabin rental company’s dog-friendly Outposts are located just outside of Atlanta, Boston, Chicago, Dallas, Los Angeles, Nashville, New Orleans, New York, Portland and other major cities, and stays range from $99 to $319 and up per night.

If those airline-provided pillows aren’t supportive enough, Purple’s Harmony Anywhere travel bundle may be a great gift for frequent flyers who want a more comfortable option. It features a pillow with a ventilated Talalay latex core and GelFlex grid.

For the frequent traveler, peace of mind is one of the best gifts. Get them safely from the tarmac to their dreamy destination (or home after a long day in the air) with a pre-booked private car from Blacklane, which has a global carbon offset program. The company’s iOS and Android app makes it easy to hail a ride or schedule one in advance, and its professional chauffeurs will treat your giftee to a five-star experience with door-to-door pick-up and drop-off in cities such as New York, Los Angeles, London, Dubai, Santa Barbara, Palm Springs, Miami and beyond. You can also hire chauffeurs by the hour and book seamless airport transfers (drivers will track your flight and wait up to one hour for free). The best part? Pricing is all-inclusive, so no need for your recipient to bring cash for tips.

Not every hotel room or charming Airbnb is designed with modern technology in mind — and for frequent travelers who also tote around cameras, smartwatches, tablets and other gear, this international power adapter ensures that their gadgets are juiced up no matter the locale.

Inspire your favorite traveler to relive their adventures with a gift card to Artifact Uprising, which lets recipients create beautiful hardcover photo books, calendars, framed prints, wood display boxes and other memorabilia.

Whether we’re flying or sitting in the passenger, Cabeau’s neck pillow is our go-to for snoozing comfortably while traveling. (Kristen Bell, Lizzo and Heather McMahan have also flown with it.) The adjustable strap and raised sides keep your head from rolling forward (or onto your seat neighbor) and straining your neck, and the design helps to support spine alignment. We like that it folds away into a compact case that can be attached to your backpack to save space.

Foster a love for languages and gift them a lifetime membership to Rosetta Stone. Plans include access to over 25 languages (including French, Spanish, Italian, Japanese, Portuguese, Swedish, Tagalog, Greek and much more) through expert-designed Dynamic Immersion courses and TruAccent speech recognition for perfecting accents.

Photography enthusiasts won’t mind making space in their luggage for Polaroid’s travel-friendly Go instant camera, which clocks in at just 4.1 inches long by 3.3 inches wide by 2.4 inches tall. The palm-sized device produces approximately 2.6-by-2-inch photos, and shutterbugs can play with double exposure, flash or no flash and a self-timer.

A luxe cashmere scarf is a vacation-ready staple that your giftee might not splurge on for themselves, making it the perfect travel gift. Jet-setting stars like Angelina Jolie, Eva Longoria, and Jennifer Garner are among those who have worn White + Warren’s best-selling oversized wrap, which can transform into a warm blanket on chilly days and cold flights. This unisex accessory is made of seven-gauge, two-ply cashmere and measures 81 inches by 36 inches. It’s available in an array of colors and in a smaller size.

For travelers with a sensitive stomach, LifeStraw’s handy reusable stainless steel straws are a discreet way to drink tap water while filtering out dirt, bacteria and microplastics. The Climate Neutral Certified company is known for its humanitarian efforts to make clean water accessible in underserved communites, refugee camps and elsewhere through its Safe Water Fund, while its give-back program helps to provide a year of safe water to a child in need with every straw purchase.

Label lovers can arrive at their destination in style with Gucci’s signature GG duffle made with the fashion house’s iconic monogram design.

If your adventure-loving giftee is furry and four-pawed, this hiking backpack by Ruffwear ensures they (and their biped parent) have the proper carryall for stowing a day’s worth of essentials. It has two saddlebags designed to stabilize the weight, reflective trim for the evening and other low-visibility settings, and reinforced leap attachment points.

For the active adventurer (or fitness lover) who lives in their leggings (and hates VPL), comedy writer-founded brand Skimpies makes one of the best gifts for women who want to travel light. The organic cotton liner is designed to replace underwear — recipients simply stick it directly into their leggings, shapewear or other tight-fitting bottoms to enjoy wedgie-free adventures.

For the build-their-own-adventure type who also doesn’t mind letting a pro handle the planning, a gift card to 57 Hours will let them book outdoor excursions guided by an expert. From rock climbing in Joshua Tree and backpacking in Alaska, to hiking Iceland’s volcanic trails and a 15-day trek on the Annapurna circuit in Nepal, there’s truly something for every type of expeditioner.

For travel-obsessed families, No Reception Club’s Hideaway Carry-On Duffel is a versatile piece of luggage for parents who don’t want to sacrifice style and organization when adventuring with kids. The removable Cubby packing organizer has customizable sections for flexibility, and the bundle includes a dopp kit, a pair of hanging straps, detachable backpack straps, a sternum strap and laundry sack.

If their perfect vacation involves cozying up in a cabin or decorating their own abode like a home away from home, author Laura May Todd’s How to Get Away is filled with inspiring imagery and expertise from architects, designers and homeowners. From modern forest respites and contemporary cottages to coastal retreats and more, the 256-page coffee table book explores seven types of retreats that will spark even more wanderlust.

For when they can’t hop on a plane and sip on an espresso or latte at a coffee bar across the world, Atlas Coffee Club’s monthly subscription will deliver global cups of joe to them instead along with a postcard from where the beans were grown. Choose from half, single or double bag subscriptions every two or four weeks; as well their roast preference (light to dark or a variety) and grind type (whole bean or ground).

This set of seven hexagonal capsules by Cadence will help your giftee save space while keeping their skincare, jewelry, medication and more all in one place. The tops, sides and labels are magnetic, so the pieces snap together into a honeycomb design, and the wide mouth makes it easy to add your favorite apothecary products (while ditching the travel-sized bottles). The labels are removable and customizable, and the containers are made from a blend of recycled plastic pulled from beaches and reused scrap plastic.

The outdoorsy folks on your list will appreciate Cotopaxi’s newly-designed Allpa 35L backpack that now boasts 100 percent recycled fabrics, mesh shoulder straps for comfort, an exterior stretch water bottle pocket and a luggage pass-through strap. It’s got plenty of zippered organizers and pockets inside to keep you organized for a week, too (which is why it’s our favorite compact carry-on backpack).

Overpacking can be totally avoidable when you give your favorite traveler this portable luggage scale, which can weigh baggage up to 110 pounds. The pocket-sized device conveniently turns off automatically to save the battery, and it also senses your luggage’s temperature.

Whether they’re a touring musician or a jet-setting festgoer, these reusable noise-canceling earplugs by Vibes protect your giftee’s hearing. Fitted with silicon tips (which come in different sizes for a snug fit), the earbuds filter out damaging decibel levels without silencing out all sound, so wearers can still enjoy concerts and other environments where they want to be tuned into their surroundings.

When they don’t know what weather they’ll be landing in, Quince’s water-repellent puffer jacket (also available in women’s sizes) makes it easy for giftees to pack and unpack for unpredictable temperatures.

Away’s expandable softside carry-on gives travelers the peace of mind that their belongings will stay dry and protected on almost any trip.

Puffer Hug’s signature travel blanket has a fleece-lined kangaroo pocket and a cozy foot pocket, and folds into a compact pillow — making it a great gift for road trippers and jetsetters alike.

Available in chic colorways, celeb-loved travel brand Tumi’s compact crossbody has zippered pockets on the inside and outside, plus a handy key leash — making it versatile for flights, errands and every activity in between.

For the vagabonds who want to be ready to hang out at a moment’s notice, Wise Owl Outfitters’ portable hammock lets them post up at camp (or at airports, if necessary). It’s made of 210D ripstop nylon and takes about a minute to set up; a kids version is also available.

If they frequently explore far-flung locales that are outside of cell coverage areas, gift them the peace of mind of reliable communication with Spot X’s satellite messaging and GPS tracking device with Bluetooth. Users can connect to their smartphone to access their contacts and easily send messages and S.O.S. alerts, track their routes, quickly check in with family or friends and more. A Spot X contract plan is required; prices range from $12 to $40 per month depending on coverage needs.

For the wanderlust who prefers experiences over tchotchkes, contribute to their next vacation fund with an Airbnb gift card. Available in custom amounts, this is one of the best travel gifts for frequent adventurers looking for their next getaway, as Airbnb has thousands of hosts located across the globe.

For the cool creative that’s always in transit, treat them to this essential travel set from Bellroy, which uses sustainably sourced leather and recycled materials for all of its products. The wide-opening toiletry kit plus is ideal for stowing away (and easily accessing) skin care, grooming products, medications and more, while the tech kit zips open flat for easy access to cables and other accessories.

Whether they’re a backpacker, a preparedness enthusiast or someone who just prefers peace of mind when they’re sipping water in the wild, your loved one will appreciate this filtering 24-ounce water bottle from Grayl. The purifier cartridge removes viruses, bacteria and protozoa and filters out particulates (such as dirt and microplastics), chemicals and heavy metals from a total of 65 gallons of water before it needs to be replaced.

Scarlett Johansson Is Responsible For Bobby Flay’s Meet-Cute (And I Did Not See This Coming)

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Justin Baldoni Cuts More Ties After Leaving LA Following Blake Lively Lawsuits

Insiders Claimed JLo Was Getting Too Serious About Brett Goldstein Too Quickly, But Not So Fast

Thank Goodness A Nobody Wants This Star And I Have The Same Weird Emotions About Morgan And Sasha

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Bobby Flay definitely hasn't left The Food Network, as he’s got plenty of competition shows to host amid the 2026 TV schedule. But, outside of cooking up a storm, he has also cooked up some sweet love with American chef and restaurateur Brooke Williamson. However, he didn’t do it alone. Flay recently credited actress Scarlett Johansson with his meet-cute romance, and I definitely wasn't expecting to get these details.

While Flay and Williamson interviewed with People, he spoke about hanging out with a friend who helped set him up with his romantic partner. Without name-dropping, he gave some hints like this friend being a huge movie star and being married to SNL’s Colin Jost. Of course, that latter detail was a dead giveaway regarding Johansson!

Flay apparently first met Williamson when they were both judges of Supermarket Stakeout back in 2020. However, he didn’t get together with the Top Chef winner until season 6 of BBQ Brawl five years later. Here’s his true tale of why it took him so long to ask Williamson out and how Johansson helped pick up the pace:

And I was just telling [Johansson] this quandary I was having that I was, like, totally falling for somebody that I work with. And I’m just frustrated because I’m not gonna go there. And I just don’t know what to do. And she told me I was out of my mind. That’s how I sort of came to terms with it. And literally on the very last day of that season of BBQ Brawl, I went up to [Williamson] and I was like, ‘I need to talk to you about something.’ And she’s like, ‘What?’ And I was like, ‘If you’re still interested…’ It had gone on for a while. ‘If you’re still interested, I’m ready to make a commitment to you.’ And that was sort of the beginning of the beginning. There was nothing but support and thrilled that we had decided to make it an actual thing.

Aww, that is too cute for words! It’s adorable that, for a man who knows how to put two and two together when cooking up a delicious dish, he had trouble putting himself together with his beautiful co-worker. I also can't help but love Johansson’s honesty in telling her friend to make a move. The actress may have only played Black Widow in the Marvel movies, but she was a real hero for Flay. And now, Flay and Williamson have been together for over a year.

Before Brooke Williamson, Flay was married three times, so he's certainly had his share of experience with relationships. With that in mind, Flay went on to explain to People what he wanted in a new romance and how that all aligned with Williamson:

I mean, to me, the most important thing is I want a life partner that I’m committed to. I don’t need all of the documentation. But at the same time, I need to make sure that Brooke feels like she’s committed to. We were in London and we were sitting on a bench in The Regent’s Park, and I was like, ‘I got you a gift.’ And I said, ‘But before I give you the gift, like, you’re cool with not getting married, right? Like, we’re on the same page?’ Because we had obviously talked about that. And she’s like, ‘Yeah, yeah, absolutely.’ I was like, ‘Okay.’

So Flay surprised his partner with a huge diamond ring that cemented his commitment to her without a wedding of sorts. To some, it may seem confusing to give the woman you love a ring without the promise of marriage. However, I find it pretty cute, as it’s like a promise ring that still pledges loyalty. And, of course, at the end of the day, it's Flay and Williamson's business as to what they want their relationship to be.

On that note, Bobby Flay and Brooke Williamson seem to be quite happy, and I love to see it. Also, kudos to Scarlett Johansson for intervening a playing a bit of cupid. Fans can watch Flay and Williamson’s Food Network shows using a Hulu subscription or an HBO Max subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

5 Of The Biggest Gas Discounts Available Through Reward Programs Right Now

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Since Trump's war with Iran kicked off in February of this year, gas prices have been on everyone's mind. Who could've predicted we'd see gas prices skyrocket as the result of such a war? Well, we did. Worry not, though, dear reader. Gas prices are sure to come crashing down any moment, especially now that three tankers have hit mines in the Strait of Hormuz. Maybe after such a flawless victory, we should start calling it the Strait of America.

In the face of gas averaging around $4.20 per gallon nationwide (as of this writing), and with no signs those numbers will drop significantly anytime soon, many of our readers have started budgeting to account for high fuel prices. Meanwhile, others are scouring their cities to find the best possible price. Right now, memberships and rewards programs are hands down the best ways to get a substantial discount at the fuel pump, though some good deals are unfortunately drying up, forcing buyers to pivot to new methods to secure the best price in town.

The most notable loss in recent weeks has been the death of the Kroger gas discount. Fortunately, Costco, Love's, Walmart, and other gas and grocery chains still have worthwhile incentives that can save you a ton at the gas station. If you're filling up a big tank or driving a lot, such deals stand to save you a lot of money for the foreseeable future. Stack these savings with a credit card with good rewards for gas purchases, and you might even be able to convince yourself it's 2024 again and gas prices are hovering just above $3 per gallon.

The first rewards program gas discount on the list is only temporary, but it's a big one. Regular Save Mart shoppers are going to want to strike while the iron is hot because this West Coast grocer is offering up to $1 off per gallon at participating Shell locations, but only through the end of 2026. Signing up to be a Save Mart Rewards member is free, and a dollar spent in store equals one point. You can turn 100 points into 10 cents off per gallon, and this benefit stacks up to 1,000 points for a total of $1 per gallon off. You can use these points at the pump by providing your Save Mart-associated phone number at a participating station.

If you live in Save Mart's service area, you're probably staring down gas prices of around $5.50 per gallon, so this gas deal represents up to 18% off at the pump. Not bad at all, though of course it requires spending about $1,000 on qualifying groceries to unlock the full discount. This deal was initially scheduled to end in September of 2026, and was extended, so there's a chance you'll be able to find it next year, too, but don't count on the supermarket's biggest fuel deal ever lasting that much longer.

There's some fine print you'll want to know about, too. First, you're limited to 20 gallons per fill-up, for a maximum savings of $20. Second, Save Mart points don't last forever. They expire around three months after they're earned, so you're heavily incentivized to use them regularly rather than accumulating points and waiting for the best deals to drop each year.

Perpetual fan-favorite wholesaler Costco promises deals of all types all the time. Those $1.50 hot dogs are famous for a reason, and they really are a helluva deal, but Costco fans swear by their gas prices. Full disclosure: I'm one of those people. Their fuel deals aren't part of a rewards program in the traditional sense. There's no spending to accumulate points and then spending points for further deals. Instead, access to Costco gas stations is locked behind a $65 per year membership that includes online and in-person wholesale shopping, membership for two people, and a long list of benefits from prescriptions and eye exams to car maintenance and insurance offerings. A Costco membership also gets you discounts on Volkswagen SUVs, if you're in the market for a new ride.

The elephant in the room with getting your gas from Costco is the frankly unbelievable lines you might see there. I actually refused to go for a long time, as the lines looked like too much of a headache to be worth the savings. However, Costco pushes fuel to a high volume of drivers surprisingly efficiently, thanks to their numerous pumps and clear signage that indicate when a new pump has opened up and who gets to use it. I've rarely had to wait more than a couple of minutes, and for 10 to 20 cents of savings per gallon, the wait is well worth it.

If you're trying to eke out as much value as possible, you can also sign up for the brand's credit card for 5% back on gas bought at Costco. Those who refill weekly can recover their membership fee with ease.

Love's Travel Stops have a lovable history, as well as a respectable business model, and of all the things they do right, their gas reward program might just be the cherry on top. One aspect of the program that sets it apart from much of the competition is that the Love's Rewards program takes a two-tiered approach, offering different incentives for "casual" drivers and those who take to the road as a profession.

For non-pros, this rewards program offers one point per gallon of fuel purchases, two points per dollar spent in the store, and an automatic 10 cents off every gallon of gasoline. Unlike the Save Mart program, points don't expire as long as your account remains in good standing and stays active. Additionally, members get up to 25 cents off auto diesel purchases. The Love's app also gets you access to basic Wi-Fi at travel stops as well as mobile deals and the ability to order ahead at their connected restaurants.

Professional drivers get assigned a membership tier based on how much fuel they've purchased in any given month. It starts at Base for up to 499 gallons purchased, Gold is unlocked at 500 gallons, and the Platinum and Diamond tiers start at 1,000 and 1,500 gallons, respectively. Members get benefits like free showers and drink refills depending on their tier and earn scaling points for gallons fueled, dollars spent in-store, and dollars spent on truck care. These incentives make OTR trucking just a little bit easier and a little bit more comfortable, which can go a long way to ensuring drivers stay safe and happy on the road.

The Albertsons forU program works like most other grocery store rewards programs, but it has one of the most impressive fuel rewards deals out there. Like other such programs, it unlocks personalized deals, digital coupons, and the occasional free item, and it can also recommend meal plans that include recipes you can add the entirety of to your cart with the click of a button. You get one point per dollar spent on groceries, though these points expire at the end of the month after they're acquired, which is a faster turnaround than even the Save Mart program. If you're the type of person who likes to give gift cards for the holidays, you can earn points faster, though, with a rate of two points per dollar. There are also periodic multiplier events to help you rack up points.

The gas perks apply to gas stations across the country, and the membership generally saves you 10 cents per gallon at Walmart fueling stations, as well as CITGO, Mobil, Murphy, and Exxon stations. For those with Sam's Club memberships, though, the discount doesn't stack. Instead, Walmart+ simply gets you access to Sam's Club stations at Sam's Club member prices. If you find yourself in Alabama, you may also notice the discount isn't consistent, landing somewhere between five and 10 cents depending on the location.

Getting the gas perk requires keeping the Walmart app on your phone, and you'll either need to scan a QR code at the pump or use a discount ID provided by the app. It's not as convenient as other options, but it's still a solid discount. This option doesn't earn your membership money back quite as fast as the Costco offer does, though, so it's probably reserved for people who can take as much advantage of the other perks as possible.

United Airlines Pilots Defy ATC Orders To Fly Through “The Worst Part” Of A Severe Thunderstorm

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

United Airlines pilots scheduled to operate a nonstop flight from San Antonio International Airport (SAT) to Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) refused to accept the air traffic control's proposed routing to the US capital, after they identified that the route would take them directly through a severe thunderstorm.

A recording shared by Aviation MP4 shows the moment the pilot questioned the clearance from ATC, which, instead of taking the service around the worst part of the weather, put the jet on a trajectory to travel through the worst part of heavy rain and thunderstorms. The pilot in command ultimately has the final say as to whether the flight can operate, as they hold the operational responsibility and safety for the flight. For this event, the pilots' concern was also backed up by United dispatch.

United was scheduled to operate flight 471 from San Antonio to Washington Dulles on Friday, November 6. As the plane had pushed back from the gate and was taxiing to the runway, the recording caught the moment that the crew queried the proposed route. Initially, air traffic control had rerouted the Boeing 737-700 to depart south and travel around the severe weather.

During the flight's taxi, the moments shared had the flight crew challenging the decision, with the pilot noting that he was going to speak with United dispatch, and that the crew was refusing to fly through what would have been up to four hours of challenging weather conditions for the crew, and unfavorable comfort for the passengers on board.

Simple Flying reached out to United for comment, and the airline confirmed the flight landed safely. It is believed that this incident occurred on Friday, November 6, but has not been confirmed by Simple Flying

Pilots onboard the flight quickly noted that they were unable to fly the proposed route, instead requesting that the initial routing, which would have taken the Washington-bound flight south of the severe weather. The air traffic control reevaluated the plan, while the United pilots spoke to airline dispatch, which backed them up, noting that they should not be flying through the adverse weather.

In the end, the crew was permitted to fly an alternative route, highlighting the authority that pilots have to reject an unsafe clearance request and opt for an alternative. While air traffic control provides the instructions and proposed flight paths, pilots in command have the final responsibility for the aircraft's safety, and all of those onboard, including passengers and crew.

United flight 471 is a daily service from San Antonio to Washington Dulles that has a scheduled departure time of 7:00 AM, with an expected arrival time into Washington of 11:13 AM. The usual flight duration is two hours and 45 minutes. For the proposed route through the thunderstorm would have added almost an hour of extra flying time according to the pilot's commentary in the video shared above.

The lasting impacts of the unthinkable on ATC.

The Pilot-In-Command (PIC) holds the final authority over the operation and safety of an aircraft. They are able to challenge or disagree with air traffic control or airline dispatch if they believe that the situation could be unsafe. Pilots have specific rights and protocols that they are allowed to use.

This can include rejecting clearance and requesting an alternative. They can also declare an emergency and deviate from any air traffic control clearance without prior permission if safety is compromised.

From San Antonio, United and United Express maintain regular scheduled flights to Chicago O'Hare International Airport (ORD), Denver (DEN), Houston George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH), Newark (EWR), San Francisco International Airport (SFO), and Washington Dulles.

Steam Bails Out Dead by Daylight After Review Bombing Campaign

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Dead by Daylight's recent Steam rating is back to Mostly Positive, but it wasn't a sudden change of heart among players that got it there. Valve has excluded a wave of reviews posted during the fallout over Behaviour Interactive's dealings with streamer TheBurntPeanut.

Chunky joins the ever-growing list of horror icons removed from Dead by Daylight, leading to fans questioning how well Behaviour can manage licenses

Reviews submitted between November 1 and November 6 have been marked as off-topic after the game was hit by a review-bombing campaign. As Dexerto reports, the decision reversed a slide to Mixed, leaving an asterisk beside the game's Steam score.

Visit the Dead by Daylight Steam page and you will see a notice explaining that reviews from an unusual period of activity have been excluded from the displayed score. At the time of writing, 74% of its recent reviews are positive, putting the game back in the Mostly Positive category.

That doesn't mean Valve has deleted thousands of angry reviews. The reviews are still there; they simply don't count toward the score most Steam users see.

This is part of a system Valve introduced in 2019 to deal with review bombs centered on matters it considers unrelated to the experience of playing a game. It identifies a period of unusually heavy review activity, investigates it, and can exclude that period from the score shown to most shoppers by default.

You can still change your Steam Review Score Settings to include those reviews if you want the unfiltered picture. The filter covers the entire date range, not just the negative posts about a particular controversy. Even an ordinary gameplay review written between November 1 and November 6 is left out of the default calculation.

The reviews are still there; they simply don't count toward the score most Steam users see.

The review bomb was also coming from two directions. Some players objected to Behaviour associating itself with TheBurntPeanut, while others were angered by the developer's decision to distance itself from him. Both groups ended up expressing their frustrations on Steam, even though neither grievance was primarily about how Dead by Daylight plays.

The trouble began on October 30, when TheBurntPeanut played Dead by Daylight on stream and a member of Behaviour's team granted him access to the game's paid DLC, including characters and perks. The studio later explained that staff had spotted an opportunity to introduce the game to a large new audience, but the gift went ahead without the usual internal approvals.

Players questioned Behaviour's willingness to support a creator whose previous content they felt conflicted with the inclusive environment the developer says it wants to maintain.

Things only got worse when TheBurntPeanut offered to return the DLC and an official Dead by Daylight account responded with, "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP." The reply made it appear that the team was dismissing concerns before it had even considered them.

Behaviour issued an initial apology on November 1. In a longer statement on November 3, it acknowledged that staff had moved too quickly, admitted that its response had escalated the dispute, and confirmed that the gifted content had been removed from the streamer's account.

The developer also promised to rewrite its creator guidelines, tighten the process for approving gifts, and provide further training to its staff. It said it was taking internal action over the incident, although it wouldn't disclose details about individual employees.

The apology hasn't pleased everyone. Some players who objected to the DLC gift still question Behaviour's judgment, while some of TheBurntPeanut's supporters believe the studio caved to pressure. The divide is especially noticeable in a game that, as director Mathieu Côté explained in a June interview with DualShockers, has several distinct communities.

Valve's filter has improved the rating prospective buyers see, but it hasn't done anything to settle the dispute behind the reviews.

GTA 6 PS5 Physical Boxes Have Hit The Wild, A Harbinger Of Our Disc-Less Future

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6Grand Theft Auto VIGTA 6Physical GamesPS5Rockstar Games

We’re closing in on Grand Theft Auto 6‘s launch, which includes store shelves stocked with empty boxes. Physical cases for the PlayStation 5 version of the game are already appearing in the wild ahead of stores being allowed to sell them on November 12.

Photos of a shrink-wrapped copy of GTA 6 for PS5 appeared on the GTA6unmoderrated subreddit earlier today and were apparently posted by a German retail worker. It looked exactly how you’d expect it to, with a white label around a translucent blue plastic case. A warning label on the front of the box art reminds consumers that there’s no actual disc inside the physical box, and that an internet connection is required in order to redeem the digital download using the code inside.

A look at the physical Grand Theft Auto VI box

Image: u/IseeIceTea pic.twitter.com/qT9OhuFVwa

— CharlieIntel (@charlieINTEL) November 7, 2026

The user has since deleted the thread and their Reddit account, potentially worried that the leak might be traced back to them given Take-Two’s track record and the high-alert status everyone will be on in the lead up to the game’s November 19 launch. Of course, not shipping the game on a physical disc is one way of cutting down on these exact kind of leaks that can arise from stores breaking street dates on warehouse workers potentially stealing early copies.

GTA 6 is available to being preloading on November 10, with physical copies being distributed starting November 12 for those who pre-ordered them. People will be able to take the box home a full week before the game is actually live on North American servers. Take-Two’s decision not to release GTA 6 on a disc gives us our first test run of the disc-less future Sony has promised starting in 2028.

Some publishers will likely also use codes in boxes to maintain a similar presence in brick-and-mortar stores. They’ll leave diehard fans with the option to go around collecting empty display boxes if they wish. I will certainly be one of them.

Major study argues we need immediate rules to protect young people from the harms of digital media

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Lancet experts say digital media harms to young people come from app design and weak rules, not lack of willpower. They urge governments to act now.

When teenagers can’t stop scrolling on their mobile devices, the easiest explanation is to label them as having weak self-control.

A group of public health experts has a different answer. The feed was built to keep that thumb moving, and the responsibility belongs to the companies that built it and the governments that let them.

In a three-paper Series in The Lancet, the experts said the harm that digital media does to young people comes from business models, design choices and weak rules. They want governments to act now, before anyone has proof of cause and effect.

If they’re right, the question is no longer how many hours a child spends on a screen. It is what the apps on that screen were designed to do.

Professor Ilona Kickbusch of the Digital Transformations for Health Lab in Switzerland chaired the Series. Dr. Jay Shaw of the University of Toronto led its first paper.

The caveat arrives early. Heavy, addiction-like use is linked to poorer mental health, sleep and physical activity, Kickbusch said, but the evidence isn’t conclusive. A link isn’t proof of cause, and the authors rate the evidence low in certainty.

In an interview with Earth.com, she said the size of the effects is only half the question. “If you consider how many children are now using digital media, even small adverse effects might have significant public health consequences,” she said.

“That is why we are calling for a precautionary approach to policy rather than reacting only after harm has occurred.”

Open almost any social app and the feed never ends. Designers built that bottomless scroll deliberately, Shaw and his co-authors wrote, and it has encouraged amounts of screen time already known to be harmful.

The companies behind those features have lobbied to hold off regulation while knowing about the harms, the authors wrote.

“Most digital platforms are designed by companies to capture attention and collect data, not to protect health,” Shaw said. He added that infinite scroll, autoplay and personalized recommendation feeds are deliberate design choices that draw attention.

Scale turns a design detail into a public health question. By one estimate the authors cite, people who were online in 2025 spent an average of 141 minutes a day on social media apps, about two hours and 20 minutes.

In a 2025 survey of more than 10,000 adults in the United States, the average was more than 6 hours a day on phone apps, which is most of a school day.

Shaw’s paper compares the technology industry with tobacco and ultra-processed food. Both of those industries worked to weaken regulation while their products harmed health.

Governments have allowed digital companies to regulate themselves for decades, the Series reports, and commercial growth has come before health.

“Technology companies are borrowing a playbook well known from tobacco and fossil fuels: manufacture doubt, discredit critics, frame regulation as a threat to freedom,” said Professor Barbara Prainsack of the University of Vienna, a co-author on the Series.

Missing regulation doesn’t mean a technology goes ungoverned, she added. It means the companies set the rules instead of public policy.

Shaw and his co-authors called today’s approaches to governing that system deeply insufficient to protect public health.

They also describe adolescence and early adulthood as periods when the brain and social life are especially sensitive. The idealized selves people build online are linked to body shaming and poorer mental health, especially in young people.

Asked by Earth.com what surprised her most in the evidence, Kickbusch named the gaps. “What was most surprising about the evidence on the health impacts of children’s digital media use was what it doesn’t tell us,” she said.

Most studies measure individual behavior, such as screen time, she explained. Researchers have paid very little attention to the bigger structures, such as content created by algorithms and targeted advertising.

Young people also care about the benefits of going online and about AI, she said, and researchers have studied both of these areas less.

Access to data is another problem. “The most valuable data on how these platforms actually affect users are hidden within technology companies, limiting independent scrutiny and slowing scientific progress,” said Louise Holly, a Series author at DTH-Lab.

Shaw’s paper lists eight recommendations for researchers. One is to audit and publish the practices of technology companies that harm health, using insider accounts from former employees and investigative reporting.

Another is to keep industry money out of the research itself, a lesson the authors take from tobacco and pharmaceuticals.

Single measures such as age limits aren’t enough on their own, so the Series calls for action at the company, national and global levels at once.

Companies would have to test new products and features for harm before releasing them. They would also share data on how those features affect users’ health with vetted public-interest researchers.

A global treaty through the World Health Organization would set baseline standards, so weak rules in one country can’t undo progress elsewhere. A dedicated international body would monitor AI harms to children and young people.

Kickbusch told Earth.com that governments must work on several fronts in parallel: reduce access, regulate individual technologies and address the commercial models and algorithms behind social media. They also need to cooperate across borders, she said. “They must accept this is a major public health issue!”

Lawsuits are part of the picture too. Courts can compel companies to disclose internal documents in a way regulators often can’t, Kickbusch said. She sees litigation as one route to accountability among several, not a substitute for regulation.

The authors also worked with a youth advisory group of six adults aged 23 to 30, who talked with them about the lived experience the research can’t capture.

The steps governments and companies need to take will take time, Kickbusch said, because they are politically contested. Families don’t have to wait.

“Meanwhile, parents must help children mitigate risks by significantly reducing their use of digital media and the access to smartphones. They must explain to children how digital platforms and algorithms work,” she said.

Families who understand the design features and the commercial motives behind the platforms can protect themselves better, she explained, even when cutting back leads to difficult conversations at home.

“And parents themselves must change their social media use and be an example for their children,” she added.

No one has proved that the design features cause the harms linked to them, or measured how large those harms are, and the authors say so plainly. The links are modest in size and rest on evidence they rate as low in certainty.

The scope is also wider than the kids with phones. The same industries shape the health of people who don’t use the technology themselves, Shaw’s team wrote.

By the International Telecommunication Union‘s count, 2.2 billion people had no internet access in 2025. Africa was the least connected region.

Shaw said the field needs much more research into how specific design choices change the way people use digital media, instead of only measuring how long they spend online. That research depends on data the companies now keep to themselves.

Until that data is shared, the most important measurements of how these platforms affect adolescent health stay inside the companies that built them.

The full study was published in the journal The Lancet.

Nottingham Forest 3-1 Man City: Alarming early goal stat revealed as Enzo Maresca's side fall behind Arsenal and Mikel Arteta

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 07 Nov 2026 20:10

Manchester City have conceded in the opening 11 minutes in each of their last three Premier League games.

The Citizens were beaten 3-1 by Nottingham Forest on Saturday, with the loss leaving the club six points behind league-leaders Arsenal.

City's task was made difficult after conceding in the sixth minute, when Josko Gvardiol lost possession in his own half and allowed Jair Cunha to score.

Enzo Maresca's side have also lost each of their last three top-flight matches, suffering defeats against Forest, Aston Villa and Brighton & Hove Albion.

The Citizens also conceded early goals against Villa (sixth minute) and against Brighton (11th minute), and it will be difficult to challenge Arsenal unless they can stop going behind in the opening stages of matches.

Manchester City have conceded in the opening 11 minutes of three consecutive games in the Premier League: 6' v Aston Villa ❌ 11' v Brighton ❌ 6' v Nottingham Forest ❌ https://t.co/m5bL7cfO5G

When teams consistently concede in the opening stages of matches, it suggests that there could be a mentality issue at play.

After the Citizens won three of their first four games following confirmation that they had been found guilty by an independent panel in September for breaching the Premier League's financial rules on an industrial scale, Maresca was able to swat aside suggestions that his team would be impacted negatively.

City have since lost three times and won twice, though one of those victories was an EFL Cup triumph against Brighton on penalties.

Maresca's side have also conceded at least two goals in six of the nine games since the October international break ended, and there are clearly severe issues for the head coach to address.

The good news for City is that Arsenal face a difficult fixture schedule in the Premier League after the November international break, with the Gunners set to play Newcastle United, the Citizens, Brentford, Tottenham Hotspur, Bournemouth and Manchester United.

Maresca's side arguably boast an easy next six games in the league, with the club set to face Fulham, Arsenal, Leeds United, Brentford, Chelsea and Hull City.

It remains to be seen if City can rediscover form, and if they fail to stop conceding early, then they could soon find themselves in a battle for top four.

Sydney Sweeney Sure Knows How To Make An Ad Splash. There's Conflicting Evidence About The Impact

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

A Lot Of Elizabeth Holmes' Former Co-Workers Have Now Seen You Can See Everything: 'He Fell For It Too'

Haters Gonna Hate Hate Hate On Sydney Sweeney's Lingerie Brand, But There's A Twist

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There’s no question that when Sydney Sweeney releases an ad campaign, it gets people talking. Whether it's selling soap infused with her bathwater, starring in an ad for American Eagle that led to serious backlash, or becoming an equity partner in Novig and doing an ad where she wore nothing but footballs, she knows how to make a viral moment. However, while these instances burn big and bright for a moment, do they actually turn into long-term sales? Let's talk about it.

When Sweeney’s first campaign with American Eagle launched, it was met with an intense amount of discourse. While it faced serious backlash, as some thought the campaign was promoting eugenics based on how it used the word “jeans” like “genes,” it did bring in 790,000 new customers in six weeks, and in Q3 of 2025, sales grew 1% according to Adweek. It also reportedly added about $400 million in market value and upped web traffic by 100%.

Then, after both Sweeney and Travis Kelce’s AE campaigns launched, there was a growth in sales around the holiday, as American Eagle Outfitters reported “record holiday-quarter sales of $1.8 billion.”

While Sweeney’s first campaign with American Eagle was a very big deal and did make an impact, the second did not lead to the same kind of gains. In this new report, it’s stated that in Q1 of 2026, AE’s sales fell 2% (even though total comps rose to 8% due to a 25% jump from the brand Aeire). In reference to that, Jay Schottenstein, the CEO of AEO, said that the overall results were mixed.

Michael Gunther, Consumer Edge’s SVP of Research and Market Intelligence, explained that when it comes to American Eagle’s second campaign with Sweeney, there’s “little evidence of a sustained improvement comparable to the original campaign’s holiday performance.”

Overall, it would seem that Sweeney’s campaigns do generate sales. However, once the shock of her ads wears off, sales dwindle. For example, when her Dr. Squatch ad about her bathwater came out, it “drove a 20% week-over-week jump in revenue,” according to the brand’s then-VP of Global Marketing, John Ludeke. However, in the months after that, Consumer Edge’s card data showed that Dr. Squatch’s slice of this market went down by 25 basis points.

It was also reported that while the brand sees double-digit growth in its future, it has seen a slowdown in bar soap sales. Dr. Squatch’s CEO, Josh Friedman, did make it clear, however, that Sweeney’s ad campaign was also successful because it ultimately helped the brand launch a body wash line.

As for the actress’ latest ad campaign with Novig, which did cause backlash, it’s had some notable early wins. Novig has raised funding at a $2 billion valuation, which is up from the $500 million it was at earlier this year. Along with that, first-time despitores went up over 281% in the weeks after Sweeney’s ad premiered. So, things are looking up for this partnership; the question is, can it be sustained?

The data seems to say her work provides sales in the short term. The long term is a bit of a different situation. However, if there’s one thing that’s certain, it’s that getting Sydney Sweeney in an ad is sure to generate a big conversation.

Star Wars Zero Company studio Bit Reactor lays off staff, a month after bringing furloughed developers back

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

"While it stings, I'm really proud of what we built together."

Star Wars Zero Company developer Bit Reactor has laid off an unspecified number of staff, a month after bringing some of its furloughed team back to work.

Several developers announced on LinkedIn on Friday, 6th November, that they had been let go, as first reported by Bespin Bulletin. Bit Reactor hasn't said how many people are affected.

Among them is senior environment artist Ryan Lopez, who spent three and a half years working on Zero Company, from pre-production to launch, and was laid off after a three-month furlough. UI engineer Tyler Eichten, who worked on much of the on-screen UI for the game's tactical missions, and Ryan Mosher from the QA team have also confirmed they were let go.

"While it stings, I'm really proud of what we built together," Lopez wrote. "It was a privilege to ship a game with you."

Bit Reactor furloughed most of its staff in the weeks before Zero Company launched on 27th August, with one source putting the figure at roughly 80 percent. The studio appeared to have run out of money just short of release.

The game did well regardless, and is thought to have sold more than a million copies within a month. Then, in a message shared on Bluesky on 7th October, CEO Greg Foertsch said the studio had "started the process of returning more of our team back to work". Asking staff to step away so close to launch was "an incredibly difficult decision", he said, "but one that was necessary to help us eventually bring as many people back as possible".

It's thought more than half the studio returned, though not everyone went back on full pay. It's not clear whether any of those who returned were among the people laid off this week.

In Eurogamer's Star Wars Zero Company review, we awarded the game four out of five stars, saying, "an already winning genre is matched with the perfect setting in this earnest, heartfelt, smart and slightly ramshackle turn-based tactics gem".

“It looks like nothing serious, but we will see tonight and tomorrow.

“I hope he will be able to go with the national team because he deserves this opportunity. It's amazing for him, amazing for the club, amazing for us, and we are so pleased and so happy for him.”

The injury blow comes at a tricky time for King, who was handed a surprise call-up to the England senior squad by Thomas Tuchel for the upcoming Nations League fixtures.

The Three Lions boss emphasised that the 19-year-old’s versatility across the frontline was a key part in his plan to select him ahead of the likes of Chelsea star Cole Palmer, who was a shock omission from Friday’s squad announcement.

"We decided to nominate 22 outfield players for flexibility in training and he is the extra player in training, if you will. It is the attitude and the personality,” the German said.

"In pre-season he helped us in training as a full back but we will not do that again," Tuchel noted. "He is an exciting player, a versatile player.

"We have enough number 10s and we stick to the guys who were in the previous camp because we were happy with the two and a half weeks we had together.

We have Josh as a new player in there, but I want to see him on both wings. He is kind of an extra player and he can play all four positions."

You Officially Missed Your Chance To Buy A New Toyota Supra Or BMW Z4

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

We all knew this day was coming, but none of us wanted to face it; the MkV Toyota Supra and G29 BMW Z4 are officially off the new car market. Our friends at CarsDirect analyzed national dealer inventory data on Tuesday and found that there are no more new Supras or Z4s on dealer lots across the United States. Both models were built at the Magna Steyr contract manufacturing facility in Austria, but their production run concluded in spring of this year. At least both manufacturers commemorated the end of production with Final Editions, but we're still sad to see both nameplates join the great new car lot in the sky. For now, at least.

Toyota Motor North America's senior vice president of product planning and strategy Cooper Ericksen told MotorTrend that a Supra successor is coming, but that there will be another hiatus in the iconic sports car's legacy. Ericksen said, "it is our goal to have a gap that is significantly less [than the last one]." That last gap between the fourth-generation Supra and the fifth-generation car lasted an excruciating 20 years, so hopefully the sixth-generation Supra comes much sooner than that. Sadly BMW has not publicly announced plans for a fourth-generation Z4 as of now.

The fourth-generation of BMW's Z4 hit the streets of a very different world eight years ago in 2019, and offered the classic long-nose, short-deck sports car design solely in ragtop form. The GR Supra debuted a year later in 2020, and caused quite a buzz among car enthusiasts for its Bavarian lineage, but quickly proved it was still true to the Supra formula as a powerful two-seat driver's car. BMW sold just 2,113 Z4s in 2025, and Toyota managed to shift slightly more Supras at 2,953, but neither figure is particularly impressive or cost effective, especially considering that pricey platform updates would be necessary to conform to changing regulations.

The automotive industry has been a bit unsteady as of late, affecting even industry stalwarts like BMW and Toyota. BMW slashed its outlook for 2026 in June, and typical sales leader Toyota just posted its third-straight month of sales declines despite simultaneously raising its annual earnings forecast.

Fortunately BMW isn't leaving driving enthusiasts out to dry, with the launch of its Neue Klasse vehicles including the highly anticipated iX3 electric crossover and a new iteration of the always titillating 3 Series sedan. Toyota isn't abandoning driving enthusiasts either, with a new Celica in the works. In the meantime, BMW has an array of performance cars including the excellent M2, M3, and M4, and Toyota has the hilariously fun GR Corolla and GR86, but we'll still miss the Z4 and Supra.

The Business Platinum Card® from American Express

As High As 175,000 points. Find Out Your Offer.

You don't have to be a Marriott Bonvoy loyalist to find ongoing value in the (see rates and fees).

The Marriott Bonvoy Boundless stands out in the Marriott card suite thanks to its solid lineup of perks, including an annual free night award (certain hotels have resort fees) and elite night credits, for a relatively low annual fee.

Whether you are a frequent Marriott guest or want a few extra perks the next time you stay with the brand, the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless makes sense for a wide range of travelers. Card rating*: ⭐⭐⭐⭐

*Card rating is based on the opinion of TPG's editors and is not influenced by the card issuer.

The is a cobranded card between Marriott Bonvoy and Chase. The card earns Marriott Bonvoy points, which can be used to book free nights at more than 10,000 properties worldwide.

The best way to redeem Marriott points is for free nights at Marriott hotels and resorts. However, you can also transfer your points to airline partners.

Marriott Bonvoy Boundless provides a standout suite of perks, including an annual free night award and automatic Silver Elite status, for a $95 annual fee.

I find it especially impressive that a mid-tier hotel card with a modest annual fee offers such lucrative perks, especially the annual free night award. Certain hotels have resort fees.

Here's a breakdown of the perks included with the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless:

You don't have to go all in on Marriott to get a lot out of having this $95-annual-fee card in your wallet, and that's what makes the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless worth considering.

Related: Marriott Bonvoy elite status 'soft landing' policy: How to requalify for status

The packs a punch for its reasonable annual fee. Here's what cardholders can expect.

Given TPG's November 2026 valuation of Marriott points at 0.75 cents apiece, the 35,000-point certificate is worth $263. This is nearly three times the card's $95 annual fee and is a fantastic benefit, especially since you don't have to spend on the card to earn the certificate.

While the Silver Elite status on the card is the first of Marriott's elite status tiers, it does offer a few useful benefits, such as a 10% points bonus on paid stays and priority late checkout.

If you put at least $35,000 of spending on the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless in a calendar year, you'll earn Gold Elite status. This comes with more perks, such as a 25% points boost and enhanced room upgrade (when available).

For most cardholders, it’s hard to justify putting $35,000 in purchases on the card solely to earn Gold Elite status.

If Gold Elite status is your goal, you may be better off combining the card's 15 complimentary elite night credits with 10 paid nights; this totals 25 elite nights needed for Gold Elite status.

If you're eligible for a business card, you can also pair the 15 elite night credits given by the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless with 15 elite night credits provided by the . These 30 total elite night credits leave you with just 20 nights to earn Platinum Elite.

You can also reach a higher elite status by earning one elite night credit for every $5,000 spent on purchases with the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless.

Related: How to choose a no-foreign-transaction-fee credit card

Given TPG's relatively low valuation of Marriott points, I only recommend paying for Marriott stays with the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless. There are other cards on the market that earn more valuable rewards on dining, gas and groceries.

The best way to redeem your Marriott points is to book free nights at one of the brand's hotels.

Since Marriott uses dynamic award pricing (meaning the number of points needed fluctuates based on demand and availability), you may have to take extra time and effort to strategize for maximum value.

Marriott has an award calendar search function that can help narrow down the best deal for your points. For example, a night at the Moxy NYC Lower East Side in January 2027 could cost as few as 57,000 points per night or as many as 81,000 points per night, depending on when you book.

While we value Marriott points at 0.75 cents apiece, there are ways to get better value.

For instance, TPG lead writer for loyalty programs Katie Genter previously redeemed 112,000 points for a five-night stay at the Courtyard by Marriott Toronto Vaughan for a music festival. Compared against a cash rate of $1,464, she got an impressive 1.31 cents per point from this redemption.

Also, keep in mind that Marriott offers a fifth-night-free benefit. When you book at least five consecutive nights on points, you'll get the night that's worth the lowest number of points for free. This can help you stretch your points for longer trips.

Marriott points are also transferable to nearly 40 airline partners, most at a 3:1 ratio. These include some hard-to-earn currencies, such as Air China PhoenixMiles and Frontier Airlines.

However, unless you have a specific airline redemption in mind and need to top off your balance, transferring is unlikely to be the best use of your points.

The won't be the perfect fit for everyone. Here are some shortfalls to consider:

The is a premium sibling to the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless. It charges a much higher $650 annual fee (see rates and fees) but provides more benefits for Marriott loyalists.

These perks include higher Platinum Elite status, 25 elite night credits each calendar year toward status and an annual free night award valued at 85,000 points. (Certain hotels have resort fees.)

Additionally, they can receive up to $100 in statement credits (up to $50 biannually) after spending $500 on eligible airline purchases (through June 30, 2027).

*Nights are valued at up to 50,000 points each; certain hotels have resort fees.

If you max out the free night awards and earn the statement credits for airline purchases, this bonus is worth up to $1,225 per TPG's valuations.

The current offer delivers decent value for new cardholders. That said, we've seen stronger welcome offers on the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless in the past.

We generally recommend applying when the welcome offer includes at least 150,000 bonus points or at least four free night awards (each valued at up to 50,000 points).

I find the to be an excellent choice for a wide range of travelers, from Marriott loyalists who don't want to pay a high annual fee to those who occasionally stay at the brand's hotels and resorts.

This is because you get solid ongoing value from the annual free night award without paying a high annual fee.

Of course, if you're a fierce Marriott loyalist chasing status, a more premium card may be a better choice. And, if you want an option that earns higher-value rewards at a better rate on everyday purchases, you may want to look at a general travel card that earns transferable rewards.

Overall, however, the Marriott Bonvoy Boundless certainly provides enough ongoing value in exchange for its modest $95 annual fee, making it worth keeping year after year.

There are very few reasons for Unai Emery to leave Aston Villa for Manchester United | Jonathan Wilson

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Spaniard may feel some of Old Trafford’s allure when he goes there on Sunday but he is right in wanting to stay at the club with brighter prospects

On Sunday afternoon, shortly before 4.30, Unai Emery will leave the tunnel at Old Trafford and walk along the touchline towards the bench. Will he glance around the grand old stadium – faded and leaking though it may be in parts – and think that he could have a part in restoring the club to former glories? Will he look at the home bench and imagine himself sitting there – or perhaps more plausibly, scuttling up and down in front of it – as the first Manchester United manager since Sir Alex Ferguson to land a league title?

Perhaps not. Emery is, after all, in “advanced talks” to extend a contract at Villa that already runs to 2029. However frustrating he may find the habitual summer churn, he is good at managing it. He feels the right fit for Villa, and that is arguably the most important factor in determining success. Yet all elite managers share a robust self-belief and there is a paradoxical sense that the worse the situation gets at United, the more appealing the job becomes.

Imagine being the manager who does sort it out. Imagine following in that brief lineage of United title winners: Ernest Mangnall, Matt Busby, Alex Ferguson … me? United remain the eighth wealthiest club by revenue in the world; imagine the resources that would be available if they were actually any good.

That is an allure that very few clubs in the world can match, no matter how good they were in the 1890s, and no matter how much the Villa owner, Nassef Sawiris, may dislike the fact. This is a point that cannot be made too strongly. Villa’s threat of legal action against Jamie Carragher was an outrageous attack on free speech. There is no way Carragher’s comment about the food chain of football could be construed as defamatory and, while it’s true he might have made the comment in less provocative terms, swearing is not a crime. This was a classic case of a legal threat being used to quell criticism and those fans – and, worse, journalists – who cheered it on should be thoroughly ashamed. The partisan blindness and persecution complex shared by fans of so many clubs is one of the most depressing features of the modern game.

But it’s also true United’s allure has come to look distinctly tarnished. No matter how bullet-proof a manager’s self-confidence, it would represent a remarkable risk to make the leap from Villa to United at this juncture. In almost every facet, from infrastructure to personnel, the modern Villa are better run than the modern United. And from Emery’s point of view, it’s not just that Villa are well run, it’s that they’re well run in his way.

He has the coaching staff he wants, while the president of football operations, Roberto Olabe, and the director of football operations, Damian Vidagany, are people he trusts and has worked with before. Emery would not have the degree of autonomy he has at Villa at many other clubs. United’s win over Roma on Tuesday may have eased the most immediate pressure on Michael Carrick, but nobody surely thinks that one good half against a depleted Serie A side cures all ills. (The question, obviously, is slightly absurd: nobody surely thinks Carrick is really the problem at United; it’s just that executives tend to get rid of managers because it’s what you do when there’s been a slump in form; it’s how a director acknowledges that he’s recognised there is a problem, while hoping desperately to deflect attention from his own shortcomings).

It doesn’t help that there is a clear precedent from five years ago, when a popular former United player saw his team come from two down to beat a Serie A side managed by Gian Piero Gasperini; Ole Gunnar Solskjær was sacked a month later.

The United crisis may have eased, but it is far from over. While Sir Jim Ratcliffe shows off architects’ plans and begs for public money to build a new stadium, Villa get on with building the new Trinity Road Stand. While United’s squad sags under the weight of its many contradictions, a weird mismatch of ill-fitting parts, Villa’s begins to take shape despite making 11 signings in the summer – even if the 3-0 defeat by Barcelona in midweek suggests there is still work to be done. They probably wouldn’t ideally have made such radical changes and would have preferred to keep Morgan Rogers and Ezri Konsa but, equally, there was an admirable sense of realism at Villa – a recognition of their place in the food chain, you might say – that when Chelsea or Arsenal come calling and offering huge contracts, there’s not much to be done other than to drive the price as high as possible.

Youri Tielemans is another player Villa might have liked to keep, but once United matched his release clause, the deal was done. Although Tielemans probably appreciates his new contract, it’s far from clear it’s been a good move for him from a football point of view, not least because United’s is a midfield that exposes his lack of mobility. Carlos Baleba was signed from Brighton to address that lack of dynamism in the United midfield but there were concerns about his tendency to drift in and out of games even before he suffered a hamstring injury on Tuesday.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

It even feels as though Villa have been smarter about using United players than United have. Victor Lindelöf was brought in on a free transfer in summer 2025 and has excelled since and while Marcus Rashford and Jadon Sancho had less of an impact, Villa only signed them on loan. Rashford’s time, which featured four goals in 17 appearances in all competitions, was probably the more eye-catching, but Sancho made 39 appearances in his season at Villa.

In both cases, Villa got value out of a players whose futures were uncertain but without committing to having to take them on full-time.

It’s pretty hard to imagine at the moment, but at some point, United will start heading in the right direction. It may even be that Tuesday’s win prompts a surge of adrenaline that takes them into the top half of the table. For now, though, Villa look by far the brighter prospect, a club on the up, while United are in barely managed decline.
