The $20 Billion Airport Transformation Planned Around United's Washington Hub

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Brandon's passion for aviation started at a young age. He became involved in the flight simulator and VATSIM communities, which sparked his interest in aviation and eventually led him to earn his private pilot license. Outside of work, he continues to build flight hours, explore new airports, and stay involved in the aviation community.

The Metropolitan Washington Airports Authority (MWAA), United Airlines, and the US Department of Transportation announced a capital investment program of more than $20 billion for Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) on July 29, 2026. The program covers more than 5 million square feet (464,515 square meters) of new or renovated space across the next decade, and it roughly triples the $7 billion modernization outlined in MWAA's master plan of May 2025. Eero Saarinen's Main Terminal stays. Concourse C and D, which house the core of United's hub, are slated for replacement.

The first piece arrives within months. Concourse E opens later in 2026 with 14 new United gates, direct AeroTrain access, and new lounges including an expanded United Club and a United Polaris Lounge. Behind it sits an active design-build procurement worth $4.5 billion to $5 billion covering expansion of the Saarinen terminal, a post-security connector to Concourse A, and redevelopment of the A-B concourse. MWAA CEO Jack Potter said Dulles was the fastest-growing large airport in the country across 2024 and 2025, and the airport's aging facilities have been limiting how efficiently United can expand there.

The scope sits behind the headline figure. More than 5 million square feet (464,515 square meters) of new or renovated space is the program's central measure, covering new and modernized concourses, expanded passenger processing, a new baggage handling system, and additional retail and lounge space. MWAA, United and the US Department of Transportation described the work as creating an airport of the future for the National Capital Region, delivered across the next decade rather than as a single project with an end date.

The figure represents a substantial escalation rather than a new plan. ENR reported that MWAA's May 2025 master plan outlined a modernization program of roughly $7 billion, and that the July announcement expands it into a redevelopment effort valued at more than $20 billion, tripling the investment. The underlying master plan has not been discarded. Projects within it remain active under MWAA's existing modernization program, and ENR noted that the expanded proposal was developed under that same plan rather than alongside it. The announcement described the three parties as working together to accelerate long-term plans to create an airport of the future for the National Capital Region, which positions the new money as compressing an existing timeline as much as adding scope to it.

One element is being preserved deliberately. The Airports Authority and United stated that new and modernized facilities are designed to create a smoother, more efficient and elevated experience from curb to gate while preserving architect Eero Saarinen's iconic Main Terminal design. United CEO Scott Kirby framed the program around the airline's position at the airport: "Washington Dulles is the gateway that connects the nation's capital to the world, and this transformation builds on United's long-term investment in our hub to deliver the world-class airport experience our employees, customers and millions of travelers deserve." United operates its largest hub in the Washington region at Dulles.

The first visible result arrives before the end of 2026. The initial section of Concourse E opens later this year with 14 new United gates, direct access to the airport's AeroTrain system, and new passenger amenities including concessions and lounges. The Points Guy reported it as a half-billion-dollar terminal, with crews working beyond the airport's farthest-flung existing terminal.

The lounge program is substantial. The concourse will debut new and enhanced passenger lounges, including an expanded United Club offering and a new United Polaris Lounge. United has said the new gates will help the airline expand its international network, which is the function a Polaris Lounge serves, since the product is tied to the airline's long-haul business class cabin. The Points Guy reported United Airlines CEO Scott Kirby touring construction at the airport, and separately noted that MWAA and United celebrated future development plans at Dulles in December, which is the point at which the broader program began taking public shape ahead of the July announcement.

What Concourse E does not do is resolve the underlying problem. The Points Guy put it directly, noting that 14 new gates are hardly enough to sunset the aging terminal currently housing the core of United's Dulles hub operation, which it described as well past its prime. United has acknowledged that the current construction of Concourse E is just the start of multiple phases to come for its future development at the airport. MWAA CEO Jack Potter said the airport hopes to build a sizable extension of the terminal currently being built for United.

The next tranche of work is already out to tender. ENR reported that MWAA has an active design-build procurement known as Package A, valued at between $4.5 billion and $5 billion, with contractors competing for the contract. It was developed under MWAA's existing master plan rather than created by the July announcement, which means the procurement was underway before the program expanded past $20 billion. ENR described the redevelopment as serving as the framework for that procurement, so the larger program and the contract being tendered are structured around each other rather than running independently. At roughly a quarter of the total announced investment, Package A is the single largest identifiable piece of the program currently in the market.

The scope covers the areas passengers move through before reaching a gate. Package A includes expansion and transformation of the Eero Saarinen-designed main terminal, new terminal gates, a post-security connector to Concourse A, redevelopment of the existing A-B concourse, and replacement of its regional-gate section. ENR reported that HOK has been selected to design the Dulles expansion.

Two elements of the broader program address how passengers move around the airport rather than how many gates exist. The post-security connector to Concourse A creates a walkable route where passengers currently rely on other means, and United's announcement separately referenced a walkable path to a new, large US facility serving the airport. Dulles has historically depended on mobile lounges, the raised passenger transporters that drive between the terminal and outlying concourses, and The Points Guy photographed passengers waiting for them inside Concourse D. Each piece of walkable connection removes a dependency on that system.

It will soon be time for the mobile lounges to go away as the entire airport is modernized.

The core of United's Dulles operation is housed in Concourse C and D, and both are slated for replacement. ENR reported that the redevelopment would preserve Saarinen's landmark terminal while replacing Concourse C and D, expanding passenger-processing capacity, and serving as the framework for MWAA's Package A procurement.

The condition of those facilities is why the work is necessary. The Points Guy described the terminal currently housing the core of United's Dulles hub as well past its prime, and reported passengers waiting inside Concourse D for mobile lounges.

Supporting systems are included alongside the concourse work. A new baggage handling system will be installed, along with additional retail concessions and airport lounges. The sequencing is visible in the announced timeline. Concourse E opens first with 14 gates, and United has described it as the start of multiple phases to come. The Points Guy noted that under a new agreement with MWAA, United and its Dulles competitors committed to billions of dollars of investment at the airport over the coming years, extending well beyond the 14 gates opening in late 2026. That detail matters for how the program is funded, since it indicates the carriers operating at Dulles are contributing alongside the airport authority and the federal government rather than simply benefiting from public investment.

United stands to benefit from major work that will add 36 gates at Washington Dulles.

Growth is the stated reason. MWAA CEO Jack Potter said Dulles was "the fastest-growing airport in '24 and '25 of any large airport in the country." An airport adding passengers faster than any comparable facility in the country while operating concourses that have outlived their intended purpose faces a problem that compounds annually. Potter remarked in the context of explaining why the investment had expanded so sharply, and ENR reported it alongside the detail that the proposal builds on that growth rather than anticipating it. The distinction matters, because a program sized against demand already arriving carries a different risk than one sized against a forecast.

The constraint has been limiting United directly. AirGuide reported that while United has long operated a major hub at Dulles, the airport's aging facilities have limited the airline's ability to expand efficiently, and that the redevelopment is set to change that. United's own announcement stated that the 14 new gates at Concourse E will help the airline expand its international network, which identifies where the capacity is needed.

Federal involvement is what separates this from a standard airport capital program. ENR characterized the announcement as a federal selection that transformed MWAA's long-range airport program, expanding it into a megaproject, with the US Department of Transportation appearing alongside the airport authority and the airline in the announcement. Whether $20 billion of work proceeds over a decade on the schedule announced depends on funding, procurement, and construction sequencing that has not yet been published in detail.

Steven Gerrard Names ‘Freak’ Player Who Will Be Talked About Like Messi And Ronaldo

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Former Liverpool and England captain Steven Gerrard has named the one current player he believes will be talked about in the same breath as Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo in 10 years' time.

Gerrard, now 46, is rarely one to dish out praise lightly. Such were his high standards during his playing days, when he was hailed as a Roy of the Rovers figure after dragging his beloved Reds to a memorable Champions League triumph in 2005, he was widely regarded as one of the most demanding players of his generation.

And on the rare occasions he has tipped a younger talent to reach the very top, things haven't always gone to plan. Take Adam Lewis, for example, who Gerrard once described as one of the 'best he's ever seen' but now finds himself plying his trade in League Two.

However, given the reputation this latest player has already built for himself, it seems unlikely Gerrard will be left with egg on his face this time. After all, he's already knocking on the door of Ballon d'Or contention.

There is no shortage of young talents in world football right now and, just as everyone thought nobody could ever compare to Messi and Ronaldo as their reigns at the pinnacle of the sport come to an end, there's someone else that comes along to get the tails wagging.

Rio Ngumoha, Max Dowman, and Desire Doue all rank among the best young players in the world right now. But it is not exactly surprising to learn of Gerrard's love for Barcelona youngster Lamine Yamal. He said during TNT Sports' coverage of Barcelona's most recent Champions League match:

"We're talking about a megastar. He is a freak of a player, the numbers that he's getting at the moment - goals and assists - are beyond belief, scary really.

"Just to be in the stadium tonight to watch him up close and personal, I'm sure it'll be a pleasure. We talk about superstars in England who are 16, 17, 18 and have played handful of games. He's the same age and he's got numbers that are beyond belief, he's already in the conversation for Ballon d'Ors. He's still a teenager, which is a scary thing really."

He continued: "These players come around every so often - Messi, Ronaldo - and I know I'm not saying this too soon, he's going to be spoken about like them in a decade's time."

Barcelona's attacking players have seemed to go up a whole new level so far this season, with a remarkable 46 goals from just 11 La Liga games, and 14 goals in four Champions League matches.

While top scorers Raphinha and Yamal - with the latter having finished third behind Harry Kane and Kylian Mbappe at the 2026 Ballon d'Or ceremony - have earned the majority of the praise, England international Anthony Gordon has also caught the eye following his move from Newcastle United, with five assists and two goals in La Liga.

"Anthony's a learner, he's someone who wants to grow and evolve," Gerrard said. "He wants to become a world-class player himself. The potential's there, we've seen it for club and country now.

"He was fantastic in England, I think Newcastle under Eddie Howe did wonderful things for him. Now it's about him kicking on and going to the next level.

"I think the coaching at Barcelona - and Thomas Tuchel as well - has taken him to the next level. What a place for him to be. Playing for Barcelona at a fantastic age in his peak years, playing for England, and producing for both. He's living the dream."

IREN’s Bitcoin Mining Revenue Falls as AI Cloud Sales Nearly Triple

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: IREN reported $200 million in quarterly AI Cloud revenue, up from $71 million the previous quarter, while Bitcoin mining revenue fell from $67 million to $28 million. Its November 5 results underline how quickly some former mining-led businesses are shifting towards AI infrastructure — and how expensive that shift remains.

IREN’s latest quarterly results offer a particularly stark view of the transition taking place across publicly listed Bitcoin infrastructure companies. A November 5 filing with the SEC reports $200 million of AI Cloud revenue for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, compared with $71 million in the preceding quarter.

Bitcoin mining revenue, meanwhile, dropped to $28 million from $67 million. Total revenue rose to $228 million from $137 million, meaning the overall business expanded even as mining became a much smaller share of the total.

The company says AI Cloud revenue increased 182% sequentially and reported an 86% segment gross margin. That is a large change in a single quarter, supported by data-centre capacity coming online and customer contracts. But the term ‘gross margin’ is not a synonym for cash profit after financing, corporate expenses, depreciation and the capital needed to build the infrastructure.

IREN reported a net loss of $263 million for the September quarter, compared with a loss of $684 million in the preceding quarter. Adjusted EBITDA rose to $66 million from $19 million. The coexistence of strong revenue growth and a substantial accounting loss is precisely why neither metric should be read on its own.

The company said its revenue backlog increased to $20.3 billion, about two-thirds of it associated with investment-grade counterparties. A backlog is a contractual forward-looking figure subject to delivery schedules and contract terms; it is not cash already in the bank.

Management said a target of more than $4 billion in annual recurring revenue is fully contracted and funded, but its timetable moved from the end of 2026 to January 2027 following a customer-requested design change and supplier timing. It said completion could extend into February. The distinction between contracted and operating capacity matters especially when large AI computing clusters depend on specialist components and commissioning schedules.

IREN highlighted the Horizon 1 facility entering service for Microsoft and said it had signed additional contracts after quarter-end. Its filing also described planned remaining fiscal-year capital spending and the financing it expects to use. Investors should compare the timing of expenditure with when customer payments are due rather than assuming a huge order book removes funding risks.

The trend resembles the shift described in NewsBTC’s wider examination of Bitcoin miners and AI infrastructure. However, each operator has its own contracting arrangements, retained mining fleet, power infrastructure and financial obligations.

Not necessarily. IREN’s lower mining revenue tells us about one company’s business mix, not the aggregate computing power securing Bitcoin. Network difficulty, global hashrate and other miners’ decisions could move in different directions. NewsBTC’s reporting on hashrate competition is a useful counterpoint to the corporate story.

The next disclosures worth examining are realised AI revenue, actual data-centre commissioning, customer concentration, financing terms and remaining exposure to Bitcoin mining. IREN’s results show the transformation is already reflected in its income statement. Whether the economics produce durable shareholder returns depends on far more than the speed of this quarter’s revenue growth.

Feature: Going Platinum: For About Two Months, I Was a Devout Member of the Cult of the Lamb

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Hello. Been a minute since the last one, I know, but about 15,000 games have launched in the last few weeks, and Going Platinum sort of fell by the wayside.

But I'm determined to pick up where I left off, and I left off at Cult of the Lamb, a game I was totally enraptured by for a good couple of months.

It's a curious case for me, this one. One half of the game is right up my street (the roguelike action part) and the other half sort of isn't at all (the crafting, survival, management part).

For that reason I probably shouldn't have liked it nearly as much as I did, but the combination of all its elements together is pretty much perfect.

It does something I love to see in games, where every singular aspect of the design feeds into something else.

Going out on a run allows you to gather resources and recruit new members to your cult, then you return home and improve your settlement, which in turn makes you gradually more powerful.

Both parts of the game are quite simple in their execution, but you're never at a loss for things to do; there are new buildings to construct, a new boss to defeat, new followers to brainwash. It's an extremely compelling cycle that I really loved.

It helps that it has a great art style and cartoonish charm that takes the edge off of its dark humour. It's extremely well executed across the board, really — I'm not surprised it's Devolver Digital's biggest game ever.

As for the Trophy hunt, it's another pretty straightforward one. The platinum is surprisingly rare; at the time of writing, only 1.8 per cent of players have unlocked it on the PS5 version.

Looking through the list, it seems the biggest hurdles for people are just collecting things. There are Trophies for getting all the tarot cards, follower skins, and fleeces, and fewer people have unlocked those than have beaten all the bosses without taking damage.

In any case, it's a very fun game, and an enjoyable plat. It took me just under two months, and I really loved every moment.

Do you have the platinum Trophy for Cult of the Lamb? Tell us in the comments section below.

John Goodman Thinks He Shouldn't Have Starred In George Clooney's 2014 Critical Flop

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

George Clooney enjoys a heightened status in Hollywood. He is one of the most handsome and charismatic movie stars in the history of the medium, and he relishes the opportunity to make a total fool out of himself (especially if he's working for Joel and Ethan Coen). For a moment, his instincts were unerring. On the strength of "Confessions of a Dangerous Mind" and "Good Night, and Good Luck," those instincts seemed to extend to directing. His next two directorial efforts, "Leatherheads" and "The Ides of March," were solid, but they lacked the snap of his early work. Then he just up and lost it.

Clooney's last five movies — "The Monuments Men," "Suburbicon," "The Midnight Sky," "The Tender Bar" and "The Boys in the Boat" — are bizarrely inert. I revere Clooney as an actor (I wrote a book about him), and, via interviews with people who know him well, believe that he cares deeply about his art. But there's no passion in those aforementioned movies. They're smart, considered, compassionate works, but they've got a blasé point of view. Clooney admirably promotes a humanistic point of view, but there's no technical or intellectual rigor behind his efforts. It's like watching LeBron James make layups.

I hate writing this stuff because I know Clooney has it in him to make great films. But when I run into a series of comments from a serious actor like John Goodman about how he spent months working on "The Monuments Men" with Clooney, only to walk away wishing he hadn't made the movie, I wonder where it all went wrong. While Goodman admittedly had a good time making the film, he also confessed, "It was one of those movies ... I probably shouldn't have done."

During a recent SAG-AFTRA Foundation Q&A, Goodman dished on two films that, in his estimation, he shouldn't have done. One was John Landis' reviled "Saturday Night Live" movie sequel "Blues Brothers 2000," which at least gave the rhythm and blues aficionado the opportunity to work with music legends like Aretha Franklin, Wilson Pickett, and B.B. King. He didn't mention Landis' involvement in the deaths of Vic Morrow, Myca Dinh Le, and Renee Shin-Ye Lee on the set of "Twilight Zone: The Movie," but people were still making films with the director at the time.

The other was "The Monuments Men" with Clooney. According to Goodman, "George loves movies and he loves stories and he's such a great storyteller himself, and I think he's a great director. I had fun with him. 'Monuments Men.' We were over in Germany for six months. And England."

But the film about art experts being dropped into Europe during World War II to save the continent's cultural treasures was DOA critically and commercially. It's a handsomely mounted would-be epic, but the theme is clearly stated early on, which leaves Clooney nowhere to go narratively. Protecting art, reinforcing its importance in the middle of a war that threatens to end civilization, is a wildly worthwhile premise. Especially now. I just wish Clooney and co-screenwriter Grant Heslov hadn't taken a softball approach to this subject. We need a guy like Clooney hurling heat.

Exclusive: Liverpool ready to sign €46m sensation, he's coming to replace club legend

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Liverpool are making moves to replace a club legend. Julian Ward wants an exciting sensation.

Football never stays still. It's always evolving. New players come and go. Just like life, the ravages of time catch up with us all.

That's why there are different eras. It's why some teams become conquerors and then fall from grace and some even rise from the ashes again.

This perpetual and constant change is what gives football meaning. If there was too much success for one team and no pain, the success would not feel as great.

It is because of suffering that we can experience euphoria.

No club and no anthem summarises this better than Liverpool's You'll Never Walk Alone. Right now Liverpool are walking through a storm.

But at the end of it all, there is a golden sky.

Anfield Watch can exclusively reveal that one of the goalkeepers the Reds are ready to sign is Brighton's Bart Verbruggen who is valued at €46m by Football Transfers.

Though it is believed Brighton's asking price would be a lot higher.

Verbruggen has always been admired by the club's scouts and there is a strong belief at Liverpool he could become a world-class goalkeeper within the next few years.

A tiny plant’s unusual sex life depends on water flowing uphill

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Common liverworts carry water up their stalks without a pump, helping sperm reach elevated eggs and revealing how the plants evolved.

Water runs downhill, and that’s a problem for liverworts, small plants whose sperm can reach an egg only by swimming.

In the common liverwort, a flat green species, the female holds her eggs up on a short stalk, away from the damp ground.

A new study found that the outside of that stalk carries water upward, against gravity, to the eggs at the top.

The upward flow helps resolve a conflict between fertilization and spore dispersal.

Height makes fertilization harder. But height also helps the plant spread its spores, and the study traces how the stalk came to handle both jobs.

Bo Xu, a professor at the Institute of Botany of the Chinese Academy of Sciences, led the work, and Xiaotong Qi is the study’s first author.

The team studied Marchantia polymorpha, the best-studied liverwort in biology labs.

Liverworts, mosses and hornworts belong to a group called bryophytes. They make no flowers or seeds, and they don’t have true roots.

They also lack the complex network of water-carrying tissue that larger plants rely on.

In bryophytes, a sperm cell has to travel through water to reach an egg.

Once an egg is fertilized, it grows into an embryo. The embryo then becomes a structure that makes spores, and those spores spread out and grow into new plants.

Every bryophyte has to meet both needs at once. Fertilization depends on water, while spores spread better from a higher, drier spot.

How small plants managed that is part of the larger story of how plants colonized land.

On a female common liverwort, a stalk lifts the egg-holding organs above the ground.

Xu’s team found that the stalk and the head on top of it carry water upward along their outer surface.

The flow depends on several kinds of tiny structures, each placed in a precise position relative to the others.

The researchers used three-dimensional X-ray imaging to map how those structures fit together on the stalk.

Water moving up that surface helps sperm reach eggs held well above the wet ground.

Keeping those surface structures intact helped water-based fertilization succeed, the team found.

One protein, called MpLAS, controls how all of those surface structures develop. It belongs to a family of proteins called GRAS, which switch other genes on and off.

MpLAS works by guiding how cells divide as they grow.

A second GRAS protein, MpGRAS10, helps build the stalk itself. It boosts the activity of two genes, MpKAOL1 and MpKAOL2, which help the plant make compounds related to gibberellin.

In many plants, gibberellin is the hormone that makes stems grow longer.

So one protein helps raise the eggs, and another builds the surface that gets water up to them.

Xu’s team also compared the common liverwort with its relatives and worked backward through their family tree.

The female stalk appears to have evolved only once in this group of liverworts, and several later lineages lost it.

The stalk turned up before the surface structures that carry water.

The researchers read that order as two separate steps. The elevated stalk helped the spores produced after fertilization spread farther.

The surface network came later and dealt with that problem.

In their account, later traits may have arisen to make earlier ones work better, by fixing what those earlier traits couldn’t do.

They said competing demands like these can push a complex structure to evolve one piece at a time.

Comparisons among living liverworts suggest that the stalk evolved before its water-carrying surface structures.

That sequence supports the team’s proposed explanation, although it does not prove how those traits evolved.

The findings could also inspire engineers.

By carrying water upward without a pump, the liverwort offers a model for surfaces designed to move liquid on their own.

The full study was published in the journal Nature Plants.

FEATURE | The ‘other Nmecha brother’: Borussia Dortmund revel as Felix (26) enters his prime

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Felix Nmecha is developing a rather useful habit of scoring Bundesliga braces. Borussia Dortmund’s 2-1 victory over SV Elversberg on Saturday marked the second time this season that the German international has struck twice in a league fixture. His two outstanding long-range finishes enabled Niko Kovac’s side to overcome stubborn opposition and maintain their unbeaten start to the 2026/27 campaign.

The double followed another remarkable performance in September. Nmecha came off the bench to score twice in Dortmund’s 3-0 victory over promoted SC Paderborn, with his second goal coming courtesy of a spectacular overhead kick.

Such contributions naturally attract considerable attention. Dortmund sporting director Ole Book went so far as to describe Nmecha as one of the finest midfielders in world football following Saturday’s victory. The praise certainly would have sounded extraordinary to anyone following the younger Nmecha brother’s Bundesliga career when it began five years ago.

In the summer of 2021, all eyes were on Lukas Nmecha when two Manchester City academy graduates arrived in Wolfsburg. The older brother was a European champion, an established professional, and a promising candidate to become Germany’s next senior national-team striker.

Felix, by contrast, was simply hoping to earn his place in the first-team squad.

Few could have anticipated just how differently their careers would unfold.

The elder Nmecha brother had every reason to expect great things when he returned to Germany in July 2021. Lukas had just completed an outstanding loan campaign with Belgian giants Anderlecht. More importantly, the Manchester City product had helped Germany win the European U21 Championship, scoring the only goal in the final against Portugal.

The striker finished the tournament with four goals and claimed the competition’s Golden Boot. Wolfsburg duly invested a significant transfer fee to bring Lukas back on a permanent deal. Having already spent the 2019/20 campaign on loan in Lower Saxony, the Hamburg native now looked ready to establish himself in the Bundesliga. Germany’s senior national-team head coach Hansi Flick also recognized his potential.

Five days after Wolfsburg confirmed Lukas’s signing, the club announced the arrival of another Nmecha. Twenty-year-old Felix joined from Manchester City on a free transfer. Unlike his older brother, he possessed virtually no senior professional experience. His three first-team appearances in England had all come from the bench.

Wolfsburg’s initial announcement confirmed that Felix would join the first-team training camp. His eventual place in the senior squad, however, remained uncertain. The younger brother’s arrival was hardly greeted with the same excitement.

That wasn’t to suggest that Wolfsburg had acquired Felix merely to accommodate Lukas. The brothers subsequently clarified in an October 2021 interview with Kicker that the transfers had been negotiated independently. Lukas even suggested that Wolfsburg might have approached Felix before opening discussions about his own return.

Lukas was an established striker expected to contribute immediately. Felix was a promising youngster who still needed to prove himself.

The siblings had followed similar paths through their formative years. Both were born in Hamburg before their family relocated to Manchester. They progressed through Manchester City’s youth academy and represented England internationally at various youth levels.

Lukas eventually switched to Germany, establishing himself as an influential member of Stefan Kuntz’s successful U21 side. In October 2021, the older brother was already discussing his ambition to participate in the 2022 World Cup. His younger sibling had only recently made his Bundesliga debut, appearing as a substitute against Union Berlin.

Felix nevertheless harbored international ambitions of his own. The midfielder confirmed that discussions had taken place with the German Football Association regarding a potential change of allegiance. The following year, he made his debut for Germany’s U21s.

In September 2022, Felix scored against England’s U21s at Sheffield’s Bramall Lane and reiterated that representing Germany had been the correct decision. His ultimate ambition was to play alongside Lukas in the senior national team.

It seemed an attainable objective at the time. Lukas had already earned senior international recognition, and Felix was gradually beginning to establish himself at Wolfsburg. The younger brother’s initial progress nevertheless remained slow.

He made 16 Bundesliga appearances during his first Wolfsburg campaign, starting only once. His technical qualities were apparent, but he had yet to demonstrate how they might translate into sustained success at the highest level.

The arrival of Niko Kovac ahead of the 2022/23 season would prove instrumental in changing that.

Kovac’s first few months in Wolfsburg were hardly straightforward. The former Bayern coach needed time to establish a stable tactical structure, experiment with different midfield partnerships, and extract more consistent performances from a squad possessing considerable individual talent.

Felix was among those who eventually benefited from the adjustments. The first major signs of progress came in October 2022.

Kovac singled out the midfielder following Wolfsburg’s 2-2 draw against Bayer Leverkusen, identifying him as one of the team’s most impressive performers. One week later, Felix scored twice in a 4-0 victory over Bochum.

The October 29 brace delivered both his first and second Bundesliga goals. More importantly, it offered evidence that the previously overlooked youngster was beginning to find his feet. Kovac’s assessment after the match provided the explanation.

The Wolfsburg trainer noted that Felix had previously struggled to establish a rhythm because he had been used in too many different positions. His physical development was also progressing, allowing his technical abilities to become increasingly evident in midfield.

Kovac continued refining his role throughout the season. Nmecha operated both as a deeper central midfielder and in more advanced positions. His ability to combine with teammates, receive possession under pressure, and carry the ball into attacking areas gradually became essential to Wolfsburg’s developing game.

The younger brother’s growing influence coincided with a difficult period for Lukas. After struggling for goals, the striker suffered a serious patellar tendon injury in November 2022. He subsequently lost ground in Wolfsburg’s attacking hierarchy as Jonas Wind and other alternatives established themselves.

The contrasting trajectories were becoming increasingly apparent. Felix eventually received his first senior Germany call-up in March 2023. Remarkably, the two brothers had yet to appear together for the senior national team. Lukas’s seven international appearances had already been completed by the time Felix earned his first.

The former Manchester City youngster was now beginning to establish an independent reputation.

And Borussia Dortmund were watching closely.

Dortmund invested approximately €30 million to acquire Nmecha in the summer of 2023. Jude Bellingham’s departure to Real Madrid had left a significant vacancy in the BVB midfield. Nmecha arrived with an entirely different skill set from the English international, but his physical and technical attributes offered considerable potential.

The move also represented a dramatic change in circumstances. Felix was no longer the inexperienced youngster Wolfsburg had signed without paying a transfer fee. He had become a substantial investment expected to contribute immediately at one of Germany’s biggest clubs.

His first campaign at the Westfalenstadion proved difficult. Recurring injury problems repeatedly interrupted his development, while the challenge of establishing himself within Edin Terzic’s tactical arrangements created further complications.

Nmecha’s positional versatility sometimes proved as much of a problem as an advantage. He possessed the strength and ball-winning ability to operate deeper in midfield, but his capacity to carry possession forward and threaten opposition defences suggested that a purely defensive role might not make the best use of his qualities.

Gradually, however, the situation improved.

During the first half of the 2024/25 campaign, Nmecha began demonstrating greater consistency in a deeper midfield role. His performances suggested that Dortmund finally possessed a player capable of combining defensive security with progressive passing and ball-carrying.

The improvement had already begun when a familiar figure arrived to oversee the next stage of his development.

Niko Kovac’s appointment as Dortmund head coach in February 2025 reunited Nmecha with the manager who had overseen his original Bundesliga breakthrough. The Croatian knew precisely what the midfielder could offer. He also understood that restricting Nmecha to a single positional interpretation would overlook some of the player’s greatest strengths.

Speaking in March 2025, Kovac offered a revealing assessment of Nmecha’s abilities and long-term prospects.

“Of course he can also play in the number 10 role,” Kovac explained at the time. “But with his physicality, presence, technical skill, vision, and interpretation of the position, he is a future player for the number six or eight. He is the future of this club.”

By the summer of 2025, Get German Football News’ comprehensive Dortmund tactical preview described Nmecha as something of a silent, unsung hero. The midfielder’s capacity to win possession and immediately initiate attacking moves rendered him a vital component of Kovac’s approach. His increasingly influential performances naturally began attracting attention from abroad.

Inter and Manchester United were linked with Nmecha in 2025. Dortmund subsequently extended his contract through 2030, while Newcastle United’s reported interest during the summer of 2026 generated discussion of a potential nine-figure transfer fee.

“In terms of his talent, his qualities, and his technique, he’s one of the best midfielders in the world,” BVB sporting director Nils Ole Book told Sky Germany after Saturday’s win. “If he adds finishing to his game, there aren’t many who are better.”

“It’s already his second brace of the season,” Kovac remarked at the post-match press conference. “But there’s also his presence, dynamism, and technical ability. He can solve many situations quickly with his body movements. Things like that and his acceleration help him make everyone around him better.

“I believe, he just turned 26, and is entering his prime,” Kovac continued. “Last season, there were five games near the end [of the campaign] in which we didn’t have him and, accordingly, you could see how our game thinned out somewhat. With him, our game has a totally new dimension.”

The final observation is particularly revealing. It reflects a development considerably more significant than the occasional spectacular goal. Nmecha has become a midfielder whose absence affects the entire structure of Dortmund’s play. His athleticism, technical security, positional versatility, and ability to accelerate attacking transitions have made him extraordinarily difficult to replace.

That was hardly a foreseeable outcome when the younger Nmecha brother followed Lukas to Wolfsburg in 2021.

Felix is now an established German international valued at €65m.

'Robbed': Arsenal fans react to Kai Havertz penalty decision as VAR makes glaring error

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Arsenal fans have reacted to VAR’s decision not to award them a penalty in their 1-0 win over Hull City.

Advantage Arsenal in title race as Man City stunned by Nottm Forest

Spurs to submit formal complaint after Leeds red card controversy

Arteta issues Arsenal injury latest amid fan concern over Rice substitution

Choose the right education path for your child

The Gunners thought they should have had a spot kick at the Emirates Stadium when Kai Havertz went down under challenge from Nobel Mendy, with the visiting centre-back adjudged to have pulled the Gunners forward’s shirt in the 18-yard box.

Referee Farai Hallam was advised by the video assistant referee to halt the game and review the incident on the pitchside monitor, and when he re-emerged on the pitch, he acknowledged that the Hull defender had committed a foul.

That prompted whoops from the home faithful, but they were soon quietened as he ruled that the German had been in an offside position when he was felled, and as such, a free kick would be given to the visitors, rather than a penalty to the Premier League champions.

It turned out that the decision was in fact wrong, with an offside only taking precedent over a penalty claim when the player in said offside position is looking to play the ball. In Havertz’s case, he was not, and Mendy escaped.

As such, the Arsenal players, staff and supporters were furious, and it led to angry posts on social media from fans.

One said that his side were “robbed”, while another begged for “greater consistency” of refereeing across the English football pyramid.

Another account called out the top flight, saying that the organsiation needed to “get their act together”.

Arsenal’s victory takes them six points clear at the top of the table after 10 games, and they were handed an additional boost in the title race after Manchester City were beaten 3-1 by Nottingham Forest.

The hugely popular romantic drama starring Connor Storrie and Hudson Williams has not one but two six-episode seasons on the way, sources tell Deadline. And that’s not all, there is a movie coming up, too.

The two seasons are currently filming back-to-back, sources said. As they wrap at the end of November or beginning of December, a standalone Heated Rivalry film will shoot in December, I hear. Described as a Christmas special or a holiday-themed episode, it is planned for a theatrical release next December, I’m told. According to sources, Storrie’s Ilya and Williams’ Shane are not in it. After Season 1 adapted Book 2, Heated Rivalry, which chronicles the budding romance between rival hockey players Shane Hollander (Williams) and Ilya Rozanov (Storrie), while also incorporating elements from Book 1, Game Changer (featuring Scott and Kip), Season 2 is expected to continue Shane and Ilya’s story by tackling Book 6, The Long Game, while also introducing a new couple from Role Model, Harris Drover and Troy Barrett, played by Justice Smith and Charlie Gillespie, respectively and possibly incorporating elements from other tomes in the series.

C'est la victoire de tout un groupe, les entrants notamment nous ont apporté l'énergie nécessaire pour renverser ce match. Man City's slump cannot be excused by guilty verdict: Too many blunders, too much arm waving and football problems are piling up after 3-1 defeat by Nottingham Forest, writes JACK GAUGHAN

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:52 EDT, 7 November 2026 | Updated: 15:31 EDT, 7 November 2026

This run will not be about the verdicts. This cannot be about the verdicts. Footballers do not care about these things, the stuff out of their control. Besides, all of them are earning cut-price transfers elsewhere were Manchester City to suffer relegation anyway. And cut-price fees mean bulkier salaries.

So to them the current climate, the circumstances around City – their recent history and their immediate future – does not overly matter. Sure, there might be the odd one who is slightly more philosophical and inquisitive about appeals and appendices but the majority won't be giving it much or any thought.

City insist on their innocence publicly and privately; until that changes, their players and Enzo Maresca carry on regardless. There were the usual jeers here, there were ditties about cheating, but that surely cannot affect them.

Instead, the performances over the past few weeks, this a third consecutive Premier League defeat and now sinking six points adrift of Arsenal, cannot be excused by the findings of an independent commission. The problems are football problems.

This is a squad who appear uncertain about specific aspects of their game. Namely how to build from the back – and that is prompting individual mistakes. It happened against Ipswich Town and at Aston Villa. On the river Trent, it was no different.

The statistics say City dominated another game and they deserved more but the eyes tell another story.

Man City are a squad who now appear uncertain about specific aspects of their game

The statistics say City dominated another game and they deserved more but the eyes tell another story

Three defeats in a row have seen Enzo Maresca's side slip six points behind leaders Arsenal

Too many errors. Too much arm waving. Too much feeling sorry for themselves when decisions go elsewhere. Oliver Glasner is not stupid, he will have seen examples of this play out in parts of City's victories as well as reverses.

As Liam Delap raced into the corner to celebrate his second with 17 minutes left – a huge day in his career against the club which moulded him – Glasner was dashing down the touchline with unbridled joy.

Nottingham Forest (5-2-2-1): Sels 6; Munoz 6 (Netz 65, 6), Cunha 8, Milenkovic 8, Murillo 7, Williams 8; Yates 8 (Sangare 79), Schlager 8; McAtee 7 (Ndoye 65, 6), Gibbs-White 7; Delap 9 (Jesus 87)

Man City (4-1-4-1): Donnarumma 5; Khusanov 5, Dias 5, Guehi 4 (Nunes 46, 7), Gvardiol 4 (O'Reilly 46, 6); Anderson 5; Ndiaye 6, Cherki 6, Fernandez 4 (Foden 78), Doku 6; Haaland 5

Forest displays have deserved more than their points total so far and this was vindication for their fifth manager in 14 months. Delap was exceptional, some personal redemption after criticism.

The way in which Glasner nudged James McAtee further forward, stationed higher from the off in order to pressurise the visitors, spoke to an idea that City arrived here ready to be ruffled and have been wearing a look of fragility.

By the time this was eight minutes shy of half time and McAtee was nicking possession from Elliot Anderson, drawing a foul that sparked another onslaught on Gianluigi Donnarumma's box, Forest were already two clear – and had been for some time. Phil Foden warmed up on the touchline, laughing and hugging with Callum Hudson-Odoi.

Forest preyed on City insecurities. A midfield structure that is simply not working at the moment, easily railroaded. A defence allergic to the basics and an epidemic of individual mistakes – either as a result of nervousness or blase attitude on the ball.

Straight to the mistakes. A real collection, these: the first in the fifth minute and the others 13 minutes afterwards. Jair Cunha wheeled away after picking Josko Gvardiol's pocket, meandering forward into the box and side-footing into the far corner largely unchallenged. Gvardiol seemed to have the air of a man who felt invincible when gathering possession before being eased off it.

Later, as Delap thundered forward 45 yards unchecked to hammer past Donnarumma, you wondered about the communication between these City players. Anderson and Iliman Ndiaye left things to each other near halfway, Xaver Schlager gave his thanks – finding Delap – and the City academy graduate bulldozed clear. Marc Guehi waved him through, Ruben Dias dithered and Maresca bowed his head.

Liam Delap was exceptional for Nottingham Forest, some personal redemption after criticism

Remarkably, this was the first time in seven years that City had gone two down inside 18 minutes

Remarkably, this was the first time in seven years that City had gone two down inside 18 minutes. Dias looked aghast at Guehi, who was then hooked at the break alongside Gvardiol. Erling Haaland and Rayan Cherki both stood in the centre circle, hands on hips.

It later emerged via replays that Anderson had been arguing with Abdukodir Khusanov in the build-up to Forest's opener as well, proof that City were nowhere near close to producing what was expected when on the ball in their own half.

Glasner had the weapons at his disposal to make this work but even the Austrian must have been surprised at the way in which they tickled City's soft underbelly. They had not scored at the City Ground all season. All change when owner Evangelos Marinakis made his first trip to see a game since Glasner's appointment, which felt particularly handy.

Anger permeated the eyes of some City players as the whistle went. Cherki stormed down the tunnel, animatedly discussing how to play through Forest with Fernandez when they re-emerged.

Their lifeline came after 52 minutes, a Ryan Yates deflection from Jeremy Doku's cross sitting up for Cherki to hammer home a half-volley off the bar. Doku soon almost squeezed another effort past Matz Sels and the mood shifted, City briefly creeping menacingly like the monsters they are portrayed as.

Maresca had introduced Matheus Nunes and Nico O'Reilly, with both affecting the upturn. An admission of selection errors, perhaps, although O'Reilly is managing an ongoing back issue and is set for therapy on it over the international break.

City will need some of that as a collective. Delap won the ball inside his own third, gliding past Anderson, and Forest's explosive counter ended with Delap spearing a shot in off the post after Morgan Gibbs-White carried possession unopposed for 60 yards. Delirium, Glasner darting to the corner flag.

The quirk of this is that City's failings mean Manchester United drop into the bottom three.

Magnus Ditlev Sets New Iron-Distance World Best With 7:19:17 at Ironman Florida

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Magnus Ditlev set the fastest-ever Iron-distance time at the 2026 Ironman Florida (Photo by Sean M. Haffey/Getty Images for IRONMAN) (Photo: Getty Images for IRONMAN)

One month after a heartbreaking DNF in Kona, Magnus Ditlev (DEN) rode away from the field at Ironman Florida and never looked back. His 7:19:17 took 1:47 off the previous men’s iron-distance world best that Sam Laidlow (FRA) set at Challenge Roth earlier this year.

Florida was a chance at redemption for Ditlev, who had come agonizingly close to winning the Ironman World Championship in Kona. He’d taken the lead late on the bike and looked on course for the win until he abruptly came to a stop on the run with roughly 30 minutes of racing left.

However, ahead of Kona, Ditlev said he was putting up career-best numbers across the swim, bike, and run at his training camp in Colorado. In Florida, he finally got to reap the rewards of what fitness could produce all the way to the finish line.

Ditlev came out of the water in fifth, right on the heels of Ben Kanute (USA) and Rudy von Berg (USA). He took his time in T1 and started the bike nearly a minute down, but it didn’t take him long to ride back to the front. And then, just past mile 10, he made his move. No one could go with him, and they wouldn’t be able to stay remotely close to him either.

By the time Ditlev came off the bike, he was 11 minutes clear of a chase group that included Sam Long (USA), von Berg, and Kanute. His 3:51:16 was the fastest bike split ever recorded at an Ironman, nearly two minutes quicker than the 3:53:12 Robert Kallin (SWE) rode at Ironman Kalmar in August.

Off the bike, he once again took his time in transition. But transitions were the only slow part of Ditlev’s day. Once through T2, he picked up another minute on the chasers over the opening 10 miles. He hit halfway in 1:15, on pace for a 2:30 marathon and well within reach of Laidlow’s 7:21:04. With such an unassailable lead, one can assume he must’ve known the record was within reach to keep such an aggressive pace.

Ditlev eventually closed with a 2:33:31 marathon, crossing the line in 7:19:17. Von Berg took second, 14 minutes back, securing the early Kona slot and podium he’d come for. Matt Hanson (USA), meanwhile, ran a remarkable 2:28:07 to beat Long to the final podium spot, but still finished 16 minutes behind Ditlev.

"This trailer is my way of saying goodbye": Have a listen to the Mass Effect 5 score that could have been, courtesy of one of the original games' composers

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

By this point I'm probably safe in assuming that you all know EA are shutting down BioWare, and that Mass Effect 5 has been cancelled in turn. You may have also seen that an internal trailer for Mass Effect 5, or Universe as it was seemingly going to be called, was leaked not long after the news broke, and that Sam Hulick, one of the composers on the original trilogy of games, shared he was going to offer up his re-scored version of the trailer. Well, it's N7 Day, as rough as that will be for many of the developers on the game, but Hulick has stood by his word, and shared an oral glimpse at what the world of Mass Effect Universe could have sounded like.

You can check out this new version of the trailer below, perhaps pop it on while you have a read of the rest of this post, as Hulick did also write in a Patreon blog about his experience with the series. "As part of my own closure, and as a love letter to the fans, I really wanted to re-score the leaked Mass Effect Universe trailer in time for N7 Day this year," he wrote. "BioWare edited a killer track called “Quartz” by French 79 to fit their trailer, which I think hinted at a slight directional shift in the score. Using that as inspiration, along with the musical tone from my work on the first three Mass Effect games, I hit the studio and got to work."

As it turns out, BioWare had actually reached out to Hulick asking him to potentially do some work for the project. "This year, BioWare emailed me asking if I was interested in working with them on an internal demo, and they said they were 'hoping to get some music written that would feel like Mass [Effect], supporting our new direction.' Of course, I said yes!" This doesn't mean he wouldn't have composed the actual game, as a composer hadn't been officially selected, but Hulick said he was "more than happy to collaborate on the new game’s musical direction and help make their internal demo sound as good as it could."

Hulick also spoke of some of the ideas he had for the direction Mass Effect Universe's soundtrack could take, noting how he "thought it would’ve been incredible to bring more songs with lyrics into the game," considering how many people loved the first game's end-credits song. "This would’ve been a great way to highlight more emotional moments in the narrative. Artists at the top of my collaboration list included Gunship, NINA, and The Midnight, all of whom have a moody, cinematic synthwave vibe that would’ve meshed well with the score in my head. And I’m pretty sure the Gunship guys are gamers, and if they’re Mass Effect fans, that would’ve been the icing on the cake!"

Of course, with the studio now closing, this never got to go very far, the news hitting Hulick "particularly hard, as the Mass Effect trilogy is a core part of my origin story as a composer." For the composer, ultimately "this trailer is my way of saying goodbye… at least for now." This continues to be a horrendously sad moment in the industry! As always, solidarity with the team at BioWare.

Liam Delap’s emphatic brace earned Nottingham Forest a first home win of the season and piled the misery on Manchester City with a 3-1 victory.

Delap scored in either half after Jair Cunha had given Forest an early lead as City lost for the third Premier League game in a row.

This victory could be a coming of age for both Delap and Forest, who had struggled to adapt to life under new boss Oliver Glasner, with just one win from their opening nine games.

Delap had scored once in his opening eight games following his club-record move from Chelsea, but his brilliant double proved he can be the man to fire Forest to better things this season.

The win is their first in the league at the City Ground since April and sends Manchester United into the bottom three.

City found themselves 2-0 down inside 20 minutes, but did threaten a comeback when Rayan Cherki scored early in the second half.

But they slipped to a third loss on the bounce and amid the backdrop of the ongoing appeal against Premier League charges, their season is showing signs of falling apart.

Forest enjoyed an opening 20 minutes they could never have imagined as they were all over City and surged into a two-goal lead.

Having waited the best part of eight and a half hours to score in front of their fans, perhaps unsurprisingly the drought, going back to last May, was ended by an unlikely source in the sixth minute.

Centre-back Jair robbed Josko Gvardiol high up the pitch, advanced into the area and slotted past Gianluigi Donnarumma.

It was 2-0 in the 19th minute as Delap broke his City Ground duck in fine style.

Former Forest midfielder Elliot Anderson, who had a nightmare return to the ground where he turned into a £116million player, and Iliman Ndiaye left the ball to each other in the Forest half and Delap pounced, running all the way into the City area before firing an unstoppable shot past Donnarumma.

Erling Haaland had been a virtual spectator but came alive in the 40th minute, only to be denied by some great defending by Jair.

But they did get one back in the 52nd minute as Cherki delivered a firecracker.

Southwest Airlines Flight Attendant Allegedly Quit Job After Passenger-Facing Crew Dispute

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

A Southwest Airlines flight attendant allegedly quit after a verbal argument with other cabin crew in front of a fully boarded plane. A report by View From The Wing has suggested that passengers were told a flight attendant was sick before the captain of the flight to Kansas City came on the loudspeaker and explained that the two had 'personality differences'.

Just this past Friday, Southwest flight 557 was set to operate from Dallas Love Field (DAL) to Kansas City International Airport (MCI), when the pushback of the jet was delayed due to the disagreement between the two co-workers. The argument set the flight back several hours.

Shared on a passenger's Reddit page, an eyewitness passenger onboard described the argument between the two Southwest flight attendants taking place at the front of the aircraft in full view for all of the passengers onboard. This resulted in the crew being taken off the aircraft and then it being blamed on them being sick.

This performance in front of the other passengers forced the captain to speak to the passengers onboard to explain the situation, with the blame being put down to personality differences. Further claims have suggested that the flight was already delayed by ten minutes, but the full delay of up to two hours before the jet finally pushed back and departed for Kansas City was down to the crew dispute.

Simple Flying reached out to Southwest for comment on Saturday; however, an airline spokesperson could not immediately be reached.

As suggested by VFTW, one of the Southwest flight attendants involved in the argument has since quit their job with the world's largest operator of the Boeing 737, after they were embarrassed after having a verbal argument with another crew member in front of a fully boarded aircraft.

Southwest flight 557, a regular scheduled six-weekly service between Dallas and Kansas City, was scheduled to depart at 2:25 PM. Due to an initial maintenance delay, and then the subsequent crew argument, the flight was delayed two hours and 24 minutes before the aircraft eventually took off.

The short one-hour, four-minute journey then landed in Kansas City at 6:16 PM that evening, two hours and 21 minutes behind its initial scheduled arrival time of 3:55 PM. The aircraft operating the service was that of a Boeing 737-700, bearing registration N255WN. Full aircraft characteristics are in the table below, as per ch-aviation:

N281WN the carrier's 500th 737 circled over the South Bay as pilots ran emergency checklists and prepared for a single‑engine return.

With the crew member allegedly quitting and refusing to operate the flight, this ultimately forced Southwest to find a replacement crew member to crew the service to Kansas City, and was the primary cause of the flight's delay.

To crew a 737-700, the typical requirements are two pilots and three cabin crew to serve the total of 137 passengers in an all-economy class configuration. Southwest is the largest operator of the 737-700, with more than 270 active aircraft in its fleet.

Middlesbrough, QPR & West Brom have all dodged Bamba Dieng transfer bullet

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Middlesbrough, Queens Park Rangers, and West Bromwich Albion were all linked with Senegal international Bamba Dieng during the summer transfer window, but none ultimately pursued a deal.

The 26-year-old instead joined Serbian giants Red Star Belgrade on a free transfer, where he has enjoyed some encouraging moments in front of goal after not joining Middlesbrough, QPR, or West Brom.

However, there is a growing argument that the Championship trio might have been wise to steer clear, particularly when looking beyond Bamba Dieng's domestic scoring record and assessing how he has performed on the European stage.

Dieng has certainly found the net a reasonable number of times since arriving in Serbia, but there is a significant difference between scoring in the Serbian SuperLiga and delivering consistently in the Championship.

More importantly, his performances in the UEFA Conference League have hardly suggested he would have been a transformative signing for the likes of Middlesbrough, West Brom, or QPR this season. Boro were even in talks with Dieng at one stage of the transfer window.

All three clubs should arguably be targeting promotion and, given their size and stature, would surely fancy their chances of competing for glory in a European competition of that nature if they were playing at that level.

FLW have ranked all 24 Championship clubs on how far they have fallen from their historical glory days, including Blackburn and West Ham.

A high-wage arrival such as Dieng should be demonstrating genuine upside against continental opposition, rather than simply padding his statistics and goal involvements in domestic fixtures for a club the size and stature of Red Star Belgrade in Serbia's top flight.

Red Star have faced Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes, with that trio being opponents that Championship sides would reasonably expect to beat or at least give a very good account of themselves against.

Dieng has failed to register a goal or assist in any of those three matches. Perhaps all three English clubs had the right idea by ultimately looking elsewhere or sticking with what they had in the end. The fact he limped off the pitch assisted by medial staff in their most recent fixture underlines this, too.

Dieng began his professional career in Senegal with Diambars before earning a move to Marseille in 2021, where he scored eight goals in 40 appearances. He subsequently joined Lorient permanently in January 2023, spending time on loan at Angers before enjoying his most productive campaign yet in 2025/26.

The 24-cap Senegal international may well have made the move to the Championship over the recent summer but he has also experienced missing out on a Premier League move, having agreed to join Leeds United from Marseille in September 2022 before a dramatic deadline-day change of plans saw Nice attempt to hijack the deal (BBC Sport).

Are you happy with your club's striking options with January just around the corner? Comment below!

Since joining Red Star on a free transfer in August, he has scored five goals in seven Serbian league appearances. However, he has no goals or assists in three Conference League outings, totalling just over 200 minutes, against opposition of the stature and quality of Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes.

Dieng would not have been somebody who could comfortably arrive as an expensive substitute at any of these three clubs, meaning his signing would have carried significant consequences for their existing attacking options.

At Middlesbrough, he could have been brought in instead of Will Lankshear, which would have been a considerable mistake given the former Tottenham Hotspur youngster's extraordinary goalscoring form in the Championship.

Type the top scorer next to each season. Surnames count. Two seasons had joint winners, so they need two names.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

QPR might have been tempted to install him ahead of Richard Kone, potentially stunting the development of one of their most exciting young attackers. Meanwhile, West Brom already have Jimmy-Jay Morgan performing well, with Aune Heggebo also contributing positively.

All three clubs have younger forwards worth developing rather than blocking with an expensive, high-wage alternative. Dieng might yet prove to be a success in Serbia, but the Championship trio arguably made the right decisions for their respective squads.

Mass Effect 3 Legendary Edition Mod Restores Multiplayer

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Mass Effect 3’s criminally underrated multiplayer mode has been restored and added to the Legendary Edition as a special N7 Day treat from a skilled group of fans.

Today, November 7, should be a very special occasion for Mass Effect fans. Back when the series was alive and thriving, BioWare coined N7 Day as a time to celebrate the series and share updates about its future. Of course, with BioWare and the next big game in the series, Universe, recently being shut down by EA, the atmosphere is a little dire.

As the leaked trailer for Universe and all of the new merchandise have kept proving, BioWare had some big plans in store for this year’s N7 Day. Even though the future of Mass Effect is a complete unknown and everyone is still in mourning, fans have come together to try and give the series a worthy send-off, which an impressive modding project has certainly done.

As pointed out by ResetEra user Lord Vatek, renowned Mass Effect modding group ME3Tweaks decided to cheer us all up on this rather depressing N7 Day by releasing Legendary Edition Multiplayer. The mod, which is currently live on Nexus Mods, is a “full game conversion” that adds Mass Effect 3’s multiplayer mode to the remaster.

Although Mass Effect 3’s multiplayer was a little divisive at launch, it’s gone on to be a bit of a cult classic for fans and was considered a notable omission from the Legendary Edition. It’s something that’s still talked about to this day, making its comeback more than five years later a pretty big deal. Especially considering it’s N7 Day.

The mod doesn’t just recreate the original multiplayer, though, as ME3Tweaks actually built on it in some pretty big ways. Heavy weapons like the Reaper Blackstar and Cain are now available, as are an overhauled photo mode, configurable aim assist, and improved FOV settings. New banners, accessibility options, and improved textures round everything out as an impressive expansion of the original mode.

As if all of that isn’t enough, ME3Tweaks aims to add matchmaking server statistics, a proper multiplayer store, and a total rework of the challenges screen.

There are already plenty of impressive Mass Effect mods out there, including some of this modding team’s previous work, but Legendary Edition Multiplayer has to be up there as one of the best. It’s a damn good way of showing just how passionate the Mass Effect community is, even as the series gets put through hell and back.

We opened 49 packs of the new set, this is what we thought.

Reba McEntire Reveals Dolly Parton’s Touching On-Set Gesture: ‘She Didn’t Have To Do That’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Reba McEntire was determined to make Dolly Parton feel welcome when the country legend joined her sitcom for a guest appearance—but it was Parton who ended up making everyone on-set feel special.

While speaking at NAR NXT in New Orleans alongside her manager and former real estate agent, Justin McIntosh, McEntire recalled the thoughtful gesture Parton made for every crew member behind the scenes of her hit sitcom “Reba”—a small act of kindness that left a lasting impression.

Instead of simply showing up and delivering her lines, Parton made a point of personally greeting the people working behind the scenes, bringing along Valentine’s Day candy and a message for every crew member.

For McEntire, the gesture remains a vivid example of the warmth that made her fellow singer so beloved.

“I was so nervous with Dolly being on the set. I wanted everyone to be nice. What I didn’t expect was how nice she was going to be on the set," she said.

McEntire recalled that Parton took the time to make each person feel appreciated, turning a guest appearance into a chance to connect with the crew.

“She had Valentine’s Day candies, and she went to every crew member individually and said how happy she was to be there,” she said. “She didn’t have to do that.”

The memory has stayed with McEntire, along with a conversation the two shared while sitting together on the iconic show's couch.

“I asked her if she would ever retire,” McEntire recalled. “And she said, ‘What would I do that I could have so much fun doing?’”

It was a simple answer, but one that reflected the enthusiasm McEntire remembers so fondly.

“I’ve never met a person or known a person that’s so universally loved like Dolly Parton,” she said. “I’ve never heard anything bad about her. She was that special of a person.”

That sense of gratitude for a career filled with opportunities to connect with people is something McEntire shares.

“I’m just really grateful and thankful for doing what I do at the level I do,” she said.

For McEntire, keeping her work exciting means giving herself room to try new things—and inviting her audience along for the experience.

“When I change things up, my fans stay interested with me,” she explained. “If it looks like I’m having fun, then they’re having fun.”

The actress revealed that being able to enjoy her work is a gift.

“If you are doing what you love, then that’s how blessed you are,” she said. “That’s one of the greatest gifts God can give, to give you a job you love. I’m very grateful for my job.”

The connection she has built with her audience also rests on the principle that people can recognize sincerity principle—something she believes applies well beyond entertainment.

“I try to sing the songs from my heart so when you listen to it, it touches your heart,” she said.

That emphasis on warmth and connection carries into the way McEntire searches for a home, too.

Long before her career brought her to stages and television sets, she grew up on an 8,000-acre ranch in Oklahoma.

Despite the expanse of land outside, her family of six shared a small house with three bedrooms and one bathroom.

Asked what she first thinks of when she remembers the ranch, McEntire named the people and feelings that filled it.

“Love, family, unity, closeness,” she said. “One itty-bitty bathroom, three bedrooms. We were a close family.”

Gathering around the dinner table was part of life, and the atmosphere of the house remains easy for her to recall.

“We all sat at the dinner table together,” she said. “It was earthy. I can still smell how the house smelled and the energy.”

The land remains a family home. McEntire says it stayed in the family after her parents died, and her sister and her sister’s family now live on the property.

Those early memories offer a glimpse of what McEntire still values when she walks into a house: a feeling of comfort and a connection that goes beyond its appearance.

“A house has energy, and when you come in and when you bond with that energy, it makes it a home,” she said. “So I always look for good, positive energy when I come in.”

The country icon reflected on the moment she experienced that pull while looking for property in Tennessee.

A friend who was vacationing in Florida with a real estate agent spotted a listing the agent was viewing and urged that it be shown to McEntire.

“My friend said, ‘You gotta show Reba that place,’” she recalled.

The approach to the property and a creek at the back helped capture her attention. Soon, she was returning for visits and spending time with the owners.

“The love I had for that house, it took my breath away,” she said. “I went a couple times, and I watched football games with the owners.”

When it comes to her priorities in a home, being comfortable and enjoying the surroundings rank high.

“I love a home with a view,” she said. “When I lived in Oklahoma, you could see sunrise and sunset. That’s important to me.”

McEntire said that focus on people and their needs also helped draw her to her partnership with Realtor.com®.

“What y'all do is very important, you care so much, that's why I like Realtor.com. I love your organization because you have heart...You really do dive into the family and what their needs are. You love people and you care about people, that's why I wanted to be with you guys," she said.

Whether she is discussing finding a home or maintaining the trust of an audience, McEntire returns to the importance of treating people well—and continuing to do so over time.

“You have to stay consistently honest, because when you look someone in the eye, they know how you treated them last time,” she said. “You have to stay consistent with it.”

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

The wild Premier League table since Man City’s charges were announced – hand the trophy to Arsenal now?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

It looks increasingly as though the Premier League could be a cakewalk for the reigning champions Arsenal – whether or not Manchester City are handed a points deduction.

Man City went into the last international break with a 100% winning record under new manager Enzo Maresca, with it all looking like business as usual following the departure of their era-defining manager Pep Guardiola at the end of last season.

However, things have taken a severe dip after the news broke that the club have been found guilty of the vast majority of their 115 charges.

The Athletic’s David Ornstein originally broke the news over the extended international break, and a few days later the Premier League confirmed it with a statement that could have massive repercussions for the English top flight.

“An independent Commission has found Manchester City FC guilty of all charges related to serious breaches of the Premier League’s financial rules over a nine-season period, and the majority of charges in relation to its failure to co-operate with the League’s investigation,” read their statement.

Any potential sanctions are yet to be confirmed or announced, while Man City have lodged an appeal against the verdict, having stated that they’re “both disappointed and surprised by the opinion of the Premier League Commission” and continuing to protest their innocence.

City responded in a defiant mood, having extended their winning run by going to Anfield and beating Liverpool 3-0. But a few days later, they were defeated 4-2 to PSG in the Champions League and a serious domestic slump has followed.

Maresca’s men have now suffered three successive Premier League defeats, a run that started with a 2-1 defeat away to Aston Villa. Losses to Brighton and Nottingham Forest have followed.

The result leaves them six points behind Arsenal at this fledgling stage of the Premier League campaign. The Gunners have won nine of their 10 league outings, are consistently keeping clean sheets, and the recent form is ominous.

The question of a points deduction when it comes to a Premier League title race could soon be academic.

City have picked up just six points from five matches since the verdict was announced in September. That’s nine fewer points than Mikel Arteta’s Gunners, four fewer than Tottenham, and fewer than the likes of Brighton, Bournemouth, Fulham and Everton.
