Richard Lloyd Dies: Guitarist With Influential Punk Pioneers Television Was 75

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Richard Lloyd, whose intricate guitar work for the pioneering punk band Television was a fiery counterpart to the sinewy stylings of the band’s co-guitarist and singer Tom Verlaine, died today of the bile duct cancer called cholangiocarcinoma at his home in Chattanooga, Tenn. He was 75.

His death was reported by his family to The New York Times. Lloyd had been diagnosed with the cancer in March.

With his Television bandmates Verlaine, bassist Richard Hell (later replaced by Fred Smith) and drummer Billy Ficca, Lloyd was among the innovative, even revolutionary, New York bands in the mid-1970s to establish the East Village dive bar CBGB as the epicenter of a new downtown music scene. Along with Patti Smith and the Ramones, Television led the way in creating and developing a new sound – defined later (and loosely) as punk – that was often as literate and playful as it was sonically powerful. Related Stories News Paul Howard Dies: Son Of Three Stooges' Moe Howard, Keeper Of The Flame Was 91 News Kimmarie Johnson Dies: 'Beverly Hills, 90210' & 'Dude, Where's My Car?' Actor Was 50

Formed in 1973, Television grew out of the teenage friendship of two Delaware boys, Thomas Miller and Richard Meyers, who by the early ’70s had changed their names to, respectively, Tom Verlaine and Richard Hell and set about developing the spiky guitar sound and keening vocal style (courtesy Verlaine) that would reach its apotheosis in the band’s 1977 masterpiece Marquee Moon. Watch on Deadline

By the time of their 1974 debut at CBGB, Verlaine and Hell had recruited Lloyd to be the group’s second guitarist, a move that gave Television its signature and distinctive dueling Strat-versus-Jazzmaster sound. Unlike the more power-chord-heavy guitar bombast of other bands on the scene, Television specialized in complicated, even byzantine interplay between the six-stringed instruments. And like Patti Smith, Verlaine sang lyrics that signified a devotion to poetry as surely as did his chosen name.

Born Richard John Alt on Oct. 25, 1951, in Pittsburgh, Lloyd grew up in Manhattan’s Greenwich Village and began playing guitar before he hit his teens. He became a regular in the audience of the famed Fillmore East rock palace, mesmerized by the music of The Grateful Dead and other bands of the era. He would be forthcoming about working as a male hustler to support himself during the lean years.

With an early residency at CBGB, Television quickly won over audiences as well as fellow musicians like Patti Smith, Lou Reed and David Bowie, who had caught the band during a trip to New York and set about spreading the word to anyone who would listen. Bowie would later record the group’s song “Kingdom Come” for his 1980 Scary Monsters album

The band’s initial line-up didn’t last long, though, as the old friends Verlaine and Hell began to bicker over everything from their music to the way it was performed on stage. Hell quit in 1975 and formed a sort of punk supergroup called The Heartbreakers with ex-New York Dolls members Johnny Thunders and Jerry Nolan. He eventually left that group to form Richard Hell and the Voidoids.

Hell was replaced in Television by ex-Blondie bassist Fred Smith (not to be confused with Patti Smith’s husband Fred “Sonic” Smith).

Despite its quick success, Television called it quits in 1978, in part because of Lloyd’s increasingly dire heroin addiction and mental health issues. Lloyd would release nine solo albums after Television, and he and Verlaine, despite a prickly relationship, occasionally reunited the band. Neither guitarist would ever match the success or influence of Television in its genre-building heyday.

Lloyd is survived by wife Sheila O’Keefe Lloyd; son Dylan; and brother Eric Lloyd.

Mortgage rates fell and demand steadied as inventory dipped

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Inventory declined week over week to 902,740 as mortgage rates eased for 3 straight weeks. With spreads at 2.27%, the path to sub-7% mortgage rates can happen with lower bond yields or better mortgage spreads.  AI Summary

Mortgage rates fell, demand stabilized and inventory had a noticeable decline last week. But are the changes in demand and inventory data about seasonality, or has the housing market changed with the recent mortgage rate decline? Let’s take a look at the weekend tracker and see what the data is telling us with rates falling the last three weeks.

In the 2026 HousingWire forecast, I anticipated the following ranges:

What a crazy time in the bond market recently: the key level of 5.35% has not only held since early October, but the reversal has been truly nice to watch. The midterm elections, which changed the control of the House and Senate, have added another variable into the mortgage rate equation. I discussed what Congress can do to help lower mortgage rates on this episode of the HousingWire Daily podcast with Editor in Chief Sarah Wheeler, but for now the head-and-shoulders pattern of the 10-year yield is still somewhat intact, though a fight for it to break lower is happening.

I wouldn’t get too excited about mortgage rates until the 10-year yield can break under 4.70% and have follow-through bond buying, but mortgage rates have fallen and demand has stabilized over the last few weeks.

Mortgage spreads have worsened recently, but they’re still trending near multiyear lows. Traditionally, when mortgage rates escalate with the 10-year yield and then make a big reversal — like we just had from a 5.35% 10-year yield top that dropped down toward 4.82% —  spreads do get worse to try to compress mortgage rate volatility.That said, if mortgage spreads weren’t near multiyear lows right now, we’d have no chance of getting mortgage rates below 7.25%, and demand wouldn’t have stabilized recently. Now that spreads have moved up from this year’s lows, we can clearly see that mortgage rates would be sub-7% if spreads return to that level.

Historically, mortgage spreads have ranged from 1.60% to 1.80%. Last week, spreads rose to 2.27% from 2.23% a week earlier.

Let’s compare last week’s mortgage rates to where they would have been at the best spread levels this year and the worst levels of this cycle.

Our pending home sales data provides a week-to-week perspective, though holidays and short-term fluctuations can affect results. This weekly pending sales data typically takes 30-60 days to reflect in sales data.

The key for the last three weeks: lower rates, even above 7%, have stabilized demand, as our weekly pending sales have shown. Now, this isn’t growth, just stabilization from a noticeable slowdown when mortgage rates got toward 7.60%. The last three years and 10 months show that housing demand grows when mortgage rates fall below 6.64% and head toward 6%; above 7%, we don’t have much growth data beyond seasonal demand. Last year at this time, mortgage rates were headed toward 6%, so the year-over-year comps will be hard for some time.

Here are the pending sales for last week over the last two years:

Purchase application data, which looks out 30-90 days, has softened as mortgage rates rose above 6.64% and are now above 7%. We had a small decline week to week; I can see the stabilization here as well and the year-over-year comps will also be very difficult here, as last year at this time rates were heading toward 6%. Earlier in the year, when rates were lower, pretty much every week this year showed growth.

Here are the stats on purchase apps so far in 2026:

Housing inventory growth has been very tame this year, with some weeks being down year over year. However, when mortgage rates got above 6.64% and then headed toward 7.60%, we did see some growth pick up. Also, rates were lower last year so demand was heading toward a nine-month high in December, which means it the comps will be easier to show growth in inventory.

Inventory growth is running at 7.18% even with easier comps.

New listings are in their typical seasonal decline; 2026 has been the healthiest new-listings year since 2022, with over 80,000 new listings per week a few times this year. The concern I had was that if higher rates cause sellers to stop listing their homes — as they did in the second half of 2022 — that would hurt supply and demand. However, that hasn’t happened in any meaningful way. Most home sellers are buyers, and now that rates have fallen a smidge, it looks like we can end the year on a positive note for 2026 for new listings.

In a normal environment, new listings range from 80,000 to 100,000 per week during peak periods. For context, during the housing bubble years, new listings ranged from 250,000 to 400,000 per week for several years.Here is last week’s new listings data for the past two years:

Typically, about one-third of homes see price reductions before they sell, reflecting the housing market’s dynamic nature. Overall, price-cut percentages this year have been lower than last year. This held true until rates moved above 6.64% and then above 7%; demand fell, and the year-over-year price-cut percentage rose 1%.In my 2026 home-price forecast, I called for a national decline of -0.62% for the year. Home-price growth really isn’t going anywhere this year, and my -0.62% forecast may be hard to achieve, since most home-price indexes show growth between 1% and 2%. However, with higher rates and a rising price-cut percentage, we will be closer to the forecast. Clearly, if rates hadn’t risen above 6.75%, pricing data wouldn’t have shown the recent slowdown.

You want to keep an eye on how AI stocks trade this week, in particular CoreWeave’s stock price and how the 10-year yield reacts. For now, there’s an interesting correlation between the two, but I expect it to fade over time.

It’s inflation week again, with both CPI and PPI reports. Over the next 12 months, if the Iran conflict ends and the trade war doesn’t get worse, the question is whether bond traders will put more weight on those two factors than on the current inflation reports, which could improve in the future. This would be a huge positive for mortgage rates, but still, it will take a lot of work to end the trade war and the conflict. Existing home sales will come out and it won’t be pretty. We also have a lot of Fed speeches this week and it’s critical now to see what they say and how the bond market reacts to their talking points. Also, with the midterms over, I will be watching whether any of them start to talk about the end of the conflict and trade war being a positive for their future outlook.

Because of Okafor: Nobody is Talking About How Bahoya's Leeds Career is Over

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Noah Okafor was the match-winner for Leeds United as they recorded a much-needed 1-0 victory over Tottenham on Saturday.

Okafor, 26, struck less than two minutes after coming on as the Whites earned a first win in five matches and moved back into the Premier League top-half.

The Switzerland international, who missed the 2-0 defeat at Bournemouth last time out with an adductor issue, was introduced as a 77th minute substitute at Elland Road.

He replaced Lukas Nmecha, who started as the left forward, looking to make an impact and help his side find a breakthrough after finding the Tottenham defence a hard nut to crack.

And just 97 seconds after his introduction, Okafor found the net. He combined with Harry Wilson inside the left channel before finding some space and slamming the ball past Antonin Kinksy.

It was a piece of quality and invention that was missing from Leeds, and there is no doubt that Okafor should be starting week-in-week out when fit.

He is someone that can create a chance out of nowhere and is Leeds' most potent attacker, barring Dominic Calvert-Lewin, of course. Lukas Nmecha worked hard and is a good back-up option, but Okafor, who scored eight goals last season, is the best Farke has in that position.

With Okafor coming back in and showing that he should be in Farke's XI, Jean-Matteo Bahoya's Leeds career could now be over. The Frenchman, 21, who joined the Whites on loan from Eintracht Frankfurt in the summer, was left out of the 20-man squad to face Tottenham.

But Bahoya has played just 11 minutes for Leeds since his arrival, making three appearances in the process. He came on as a late substitute in the defeat at Bournemouth, but was unable to impact the game as the Whites were beaten at the Vitality Stadium.

There is no doubt Bahoya has talent, having showed that in glimpses at Frankfurt, but he has been unable to show that since he moved to Leeds. He might not be showing enough in training, but what is clear is that Farke isn't having him right now.

With Leeds scoring just once in three matches prior to the win against Tottenham, it did feel as though Farke should have been using Bahoya more. But the German provided a 10-minute explanation last month on the reason the forward isn't getting more minutes.

Farke outlined the fact he is a "young lad still getting used to the Premier League" but reiterated he does "love" Bahoya and that he is going to get his chance.

At this stage, it doesn't look promising for Bahoya, however. While Leeds were struggling in attack, he wasn't called up and that felt like the perfect time for him to get more game time.

For now, Bahoya is going to have to bide his time, but his Leeds career might be over before it even got started, especially if Frankfurt consider recalling him in January.

Okafor should start for Leeds on the left-hand side with Nmecha as back-up and Bahoya down as third-choice, though Brenden Aaronson could even be in front of the ex-Angers man.

Daniel Farke has come under some pressure at Leeds United following the Whites' recent slump in form

Sony has delayed its Pulse Elevate wireless speakers indefinitely, less than a week before they were due to launch.

The news came in a 6th November update to Sony’s original announcement on the PlayStation Blog, first spotted by IGN. Quality testing has turned up a "production issue", Sony said, and there's no new release date yet.

We look forward to bringing Pulse Elevate to players soon."

The speakers were set to go on sale worldwide on 12th November, and pre-orders opened in September. It's unclear what, exactly, happens to those orders, but we'll update as and when we find out more.

Sony revealed Pulse Elevate in September 2025 as its first wireless speakers for desktop gaming. They sit alongside the Pulse Elite headset and Pulse Explore earbuds, and cost £199.99 ($219.99).

They connect to PS5, PC, Mac, and PlayStation Portal via PlayStation Link, and can pair with a phone over Bluetooth at the same time. There's also a microphone in the right speaker with what Sony calls "AI-enhanced noise rejection".

Midnight Black was due to be available everywhere, while the White version was limited to a handful of countries, including the UK.

Alongside the Pulse Elevate speakers, Sony revealed a range of hardware in September 2025, including a 27-inch gaming monitor that's coming only to the US and Japan.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Arsenal 1-0 Hull: The Gunners’ manager called for more pragmatism and chaos after Martin Odegaard’s goal secured a familier scoreline for Arsenal

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

After a familiar scoreline, Mikel Arteta has a new phrase.

The Arsenal manager used some variation on “insist” several times throughout his press conference for the win over Hull City, after his team had almost been left standing at the door.

Martin Odegaard instead offered a captain’s goal, for a third 1-0 win in four in the league. Like many of those, it could have been worse, but could have been better – especially in terms of finishing.

Arsenal currently always feel one setback away from a big discussion about which forward they need to sign in January. They are obviously monitoring a few options, and are expected to do some business, but Arteta clearly doesn’t want his players thinking like that.

When asked about what was actually a sixth win by one goal out of 10 Premier League games so far, he said they “need to be more efficient”.

It could be argued that such a return is the height of efficiency, and that Arsenal could have been further ahead had they benefited from that debated penalty decision – but you know what he meant, especially with regard to finishing.

Arteta lamented how his team had two further one-on-ones in the game, only to waste them. He said it was like they “are trying to score the perfect goal”.

That’s another familiar refrain with this club, if from a different era to the 1-0s. Arteta’s desired solution is to be “more pragmatic”, that they “need to be more chaotic”.

That’s undeniably true, but the manager being the one to say this poses some questions of his own.

Another phrase that Arteta used was “volume”. That, when you face a defence as well structured as Hull City, you need to be “generating volume”. In other words, numbers, a critical mass of chances where you eventually score – which is pretty much what they did. Insistence.

It’s been discussed on these pages before that Arteta is a huge “probabilities guy”, where he sees almost every game as an equation. If Arsenal consistently generate an xG of 2 or above and limit the opposition to 0.5, they are likely to win enough games to win the league.

A natural extension of this is an exactingly calibrated team, where Hull City players – like so many others – were left praising “the shape”.

But is that mentality something that encourages “pragmatism” or, as Arteta elaborated, “chaos”? Other descriptions for that are sparkle, ingenuity… magic.

That’s hard to find sufficient space for in a system that demands a lot of energy devoted to pressing and structure.

Odegaard, the match-winner, is almost the ultimate example. The playmaker is capable of some of the most ingenious and exquisite touches in the league, but it does not feel like he is as characterised by that as much any more.

When you ask people at Arsenal about him, in fact, they praise how he understands Arteta’s pressing better than anyone else. This is why he is captain and what they were said to really miss last season when he was out. Odegaard leads the press. When he goes, everyone immediately knows when and where to follow.

An irony is that Noni Madueke is one of the most chaotic players in the league – in a good way. He is a rare attacker that takes people on. As is the case when you see his and Christos Tzolis’s names on the teamsheet, you don’t quite know what you’re going to get in terms of performance level. Club insiders say it could be anything between a two and an eight out of 10.

The most frustrating thing about Madueke, in fact, is that he usually ends such runs with a predictable cross rather than real variation. He doesn’t shoot enough, which touches on Arteta’s point about trying to score “a perfect goal”.

All of this came together for a moment that should have sealed the win.

Arsenal’s attack put together a brilliant flowing move at 1-0, to leave Madueke through for one of those one-on-ones. It was close to perfect. Until, with just Konstantinos Tzolakis to beat, Madueke tried to roll the ball past him only to get his feet wrong.

While the forward clearly has potential, the point of this is that Arsenal still feel at least one attacker – but arguably two – away from really being at the level they want to be.

It should be stressed that their current level isn’t exactly much of a problem. They’ve won nine out of 10 in the league. They’re brilliantly efficient in everything outside the scoring.

Even this match has to be put in the context of a tough match against a very respectable Hull City side. They’re so well structured you can see them being this season’s Sunderland.

Arsenal were known to be impressed afterwards, with Arteta especially respectful. His team still get through, and you can see them being convincingly ahead by Christmas.

But the league isn’t the level anymore. It’s about beating Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League, about being European champions as well as English champions.

That is something they really have to insist on.

Time between pregnancies may shape how fast mothers age

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Each pregnancy adds biological age that takes months to come off, and birth spacing may decide how much a mother recovers before the next one.

Tests suggest that biological age rises during pregnancy and falls again in the months after birth. Researchers are now asking whether the time between pregnancies affects how fully those changes reverse.

Two Harvard researchers propose that the time a woman gets between pregnancies, not only how many she has, may shape how fast she ages for decades.

If the next pregnancy begins before that recovery is finished, the extra age may not be fully reversed, the authors wrote. The effect could then build with each closely spaced birth.

Stephen Albright and Jesse Poganik of Harvard Medical School and Brigham and Women’s Hospital laid out the idea in a review article. It pulls together studies from Europe, the United States, Israel and the Philippines.

The result is a hypothesis with testable predictions attached, not a new trial, and Poganik said the changes it describes don’t necessarily amount to damage.

“Our previous research has found that biological age transiently rises during pregnancy, peaking around the third trimester or at the time of delivery,” said Poganik, the corresponding author.

“If we accept the idea that these short-term changes in biological age occur, the question is how we can relate those changes to the overall trajectory of aging over the course of a lifetime.”

Biological age is an estimate of how much age-related change a body’s cells and tissues have accumulated, separate from the years on a birth certificate.

One way to estimate it is to read chemical tags on DNA that shift in a predictable pattern as people get older. Researchers call those readouts epigenetic clocks.

Run those clocks on blood drawn through a pregnancy and the number climbs as the pregnancy goes on, then partly falls after the birth.

Women on three continents show the same pattern, and so do mice. The authors wrote that this level of agreement is uncommon in their field.

The changes behind the number are physical. Blood volume rises by about half.

Cortisol, a stress hormone, climbs through pregnancy, and estrogen runs at roughly 100 times its usual level. The body also builds a new organ, the placenta, from nothing.

A detailed picture of the recovery comes from Israel, where researchers analyzed 76 routine lab tests from more than 300,000 pregnancies.

About half of those markers needed anywhere from three months to a year to return to where they had been before the pregnancy.

An aging clock built from those same lab results showed a dip before a large rise. In the first trimester a woman’s results looked about five years younger than her age.

From there they rose by about 20 years toward delivery, and it took roughly a year for them to recover.

Not every system recovers on the same schedule. Among more than 200,000 people, the reading from a clock built from wearable pulse sensors rose by about 3.5 years during pregnancy, then declined only slowly after birth.

Brain scans of first-time mothers showed gray matter volume falling across nearly all of the cortex during pregnancy and only partly recovering afterward.

The most direct evidence comes from a 2026 study that followed 130 women from pregnancy through nearly four years after the birth.

Among women who became pregnant again, the decline in their measured pace of aging was interrupted, and two other aging measures moved in the same direction.

Albright and Poganik call the time between a birth and the next conception the recovery window, and their hypothesis is simple to state.

Each pregnancy adds some biological age, and given enough time the body can undo at least part of it.

Under their hypothesis, another pregnancy could add to any biological-age increase that has not yet reversed.

Two women with the same number of children could therefore end up aging at different rates, depending on how far apart the births were.

The authors wrote that this is their central prediction, and it hasn’t been directly tested. No study has yet measured the spacing of pregnancies alongside biological age tracked for decades afterward.

Mothers of many children tend to die earlier in some populations and not in others, and the reasons remain unclear.

In the Finnish Twin Cohort, the risk of dying was highest among women with no births and women with five or more, and lowest among women with two or three.

Among adoptive parents, death rates declined as the number of adopted children increased.

A study of 2,356 women aged 35 to 74 linked more births to small increases in epigenetic age, measured well after their last pregnancy.

In 1,735 young women in the Philippines, with an average age of about 22, each additional pregnancy was linked to roughly two to three months of extra aging.

Spacing could explain why the count alone gives such mixed results, the authors wrote. Where women with many children tend to space them well, the count would carry little cost.

“It’s possible that this could change the overall trajectory of aging,” Poganik said.

“We know that some level of parity, or carrying a pregnancy to term, is actually beneficial to a person’s lifespan.”

The World Health Organization has recommended since 2005 that women wait at least 24 months after a live birth before conceiving again.

The advice rests on evidence linking short gaps to more preterm and low-weight births.

One review of 129 studies and nearly 47 million pregnancies linked gaps of 18 to 23 months to the best outcomes for babies. Gaps under six months were most consistently linked to harm for both baby and mother.

When a new pregnancy began six months after a birth, instead of 18 months, only women 35 and older faced a substantially higher chance of dying or becoming severely ill.

The age pattern fits their idea, Albright and Poganik wrote, because an older mother starts the recovery period biologically older, with less reserve.

Some factors were associated with differences in recovery.

Among about 120 women in one cohort, those who breastfed exclusively had a lower biological age three months after birth, whatever their body mass index.

A higher body mass index before pregnancy predicted a higher biological age afterward.

Sleep loss and chronic stress are suspected of slowing recovery too, though the authors found little or no research on either after birth.

Not every population fits. Tsimane women, who farm and forage for a living, have many children and little spare food.

Over a 10-year study their nutrition and immune markers largely held up however many children they had.

But those measures weren’t combined into a biological age, the authors wrote, so the cost they propose can’t be ruled out there.

Whether the extra age is wear, or the body remodeling itself for a demanding job, isn’t settled either. Clocks designed to tell the two apart are one way to find out.

Poganik said the work has a nearer-term purpose as well.

“What motivates me on a more near-term basis is addressing current clinical challenges, including the huge maternal mortality crisis in the United States compared with other industrialized countries,” he said.

“Any insights we can gain that can improve the health of mothers makes this a worthwhile research endeavor.”

The question underneath all of it is whether an incomplete recovery leaves a mother permanently older on the clocks, or whether the deficit fades given enough time.

Answering it means measuring biological age through several pregnancies, then following those women for 10 to 20 years, and no cohort has done that.

Until then, the 24-month guidance rests on pregnancy outcomes. Poganik said any advice based on a mother’s biological age would have to wait for that longer follow-up.

The review was published in the journal Trends in Endocrinology & Metabolism.

D’Amico: ‘Roma learning experience at Manchester United, need new resources’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Roma director Tony D’Amico admits the defeat to Manchester United was ‘a learning experience’ for them, confirming the need to sign ‘a forward who can do damage on the counter-attack’ when leading.

The match with Sassuolo kicks off at the Stadio Olimpico in Rome at 19.45 GMT (20.45 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s four Serie A games on the Liveblog.

The Giallorossi are still unbeaten this season in Serie A, but have twice thrown away massive leads in the Champions League for defeats to Real Madrid and now Manchester United.

That Old Trafford collapse from 2-0 up to lose 3-2 confirmed this is a recurring problem, so this evening the pressure is on to get a result against struggling Sassuolo.

“It is a problem we realised towards the end of the transfer session, that we lack a wide forward who has pace and can do damage in those situations on the counter-attack,” D’Amico told Sky Sport Italia.

“We will try to correct that when the January window comes around, so will try to give the coach this extra characteristic that we don’t currently have.”

It has been said that Roma lack experience in the Champions League and that cost them when their heads dropped in those situations.

“In an atmosphere like Old Trafford, the fans make a big difference and they helped turn the tide of that game,” insisted D’Amico.

“What I saw in both the games against Manchester United and Real Madrid was a great Roma side that imposes its style of play on the strongest opponents. There are going to be moments of difficulty in fixtures like this.

“I don’t think enough has been said, partly because we don’t like to court controversy, but the penalty for Manchester United was more than generous. So we did have a moment of difficulty, but that penalty sparked the comeback.

“As I said after the Real Madrid defeat, these are learning experiences for a club that has not been in the Champions League for seven years, it also teaches us what resources we still need to give to the coach in order to continue this progress.”

Gianluca Mancini is the latest absentee with a flexor issue that also ruled him out of the Italy squad for the Nations League games with France and Belgium.

He joins Devyne Rensch, Pierluigi Gollini, Mile Svilar, Paulo Dybala and Lorenzo Pellegrini on the treatment table, but the good news is the return of Manu Koné for the first time since his muscular injury against Real Madrid on October 14, even though he wasn’t called up for France.

“We speak with all the international coaches, Koné returned to training this week, we are pleased he is back and it gives us the chance to rest someone who played nine games in 28 days.”

£5m Wolves man faces fresh dilemma: Cardiff City & Norwich City must act

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The recruitment team at Wolves will already have one eye on the January transfer window as they look to strengthen Cesar Peixoto’s squad to help their promotion push.

It seems inevitable that Wanderers will try to bring in a new striker, but, aside from the lack of depth up top, the Portuguese head coach will be pleased with the overall state of the squad.

However, one problem facing Wolves is the size of the squad, as they are already at the 25-man limit. Therefore, to secure new additions, players will have to leave.

And, one man who may be open to a move is Tommy Doyle, with the midfielder having only played 281 minutes in the Championship this season heading out of the midweek fixtures.

As well as that, Peixoto doesn’t seem to know Doyle’s best position, as he had operated higher up the pitch before coming on as a number six in the recent win over Bristol City.

So, the £5m signing may feel he is at a stage in his career where he can play regularly and nail down a permanent home.

With that in mind, we look at TWO Championship clubs who should be targeting the 25-year-old in the New Year…

The Bluebirds have had some fantastic moments back in the Championship under the guidance of Brian Barry-Murphy, who has implemented a clear style of play with the Welsh side.

Yet, the one major problem Cardiff have right now is a lack of depth, and, in truth, they could do with strengthening in various areas in the January transfer window.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

A new midfielder would certainly be welcome, even if it isn’t a priority, and Doyle is ideally suited to the way that Barry-Murphy wants to play, as he is excellent on the ball.

The Cardiff fans got to see Doyle’s qualities during a loan spell in the 2021/22 campaign, but he has matured a lot as a player since then, and he could now slot in as an important figure in the XI.

Again, he should be able to flourish in a possession-based approach, as Doyle can help control the tempo, and he would get the ball forward quickly. Plus, his mentality and experience at this level should help a relatively young Bluebirds squad.

After a slow start to the season, Norwich look to have finally turned a corner, and there will be a belief that they can still win promotion this season, which was always the aim under Philippe Clement.

But, the first few months of the season have shown that Norwich are still lacking depth, even if they have been very unfortunate with injuries.

With Kenny McLean missing too many games through injury, Norwich are in need of a new midfielder who can hold the ball, move it quickly, and help control games - which is what Doyle is all about.

It remains to be seen whether Wolves would be willing to sell to the Canaries, as they could be promotion rivals, but it’s certainly something that Norwich should explore.

That’s because, on paper, it could be a great move for Doyle and Norwich, and he is the sort of addition that Clement should look to make to ensure his group can last the distance in the promotion race this season.

Charlie Austin reacts to awkward Derby County, Sammie Szmodics interview

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Former QPR, Burnley and Southampton striker Charlie Austin has backed Derby County forward Sammie Szmodics after his tense interview with a local reporter last weekend.

It has been a turbulent start to the season for Derby, and Szmodics made his frustration clear after the 4-1 defeat at Millwall last Saturday when he was involved in a heated exchange with BBC Radio Derby's Ed Dawes.

It was another day to forget for Derby last Saturday as they were comprehensively beaten by Millwall at The Den, with three of the Lions' goals coming in the opening 17 minutes, and Szmodics fronted up to the media post-match.

However, when Dawes suggested to Szmodics that he would be particularly angry about the situation as he was 'John Eustace's boy', it is fair to say that the 31-year-old was less than impressed, responding: "Yeah, of course, because I'm a professional footballer that wants to win. I'm not John Eustace's boy."

Dawes later attempted to clarify his comments, stating that he was referring to the fact that Szmodics had played for Eustace previously, to which he replied: "Yes, I've played under the manager, I'm not his boy, but we're all in it together."

After spending the second half of last season on loan at Pride Park, Szmodics returned to Derby on a season-long loan in August, and the move will become permanent in the summer for a fee of around £2.7 million — or £5 million if the club win promoted to the Premier League — after an appearance-related clause in the deal was triggered.

Szmodics has scored four goals and provided one assist in 14 appearances this season, but he is set to be out for six weeks after aggravating a knee injury that had been troubling him for much of the campaign in the 4-0 defeat to Norwich City on Tuesday night.

Szmodics' interview has caused plenty of debate over the past week, and Austin has become the latest pundit to give his opinion on the saga, praising the Derby forward for his reaction to what he believes was an inappropriate question.

"They know they're not performing," Austin said on his Chaz Kicks Off! podcast.

"Eustace was the assistant manager at QPR when I went back there, but he was also in the Derby starting XI when Bobby Zamora scored in that play-off final to beat them 1-0 and Richard Keogh cried.

"Hear me out here, Sammie Szmodics saying to that reporter 'I'm not his boy', fair play to you mate, I've got a lot of time for it.

"The reporter was being an absolute one of them mate, let me tell you, suggesting 'you're getting picked when you're not performing because you're his boy'.

"Fair play to Sammie Szmodics for giving him a little bit back.

"Players know they're underperfoming, they don't need someone from the radio coming out with nonsense questions.

"He's had a little nibble back, fair play to him.

"The problem you've got is, you can only do the talking on the pitch, and that's what matters.

"At the moment, Derby are not talking, they're not performing, and they are in trouble."

Szmodics is one of a number of Derby players who have worked under Eustace at one of his previous clubs, and while the pair have a strong relationship, it is understandable that he took exception to Dawes' question after the defeat at Millwall.

After a 10-game winless run, the Rams lifted some of the pressure on Eustace with a 3-1 home victory over Bolton Wanderers on Friday night in Szmodics' absence, but they will still head into the international break sitting in the Championship relegation zone.

Should Derby County part company with John Eustace during the upcoming international break? Tell us in the comments below!

It seems likely that Eustace will now be given more time to turn Derby's fortunes around, and he will be hoping that he is still in charge at Pride Park once Szmodics returns from injury in the New Year.

Delap’s Nottingham Forest stunners hand Manchester City third league defeat in a row

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Wait long enough and results will come to reflect performances. Manchester City may have won their first seven league games of the season but they always looked vulnerable, and now have lost three in a row. Nottingham Forest may have been second bottom but, other than the twin curses of missing chances and giving away daft goals, they seemed to be playing reasonably well. Put the two together and the outcome was a dramatic regression to the mean. It turns out the stats were right, after all.

Liam Delap scored twice to remind people just what a good striker he can be. Oliver Glasner, after last week’s anger, can breathe a little easier in his job. And Enzo Maresca remains a paradox, a manager obsessive about control whose teams always seem oddly susceptible to direct running.

After last week’s defeat at Brentford, Glasner said he regretted compromising too much in the early weeks of his reign at Forest. Although he gave no precise details as to what that might mean, he elaborated a little on Thursday, speaking of the need for “trust and respect” in the appropriate balance, suggesting there had been too much trust and not enough respect this season at Forest, too much “Woodstock 69” and not enough “high-performance nature of military”.

Glasner’s brave new uncompromising world was ushered in with just two changes to the starting lineup from defeat at Brentford, Ola Aina and Dan Ndoye dropping out for Daniel Muñoz and Ryan Yates, who both came on at half-time last week. It may have been an underwhelming revolution but it soon worked, Jair Cunha dispossessing Josko Gvardiol and running through to opening the scoring after six minutes, Forest’s first home goal since Morgan Gibbs-White’s strike against Bournemouth 34 minutes into the final game of last season, a drought of 510 minutes.

No side this season had underperformed their xG by such a margin as Forest; they began the weekend third in Opta’s expected table but second bottom of the actual table. To an extent that is a matter of misfortune: defeat at Brentford, for instance, was a result of a cross and a shot that both looped in off Nikola Milenkovic; when you concede one shot on target but two goals, you know you’ve been unlucky. And when, on Saturday, three minutes after Forest had taken the lead, a Jérémy Doku shot hit Milenkovic and, rather than looping agonisingly past Matz Sels, cannoned wide for a corner, Forest must really have known their luck was in.

One of the biggest issues for Forest this season has been the form of Delap. A gruelling spell at Chelsea had seemingly sapped his confidence but, 18 minutes in, he surged from inside his own half, ran, as Cunha had for the opener, between Rúben Dias and Marc Guéhi, and thumped his shot over the dive of Gianluigi Donnarumma. With confidence, it turns out, Delap is a very different player.

Guéhi’s form, meanwhile, is becoming a major concern for City. With Dias wary of Xaver Schlager running to his right and potentially becoming a passing option, it was surely Guéhi’s responsibility to close Delap down. As it was, the pair left a huge channel, inviting Delap to keep going to score his second goal since joining Forest. Guéhi and Gvardiol were both withdrawn at half-time.

In the Guardian app, tap the Profile settings button at the top right, then select Notifications. Turn on sport notifications.

If you already have the Guardian app, make sure you’re on the most recent version.

If you don't have the Guardian app, download it from the iOS App Store on iPhone or the Google Play store on Android by searching for 'The Guardian'.

If James McAtee had not missed the target with two very presentable opportunities, Forest could have been out of sight by half-time. It was never, though, going to be that easy. Seven minutes into the second half, a long diagonal set Doku in behind Muñoz, and although his cross was half-cleared, Rayan Cherki slammed in a brilliant half volley. In a moment, the mood changed. All the anxiety stored up by two months of disappointing form bubbled to the surface again.

Forest may feel that, after the season they have had, they are owed some luck. They were nearly paid back a slice of that when a Yates shot deflected off Elliot Anderson and dropped on to the back of the bar. But still they had burned through a fair amount, with blocks, brave headers and clearances off the line, when Gibbs-White set Delap away on the counter. A week ago, it would barely have registered as an opportunity but, self-belief restored, Delap swept into the box and drove his finish in off the far post.

Forest are out of the relegation zone and the idea that City might challenge Arsenal for the title, even if there is no points deduction, is beginning to look a little fanciful.

Flick and Barcelona aiming to cement top spot against ‘excellent’ Atletico

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Hansi Flick has stressed the importance of going into the international break at the top of LaLiga as Barcelona prepare to lock horns with Atletico Madrid on Sunday.

Barcelona make the trip to the Metropolitano with a two-point buffer over Diego Simeone's side, while Real Madrid are ready to pounce should either team slip up.

The Blaugrana have won their last four away LaLiga games against Atletico and could become the third team in the competition's history to achieve five consecutive victories at the Rojiblancos' home ground, after Athletic Club in 1955 (W5) and Real Madrid in 2013 (W6).

However, Atletico come into the clash having won their last seven LaLiga matches, only managing a longer winning run in January 2025, when they won eight games in a row.

Simeone's side have also won two of their last five games against Barcelona in all competitions (L3), one more victory than they managed in their previous 10 meetings with the Blaugrana (W1 D1 L8), and Flick is expecting another stern test for his players.

"Every game is important. There are three or four excellent teams in LaLiga that are tight in the standings," Flick told reporters.

"It's important to beat Atletico there. We'll give it our all, but there's a long way to go until the end of the season.

"They are a tough team with a fantastic coach. It will be a demanding match. The previous games are in the past.

"The important thing is tomorrow, and we know that they are a fantastic team, and we want and must win to be at the top of the table before the break."

Big stage. Big opportunity. https://t.co/rLSk2Boguj

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) November 7, 2026

Barcelona arrive in a rich vein of form, having returned to winning ways in the Champions League with a comfortable 3-0 victory over Unai Emery's Aston Villa.

Flick's side have scored 46 goals in 11 games in the 2026-27 LaLiga season, with only Athletic in 1930-31 (49) netting more goals after their first 11 matches of a single campaign in the competition's history (Sevilla also scored 46 in 1940-41).

And their form has been spearheaded by Raphinha, who has scored 15 goals in 10 games, becoming only the second player to reach this tally after his first 10 appearances of a campaign in the competition in the 21st century, after Cristiano Ronaldo with Madrid in 2014-15 (18).

The Brazilian put pen to paper on a new contract with Barcelona earlier this week, extending his stay in Spain until at least 2030.

Raphinha has made 190 appearances in all competitions for the club, scoring 94 goals in total as well as providing 55 assists, with no player recording more goal involvements (149) or chances created (347) for the club in all competitions since his debut.

Only Robert Lewandowski (120) has scored more goals than Raphinha in that time, with only Lamine Yamal (56) bettering his assist tally.

"I think it's the best situation for the club and for Rapha. I'm happy with his renewal, it's important for the future of the club. He's our captain," Flick said of Raphinha.

Tottenham ‘have confidence’ in VAR despite Bogle decision, says De Zerbi

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Roberto De Zerbi insisted Tottenham have "confidence" in VAR, despite the decision not to send off Jayden Bogle during their defeat to Leeds United.

Spurs saw their recent resurgence brought to an end at Elland Road, where they were beaten 1-0 following a run of three straight wins.

However, Leeds were fortunate not to be reduced to 10 men in the first half, when Bogle caught the back of Mathys Tel's Achilles.

The Spurs winger was forced off, but following a four-minute VAR review, Tony Harrington did not advise referee John Brooks to refer to his pitchside monitor for a second look at the incident.

De Zerbi was later incensed when Pedro Porro was booked for a less malicious challenge on Lukas Nmecha during the second half.

The Spurs boss was subsequently shown his third yellow card of the season, meaning he must now serve a touchline ban during their next game against Ipswich Town.

Nevertheless, De Zerbi was philosophical when reviewing the performance of VAR during his post-match interviews.

Thank you for your backing in West Yorkshire today, Spurs fans https://t.co/WeMmdU10Xh

— Tottenham Hotspur (@SpursOfficial) November 7, 2026

"No [the referee should not have been sent to his pitchside monitor for the Tel challenge], I have big confidence in the referee," he said. "Nothing changes if he goes to watch the clip or not.

"There was the VAR, and if they didn't take the right decision, I don't know, but we have confidence. We have to keep our eyes on the performance where we can improve.

"Pedro Porro was a normal tackle, a normal duel and was an incredible yellow card. The first situation of Mathys Tel, I didn't see, I can't say nothing."

It was only Tottenham's second defeat in 14 Premier League meetings with Leeds, while they also failed to record a fourth straight victory in the competition at Elland Road.

Spurs recorded 67% of possession during the second half, but registered just four shots to their opponents' 13, and De Zerbi felt his side were made to pay for that lack of a cutting edge.

"First half, I thought we played well, better than Leeds. We created two or three chances to score," he added.

"In the second half, we played badly, and Leeds played much, much better. They deserved to win. We didn't show what we showed in the Chelsea game, and the other games [that we won]."

Nicolas Jackson has emerged as one of the Premier League’s most dangerous attacking players this season, and his impressive performances highlight why Chelsea could eventually regret letting him leave.

The former Chelsea striker has rediscovered his best form and become an increasingly influential figure for his current side. His contribution in front of goal has been particularly impressive, with the £65 million-rated attacker responsible for half of his team’s goals so far this season.

According to Opta Analyst statistics via Daily Briefing, Jackson has scored five goals in eight Premier League appearances for Aston Villa, making him the club’s leading scorer in the competition. He has also found the net in consecutive league matches, suggesting that he is building considerable momentum under Unai Emery.

Chelsea allowed Jackson to leave Stamford Bridge in the summer, with Aston Villa securing his services in a deal reportedly worth £65 million, including add-ons. The decision could prove costly for the Blues, particularly given the Senegalese international’s ability to offer pace, movement and a consistent goal threat in the final third.

The 25-year-old previously struggled to convince everyone during his time in West London, but he appears to have found an environment where he can showcase his attacking qualities more effectively.

His previous experience working with Emery at Villarreal could also be playing an important role in his resurgence. The Villa manager knows how to get the best out of the forward, and their existing relationship could help Jackson maintain his impressive standards throughout the campaign.

Meanwhile, Manchester United will have to be particularly wary of the former Chelsea star when they face Aston Villa. Ruben Amorim’s side have conceded 15 goals in nine league matches without keeping a single clean sheet, leaving considerable room for improvement defensively.

Jackson’s current form makes him a major threat, and he will be determined to demonstrate exactly what Chelsea are missing.

For the Blues, watching a former attacking talent flourish elsewhere could become an uncomfortable reminder of their summer transfer decision.

PSG: Luis Enrique believes he has the injuries under control

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Lately, Paris Saint-Germain has not been polished. The capital club has lost several players to injury: Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, Nuno Mendes, Ousmane Dembélé and Marquinhos have all joined the infirmary for a while. Asked about these physical problems on the eve of the trip to Le Havre, Luis Enrique felt that he and his staff were doing their best to limit these injuries as much as possible.

“We like to have as much control as possible when it comes to injuries. It’s been an important topic since we’ve been here. There are players who are more adapted to not getting injured, and others for whom it is the opposite. The ability to perform high-intensity sports varies. Training loads must be varied. I think during my coaching career we have been able to control that. There are bad times and good times, and I’m happy with what we’re doing. Injuries are part of high-level football and they are difficult to control”he confided at a press conference.

60 Minutes’ Lesley Stahl Addresses Backlash Over Her Decision To Stay Amid Bari Weiss’ Tenure

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Stahl is just one of a few holdovers after the firings.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

60 Minutes underwent a major creative overhaul this past summer, which saw executive producer Nick Bilton and CBS News Editor-in-Chief Bari Weiss make considerable staff changes. Several notable series alums were fired and, since then, several new employees have been added. However, when the show returned for Season 59 as part of the 2026 TV schedule, some familiar faces were still on the air, including host Lesley Stahl. Now, Stahl is getting candid about remaining with the show under Weiss’ leadership after the firings.

Stahl – who’s been with 60 Minutes since 1991 – opted to remain with the news magazine program alongside fellow series veterans Bill Whitaker and Jon Wertheim. Ahead of the trio jointly confirming their decision to stay, there had been considerable speculation about their plans. Stahl spoke about her choice while being honored at the Power 100 luncheon this past week and claimed that she’d been on the receiving end of backlash for staying with the show. Via The New York Post, Stahl explained why she and her colleagues chose to stay:

I myself have been under attack on social media because of my decision to stay at ’60 Minutes’ under the new regime. It was not an easy decision. It was one I made with my colleagues Bill Whitaker and John Wertheim. There was an urge to walk out and protest in solidarity with our colleagues who had been abruptly and cruelly fired. But we decided to stay out of a fear, a fear that if we didn’t, ’60 Minutes’ would vanish.

60 Minutes Alum On The Show’s Bari Weiss Era And One Thing She Still Finds ‘Disturbing’

On May 28, which has reportedly since been deemed “Black Thursday" within CBS News, 60 Minutes correspondents Cecilia Vega and Sharyn Alfonsi were fired alongside others like series EP Tanya Simon and executive editor Draggan Mihailovich. Days later, longtime correspondent Scott Pelley was fired as well after reportedly accusing Bari Weiss of “murdering” the show. In the months since then, Pelley, Vega and others have made claims of editorial interference against Weiss and claimed they were asked to exude bias in their reporting.

Bari Weiss herself has been at the center of backlash as well due to the claims of journalistic partiality. At this point, the Free Press founder has denied the assertions and emphasized her intention to improve 60. Since the show returned in September, ratings have reportedly been under the average of the previous season. Weiss addressed those concerns during a recent conference and stated that the series was doing “tremendously well.” Stahl also addressed that decrease in viewership while speaking at the luncheon:

Many loyal viewers over the last 58 years have tuned us out, we presume, in protest. To those who stopped watching, I’m asking you please to come back.

Back in June, Stahl discussed the state of 60 Minutes back in June. She addressed the wave of firings and referred to the fallout as the “hardest chapter” of her career. Stahl – who’s been in journalism for over 50 years – also said the series of firings “was by far the worst experience I’ve been involved in, or even witnessed.” At the time, Stahl also said she’d met with Bilton to stress that while she and her colleagues understood that more needed to be done to the show, she hoped that the broadcast wouldn’t be “tampered with.”

Since then, Bari Weiss and Nick Bilton have added Ross Douthat, Gianna Toboni, Trevor Phillips and Sebastian Junger in host and correspondent roles for 60 Minutes. Lesley Stahl’s future on the news program beyond this current season remains uncertain. In any case, it’s evident that a new era is currently taking shape not just at 60 but at CBS News as a whole.

Former Rockstar Developer Says They Should Hurry Up On GTA 6’s PC Version Before AI Gets There First

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

AIGrand Theft Auto 6Grand Theft Auto VIGTA 6Rockstar

Grand Theft Auto, a gravity well in the gaming universe, is the most anticipated release this generation. Mere weeks away, it has reshaped the world around it, all despite being a console-first, single player experience. Speaking with Rock Paper Shotgun, former Rockstar developer Obbe Vermeij commented on the companyâs unique release priorities when it comes to PC versions, but speculates that could change with AI copycats breathing down its neck.

“I would be concerned if I was Rockstar,” says Vermeij, “somebody would just take the PS5 version and get AI to change it to PC, which won’t be as good as their effort, but it could still also hurt their sales.”

While he hasnât worked at the studio since 2009, Vermeij acted as technical director on Grand Theft Auto III. He said when the company ported the first game to the PlayStation in 1997, there were frustrations with how much had to be compromised from the PC build. Heading into the PlayStation 2, they opted to build for console specs first instead of being forced to pare down. Somewhat unconventional for a game that initially made its home on computers, but it proved to be the correct approach, giving the series a massive player base and launching it into global infamy.

AI could change this philosophy, according to Vermeij. While AI struggles to make original games, film, any art as well as the living, human counterparts it pilfers from, it has proven to be prolific at decompiling games. Able to disassemble and reassemble the parts of a game, it has accelerated mods and ports for existing games in recent months. The latest models of popular AI clients have proven to be a Pandoraâs Box. Its flooded Steam with vibe codedâ shovelware, frustrated vulnerable indie developers, disenfranchised existing modding communities, but also become a thorn in the side of major publishers, whose catalogues are just as susceptible to this form of tinkering.

“A big company like Take-Two, they would be able to get the lawyers on the case,” says Vermeij. “It’s a brave new world. What was the case before, is that there’s so much effort in developing the game, that it is not possible to rip it off, and that’s completely changed now.”

While pre-LLM machine learning-assisted ports were already a sore spot for Grand Theft Auto, Vermeij doesnât think an unofficial, AI-made PC port of the upcoming VI would meet the standards Rockstar boasts. Vermeij isnât an AI hold-out either, using the tech for his own current projects, like the environmental sim Plentiful. Regardless, the prospect of overnight ersatz threatens to prick at Rockstarâs internal release schedule, having already been deflated by high-profile leaks.

Zcash’s new security fund is 99.7% old money

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A November 7 mainnet snapshot puts the historical seed at 99.7% of the reserve, with 1.12 ZEC accumulated under NU7’s fee rules.

Zcash’s new reserve for future block rewards is still overwhelmingly inherited value. About 99.7% of its balance in an early post-NU7 mainnet observation on November 7, 2026, came from previously unclaimed subsidy and fees, leaving just 1.12 ZEC accumulated under the new rules.

At 05:08:38 UTC, the mainnet API for Zakura’s NU7 monitoring dashboard reported a Network Sustainability Mechanism, or NSM, balance of 369.7 ZEC at block 3,547,068. Its status data showed NU7 active.

ZIP 237, the specification governing the reserve, sets the historical seed at 368.58445520 ZEC. Subtracting that seed leaves 1.11895065 ZEC of subsequent accumulation, or about 0.3% of the observed total.

An early mainnet snapshot separates the historical seed from new fee accumulation, then traces the fee path into future block subsidies.

At the observed height, 1.11895065 ZEC had accumulated beyond the seed. Future support depends on fee demand over time.

Zakura observation at 2026-11-07 05:08:38 UTC, height 3547068. Difference follows reported balance and specified accounting, not a separate block-fee audit. This opening snapshot does not forecast traffic or security adequacy. Reissuance is future at block 8939664.

Integer zatoshi: 36970340585 minus 36858445520 = 111895065. Divide by 100000000 for ZEC. Seed share = 36858445520 / 36970340585 = 99.69733829%; labels round to 99.70% and 0.30%. Bar widths use unrounded shares. Flow connectors are schematic, not quantitative.

Zakura observation at 2026-11-07 05:08:38 UTC, height 3547068. Difference follows reported balance and specified accounting, not a separate block-fee audit. This opening snapshot does not forecast traffic or security adequacy. Reissuance is future at block 8939664.

The distinction matters for Zcash’s future security funding. NU7 has established a way to recycle transaction fees into later block subsidies. Its opening accumulation provides an early measure of contributions; assessing future security support requires a much longer record of fee demand.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The historical seed consists of subsidy and transaction fees that miners left unclaimed in coinbase transactions before NU6. ZIP 237 brings that fixed amount into NSM accounting. It therefore gives the mechanism a positive starting balance before any new fee contribution arrives.

For the rules deployed at NU7, ZIP 237 registers transaction fees as the reserve’s only contribution mechanism. ZIP 259, the deployment specification, excludes ZIP 233’s separate removal mechanism and ZIP 234’s alternative issuance design.

There is also no reserve reissuance at the observed height. Taken together, those rules mean the excess over the historical seed represents accumulated fee contributions under the specified accounting. The 1.12 ZEC figure follows from the reported reserve balance and those accounting rules.

The NSM balance itself is public consensus accounting for value outside issued supply. Distribution follows the protocol’s future subsidy rules.

ZIP 235 directs 60% of each block’s aggregate transaction fees into the NSM. The contribution is rounded down once per block to whole zatoshi, Zcash’s smallest unit; miners receive the remainder, including the rounding difference.

Scheduled subsidy remains separate from this fee allocation. The mechanism temporarily removes fee value from circulation and returns it through future block subsidies.

The delay is substantial in block terms. ZIP 259 assigns mainnet activation to block 3,543,405, while reserve reissuance starts at block 8,939,664. ZIP 237 sets the additional NSM subsidy to zero before that later height. Reaching that block threshold determines when payouts begin.

Once reissuance starts, each block adds a small fraction of the preceding reserve balance to the scheduled subsidy, rounded up to a zatoshi. The existing halving schedule remains in place.

ZIP 237 illustrates the scale with 100,000 ZEC removed from circulation: that amount would initially contribute 0.01375 ZEC per block under the post-NU7 formula. This hypothetical illustrates the payout rule; the actual starting payout will depend on the reserve accumulated by then.

Funding streams also receive a share of future reissuance. ZIP 237 applies funding streams’ prescribed percentages to the combined scheduled and additional subsidy. The reserve mechanism and discretionary developer grants are different arrangements.

Before payouts begin, the fee-funded portion grows with cumulative fee spending. More time permits more contributions if fee-paying activity continues; elapsed time alone supplies no funding.

That makes the useful follow-up measure the amount added beyond the fixed historical seed over clearly dated intervals. A growing total can otherwise obscure how much of the reserve still comes from inherited value.

The opening snapshot cannot establish a sustainable annual funding rate, predict future usage or show whether eventual support will be sufficient for network security. Those questions depend on fee demand over time and the balance available when gradual reissuance begins.

Florida Man Finishes Four Corners Walk Across America

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

After over three years of trekking 20,000km back and forth across the continental United States, endurance athlete Kyndal Ray Edwards has completed his ambitious Four Corners Walk. His route took him from coast to coast, then corner to corner, starting in Florida and ending in Maine, where a crowd of supporters met him last week.

Kyndal Ray Edwards, 33, is originally from Orange Park, Florida. After struggling with drug addiction, mental illness, and the justice system during his early adulthood, he decided to become an endurance athlete and mental health advocate. On January 1, 2022, he began his walking project from the beach in Jacksonville.

Over the next 15 months, Edwards walked to Long Beach, Washington, traveling 5,298km from the Atlantic to the Pacific. His plans expanded, and he undertook the challenge of walking to all four corners of the contiguous United States. From Long Beach, he headed to the northwesternmost point, Cape Flattery, also in Washington. After trekking south to San Diego, the next corner he headed for was all the way back in Florida, the southeast extremity.

Along the way, Edwards posted frequently on social media about his journey and his recovery from mental illness.

On October 25, Edwards reached West Quoddy Head Lighthouse in Lubec, Maine. This is the northeast corner of the U.S., and the finish line for Edwards. He walked a total of 19,544 kilometers in 1,022 days, averaging nearly 20km per day.

Edwards and supporters at the finish line. Photo: Kyndal Ray Edwards

A cross-country walking journey, Edwards says, has been his dream since 2013. But with the end of his Walking Across America project, Edwards is already ruminating on future challenges. Online, he has proposed going on an adventure with a stranger in all 50 states, running more ultra marathons, and undertaking endurance challenges on every continent. However, he has yet to commit to one.

The end of Kyndal Ray Edwards’s journey came only days before Karl Bushby, another long-distance trekker, completed his 28-year Goliath Expedition. The pair also happen, Edwards pointed out, to share a birthday.

Lou Bodenhemier holds an MA in History from the University of Limerick and a BA in Creative Writing from the University of Arizona. He’s interested in maritime and disaster history as well as criminal history, and his dissertation focused on the werewolf trials of early modern Europe. At the present moment he can most likely be found perusing records of shipboard crime and punishment during the Age of Sail, or failing that, writing historical fiction horror stories. He lives in Dublin and hates the sun.

Introduction: An Orient Express Adventure Via Africa

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Welcome to my next trip report series, covering a trip that I recently wrapped up, which will include reviews of flights and a cruise. This will be a relatively short series, since the trip didn’t last that long, and had a specific purpose. However, as usual, I tried to take an unconventional routing to get to and from the destination, so I could review new experiences.

You can expect reviews of Air Algerie and Royal Air Maroc business class, as well as a review of Orient Express Sailing Yachts, which is Orient Express’ new cruising venture. In this post I’d like to share a basic outline of the trip, and then I’ll have individual review installments in the near future.

The primary reason for this trip was simple, and it was to take a cruise with Orient Express Sailing Yachts. It’s a fascinating time for the luxury cruising industry, and in particular, the increasing number of new lines associated with existing luxury hospitality brands.

Prior to this year, I hadn’t taken a cruise with any cruise line since I was a child, so I’ve been curious to check this all out. At the beginning of the year, I reviewed Ritz-Carlton Yacht Collection, which was surprisingly lovely, and a very nice reintroduction to cruising.

Then over the summer, I reviewed Four Seasons Yachts, which was definitely upmarket, but had some growing pains.

The newest entrant into this market is Orient Express Sailing Yachts, and arguably it takes luxury cruising to a new level, so you can expect a full review of the experience. Full disclosure — Ford was invited on the sailing (so traveled as a guest of Orient Express) and I tagged along. I’ll still review the experience objectively, of course, but I’ll say upfront that Orient Express simply nailed it, and this matched the feedback Ford has received from clients who have sailed with the company.

Anyway, we sailed with Orient Express Sailing Yachts on a four night journey that started in Marseille, France, and ended in Malaga, Spain, so we needed to position to and from there.

I’m of course always looking for an interesting airline angle when I travel long haul, since my goal is always to try as many new airline products as possible. So rather than flying to Marseille and from Malaga on the “typical” airlines (Air France, British Airways, Lufthansa, etc.), I tried to see if I could instead check some airlines off my list that I’ve been wanting to try. I accomplished that, and below is what my routing looked like.

While I sent Ford direct, I booked a four segment routing for myself, spread across three different tickets.

I had heard a lot about Air Algerie over the years (and not in a good way!), so I decided I finally had to give the carrier a try. Air Algerie typically has very high business class fares, despite the carrier’s lackluster product.

However, I found one decent fare on the carrier’s only route from North America, from Montreal (YUL) to Algiers (ALG) to Tunis (TUN). Okay, that doesn’t start where I’d like to start, and it doesn’t end where I’d like to end, but that has never stopped me if the price is right. 😉

This included a flight in Air Algerie’s Airbus A330-900neo business class across the Atlantic, and then a connecting flight in Avion Express’ Airbus A320neo business class for the short hop in Africa (this was a wet leased flight operated on behalf of Air Algerie). So I booked the following in business class for $1,171 in cash:

10/03 AH2701 Montreal to Algiers departing 4:10PM arriving 5:00AM (+1 day) 10/04 AH4000 Algiers to Tunis departing 7:10AM arriving 8:30AM

From there, I of course had to get from Tunis to Marseille (MRS), so I had booked Tunisair’s A320 business class for $270, with the following schedule:

10/04 TU902 Tunis to Marseille departing 1:05PM arriving 3:40PM

Unfortunately I ended up being downgraded shortly before departure due to an aircraft swap. That’s because the flight was swapped to a wet leased Fly2Sky Airbus A320, and I guess they don’t offer business class on those planes. I’m still waiting on any sort of a refund.

Suffice it to say that the outbound wasn’t that smooth, between Air Algerie just royally sucking, and Tunisair downgrading me. I’m happy I had the experience, though!

I should also mention that I had to position to Montreal, so I did that in Air Canada Rouge economy, and flew the following for 11,500 avianca lifemiles plus $54 in taxes & fees:

10/04 AC1651 Miami to Montreal departing 7:55AM arriving 11:28AM

The return portion of our trip was a little more straightforward. Our priority was to get home the same day as our cruise arrived, since we wanted to minimize time away from our kids, and had a family event the next day. So we booked the one routing that would best get us home the same day, which was on Royal Air Maroc, from Malaga (AGP) to Casablanca (CMN) to Miami (MIA), with the first flight in Royal Air Maroc’s Boeing 737-800 business class, and the second flight in Royal Air Maroc’s Boeing 787-8 business class.

I booked one ticket using 57,500 American AAdvantage miles plus $85.72 in taxes & fees, while the other ticket was booked with cash, at the cost of $1,620, with the following itinerary:

10/09 AT989 Malaga to Casablanca departing 2:15PM arriving 1:30PM 10/09 AT204 Casablanca to Miami departing 3:00PM arriving 7:45PM

Obviously paying cash for a ticket wasn’t ideal, but that still ended up being less out of pocket than flying British Airways business class through London on awards, factoring in the carrier surcharges.

On this trip, we just had a single hotel night ahead of our cruise in Marseille (in order to build in a buffer), and we decided to stay at the Hôtel Dieu Intercontinental Marseille. I booked this using an IHG free night award, and it cost 60,000 points before applying the award, so I just had to redeem the difference in points.

I had intended to review the hotel, but I have to apologize, it just didn’t end up working out (since I pride myself in thoroughly reviewing hotels). To be honest, I was dead tired from the journey over, and then we had some drama to deal with back at home most of the afternoon. Then the next morning I prioritized sleeping in, as I was just getting over a cold, rather than getting up at 5AM so I could photograph all parts of the hotel.

So I’m sorry about that. My conclusion is that the hotel has a central location, and from the outside you’d think it’s super charming, but the interior feels mismatched and rather uninspiring and generic.

We had an absolutely amazing time cruising with Orient Express Sailing Yachts, and I was blown away by the experience. What I was less impressed by was my flights to and from Europe, which I routed via Africa in both directions. Air Algerie was downright terrible, while Royal Air Maroc was just unnecessarily bad.

Customers Have One Big Complaint About Costco's Bakery Items (Hint: It's Not Mold)

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

From Costco's bakery department come all the things that warm your heart, give you comfort, or enable you to celebrate your biggest life moments, like weddings or birthdays. Need an anniversary cake that feeds a crowd? Hosting a baby shower and want to make dozens of chicken salad sandwiches on flaky croissants? Or just want to bring home something a little special, like the luxurious year-round tuxedo cake, to treat yourself and your family? Costco can help you make that happen. Unfortunately, though, customers are noticing a troubling trend that doesn't involve molding or staling. According to their complaints, the warehouse's bakery items are gradually but distinctly suffering from a loss of quality.

On Reddit, one user remarked, "It's like they've forgotten what real, fresh bakery items are supposed to taste like," before going on to describe the muffins as resembling "cardboard soaked in artificial flavoring." Furthermore, while some folks praise the muffins' size, others blame shrinkflation for their frequent returns.

Another Redditor shared that the term "cloyingly sweet" is their "go-to description for almost anything in the bakery now." This is further reflected in a variety of comments stating that the bakery items just don't taste good anymore or don't have the same texture, with one Redditor calling them downright "awful."

It does seem like, these days, everything is getting worse and more expensive at the same time. And perhaps because of its sterling reputation as an employer and the fact that it hasn't thus far raised the price of its signature food court hot dog and soda combo, Costco seemed like it would be immune to those degradations.

However, a Costco bakery employee on Reddit described how, during their three-year tenure, the company gradually switched from scratch-made items to "frozen items that we would just toss in the oven." Another user who identified themselves as a Costco cake decorator shared that while "most items come in frozen," even the baked goods that do get made in-store tend to come from a mix. This contrasts with the account of yet another employee who claimed that when they worked there previously, many of the sweets and breads were indeed made from scratch, though they admitted that "things change fast."

Still, it seems the drop-off in quality is a matter of opinion, not fact. For example, one Reddit user wrote that their bakery "is still really good." Another user claimed theirs was "better than it has any right to be," with plenty of other commenters joining them in their praise.

Assassin's Creed is reportedly going to ancient Rome, while a mobile spin-off heads to Jerusalem

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Ubisoft is reportedly working on two new Assassin's Creed games, and one of them is set in ancient Rome.

That's according to YouTuber Xj0nathan, who says the Rome game is codenamed Mars. He claims two sources with access to Ubisoft's backend systems backed up the information, and that the setting will likely be linked to the upcoming Assassin's Creed TV series.

The second game, codenamed Pocket, is said to be a mobile title set in Jerusalem. Xj0nathan claims it'll play a bit like Assassin’s Creed Valhalla’s Mastery Challenge, and look similar to the most recent Prince of Persia. The hero's mentor is apparently a character called Myriam.

Insider Gaming says it's heard the codenames too, but couldn't confirm them well enough to report them independently. It puts some of that down to the turbulence the franchise has gone through since former Assassin's Creed boss Marc-Alexis Côté left Ubisoft.

Last month, the outlet's sources also claimed Assassin's Creed Hexe, the next mainline game, had been delayed internally by another year, likely to 2028. Other reports claim we'll see it next year.

If Mars does move into full production, don't hold your breath - Insider Gaming doesn't expect us to hear anything about it until 2029 or 2030.

Assassin's Creed has visited Rome before, in 2010’s Brotherhood, but that was the Renaissance version of the city.

The leak comes less than a week after Ubisoft issued a statement about the future of the Assassin's Creed series, insisting there are currently no new remakes of the series' past games in the works.
